## 2026-09-10 — Spec 005 Phase 1: YAML compiler and generic canvas preview

Implemented Phase 1 ("Source graph to simplified preview"): `v2/compiler/{yaml,labels,identity,layoutFields,recipes,convert,generate}.js` parse `project/specs/application/current/app.yaml` and its referenced components/actions/rules into a generic component tree with source-located diagnostics, and `node v2/compiler/generate.js` writes `project/specs/application/app-outline.json`. Added `v2/runtime/genericLayout.js` (row/column/fill/auto layout for an arbitrary tree, distinct from spec 003's fixed editor-workspace template) and `v2/renderers/canvas/mountGeneric.js` + `v2/preview-app.html` (a second, independent static entry point; `preview.html`/`outline.json`/`mount.js` from spec 003 are untouched).

`compiler/yaml.js` is project-owned (MIT) rather than a new dependency, deliberately scoped to the block-YAML subset actually authored here (documented limitations in `project/documentation/compiler.md`); found and fixed one real authoring inconsistency it surfaced (`rules/noDependencies.yaml` is bare prose with no top-level key, unlike the other rule/action files) by teaching the parser to accept a bare-scalar document, rather than silently rewriting the source file.

Verification: `node --test v2/tests` — 50 passing tests (33 from spec 003 unchanged, plus 17 new: `yaml.test.js` against every real current/*.yaml file, `compiler.test.js` for identity collisions/unresolved references/reference cycles/malformed-source hard-failure/determinism, `genericLayout.test.js` for row/column/fill/auto distribution). Manual verification with chrome-devtools against a local static server: `v2/preview-app.html` renders app.yaml's panels/buttons/radio group/status bar/placeholders and an 11-item diagnostics list matching the expected unresolved action1/action2, unresolved volumemeter reference, three unsupported-component placeholders (menu, two notification lights), four unresolved rule interpretations and one ambiguous-duplicate-name warning, in both the on-page panel and the browser console; resized the viewport without errors. Found and fixed two real layout bugs during this pass: (1) children authored as direct mapping keys instead of a `content:` list (app.yaml's `top` panel) were silently dropped; (2) a row/column direction default of "always column" made fixed-height toolbar panels overflow into their siblings — fixed by treating a fixed-height container as a row and stretching (not intrinsically sizing) cross-axis "auto" children so any remaining overflow stays local. One known cosmetic limitation remains and is documented rather than hidden: two notification-light placeholders overlap each other inside the 28px-tall top toolbar.

Not implemented: Phases 2-5 (split-pane workbench UI, hover/click source inspection, the Node service and Save/Refresh, State gallery and Interaction log). No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-10 — Spec 005 Phase 2: hit-testing, hover/select and the workbench shell

Implemented Phase 2 ("Point to source"): `v2/runtime/hitTest.js` (pure paint-order hit-testing, ancestry-chain breadcrumbs, and CSS-scale-correcting pointer mapping) backs new Inspect/Interact modes and hover/selection overlays in `renderers/canvas/mountGeneric.js`. When embedded in an iframe, it posts a small structured message protocol to its parent (`v2:ready`/`v2:hover`/`v2:select`, each carrying the outline's `sourceHash` as a revision guard) and accepts `v2:setMode`/`v2:selectId` back; every handler checks `event.origin`/`event.source`. Added `v2/workbench.html`: a split-pane shell with a file tree built from `app-outline.json`'s `sourceFiles`, a read-only text buffer per file with per-file SHA-256 staleness checking (`sourceFileHashes`, computed client-side via `crypto.subtle`), and a details panel (ancestry breadcrumbs, definition/use-site links, contributing-rule links) driven entirely by the iframe's messages. `generate.js` now records `sourceFileHashes` alongside the combined `sourceHash`.

Found and fixed one real bug: `compiler/convert.js` built binding objects as `{ kind: "rule", ...result.binding }`, and the spread's own `kind` (from the recipe, e.g. `"visual-state"`) silently overwrote the outer `"rule"`/`"action"` classification — this only surfaced when the workbench's "contributing rules" panel and Interact mode's mock-dispatch lookup both came up empty during manual testing (Interact-mode clicks logged nothing at all). Fixed by using a separate `origin` key, added a regression test (`compiler.test.js`) asserting both `origin` and the recipe's `kind` survive on the same binding.

Verification: `node --test v2/tests` — 59 passing tests (50 prior + 9 new `hitTest.test.js` cases). Manual verification with chrome-devtools against a local static server: hovering the control bar updates `v2/workbench.html`'s status line; clicking Play in Inspect mode opens `components/controlBar.yaml` with the button block selected and shows its ancestry/definition/contributing-rule links; clicking the `main` ancestry breadcrumb re-selects the root and opens `app.yaml` with the whole panel selected; switching to Interact mode and clicking Play logs a mock `[interact] would dispatch "transport.toggle"` that does not appear when the identical click happens in Inspect mode (verified via console message counts before/after, not just visual inspection).

Not implemented/not automated: the Node save/convert service and real editing (Phase 3), State gallery/Interaction log (Phase 4), clipping in the hover/selection overlay (no current component scrolls or clips), and an automated test for the stale-source-range banner (reasoned through the code path manually rather than exercised by editing a file on disk mid-session). No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-10 — Spec 005 Phase 3: Node service, in-browser editing and Save & Refresh

Implemented Phase 3 ("Save files and refresh"): `v2/server.mjs` (`node v2/server.mjs`, default `http://127.0.0.1:8877/workbench.html`, `PORT` overrides) serves `v2/**` statically with `Cache-Control: no-store` and exposes `GET /api/{token,files,source,outline}`, `PUT /api/source` and `POST /api/convert`. Loopback only; Host header checked; writes need a per-process token; every path is `.yaml`-only and containment-checked (including realpath) against `project/specs/application/current`; writes only replace existing files via temp file + rename; all mutations go through one in-process queue, and a conversion re-hashes its inputs after compiling and retries if an external editor changed a file underneath it. `v2/workbench.html` detects the service and becomes editable (per-file buffers with dirty markers that survive file switching, Save & Refresh + Ctrl/Cmd+S, separate `save`/`generation` status lines, 409 conflict bar with explicit discard/overwrite choices, 422 invalid-YAML drafts with the error range selected, "saved; preview stale (previous generation kept)" on hard compile failure, preview iframe reload with mode and selection restored by generated identity); on a plain static host it stays read-only exactly as in Phase 2. `renderers/canvas/mountGeneric.js` additionally posts `v2:mode` and echoes a `restored` flag on `v2:selectId` replies so the workbench can restore state without making the editor jump.

Verification: `node --test "v2/tests/*.test.js"` — 74 passing (59 prior + 16 new `server.test.js` cases, 2 of which skip on Windows: symlink-escape needs elevation and read-only directories don't block writes there). The chrome-devtools MCP browser was left in an unusable state by the interrupted session and could not be restarted from this session, so end-to-end verification ran a small headless-Chrome CDP script (scratchpad, not committed) against the real server: 42 checks covering the Phase 3 user-verification scenario (Play → Start, layout edit, disk reread, regenerated canvas, conflict, invalid YAML, saved-but-stale) plus 8 checks for the read-only static-host fallback under `python -m http.server`. All edited source files were restored through the UI and are byte-identical to their originals; `app-outline.json` was regenerated and matches disk.

Found and fixed three real bugs the browser run surfaced: textarea LF normalization rewrote every line ending of CRLF files on save (now preserved per file; pre-existing mixed-EOL files are unified on first save, documented); the Save & Refresh button had no click handler; the file header updated before content loaded, letting an immediate Save hit a disabled button. Also corrected the documented test command: on the installed Node 26, `node --test v2/tests` no longer resolves a directory, `node --test "v2/tests/*.test.js"` is required.

Not implemented: Phase 4 (State gallery, Interaction log) and Phase 5 (end-to-end acceptance/documentation pass, standalone-startup module check). No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-10 — Spec 005 Phase 4: Interact-mode tracing, Interaction log and State gallery

Implemented Phase 4 ("Component development tools"). `runtime/buttonController.js` (pure: hover/pressed/focused/disabled, pointer and Enter/Space semantics, blocked attempts reported) and `runtime/traceCollector.js` (bounded ring buffer with explicit dropped and paused counts, view-only filters) are unit-tested. `renderers/canvas/mountGeneric.js` replaces the Phase 2 `console.info` stub: Interact mode feeds real pointer/keyboard input to a controller per supported button/radio item, dispatches generated `command` bindings against an explicit, visible mock state, draws `visual-state` highlights from that state through the new shared `renderers/canvas/buttonSkin.js`, and emits `v2:trace` events (input, state, activation, command) plus a `v2:setDisabled` hook. `v2/workbench/interactionLog.js` and `v2/workbench/stateGallery.js` are workbench-only modules mounted in a tabbed panel under the preview; the gallery's fixtures are forced-state drawings and its live example traces under renderer `gallery` against private mock state.

Found and fixed a real compiler gap: the `highlighted-when-active` rule recipe and the toggle action recipes produced unrelated mock field names, so the Play highlight could never follow the Play command; `compiler/recipes.js` now aliases condition subjects (play/record) to the action fields with an explicit `activeValue` (regression test in `compiler.test.js`). Also fixed mock-state seeding order (visual-state fallbacks must not pre-empt a command's initial value) after the browser run showed `playback.status=null`.

Verification: `node --test "v2/tests/*.test.js"` — 85 passing. Headless-Chrome CDP run against the real server, 36 checks: Inspect click traces nothing; Interact click traces input → state → activation → command with the highlight following; Enter/Space activation; disabled blocks with no command; selection survives mode switch; log pause/filter/clear semantics visible in the status line; gallery fixtures/bindings/diagnostics; gallery live example isolated from the preview mock; standalone preview loads no workbench/trace modules; no console errors. Two test-script false alarms were geometry (the Interact-mode toolbar readout wraps and shifts the canvas) and legitimate leave/blur traces, not product bugs.

Not implemented: Phase 5 (final acceptance/documentation pass). Radio items select as mock UI state without a command; YAML `shortKey` is not bound. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-10 — Spec 006: CodeMirror editor with YAML highlighting, folding and lint

User request after spec 005 closed. Allocated spec 006 and vendored CodeMirror 6 (codemirror 6.0.2, @codemirror/lang-yaml 6.1.3, @codemirror/lint 6.9.7, @codemirror/theme-one-dark 6.1.3 and dependencies, all MIT) as one esbuild-produced ES module in `v2/vendor/codemirror/` with the bundled entry, licence text and a README listing every package, version and the regeneration command. `v2/workbench/editor.js` wraps it behind a small host API; `workbench.html` swaps the textarea for it without changing the buffer model or the save protocol, adds Fold all / Unfold, binds Mod-s to Save & Refresh and exposes `window.v2Workbench.editor` for automation. The linter runs `compiler/yaml.js` on the buffer (so it flags exactly what `PUT /api/source` rejects) and overlays the compiler's diagnostics for the open file only while the buffer is clean and matches the last generation. This supersedes 005's "plain textarea, no IDE dependency" design note; `AGENTS.md` now states the vendored-bundle policy.

Verification: unit suite unchanged at 85 passing; all four headless-Chrome scenarios re-run against the new editor — Phase 3 loop 50/50 (eight new editor checks: mounted, highlighted, fold gutter, fold/unfold, compiler warnings overlaid, overlay withdrawn on edit, live syntax error underlined), static read-only 8/8, Interact/log/gallery 36/36, resize + standalone 10/10 (standalone preview requests no `vendor/` or `workbench/` module). Screenshots of the highlighted, lint-underlined and folded editor reviewed. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-10 — Spec 007: release build, generated application and bundle

User direction: the release must be plain JavaScript, not an outline interpreter; unresolved warnings never fail it but are listed as loose ends (affected nodes not rendered or no-ops); two outputs, a readable well-structured app in `v2/app/` and a Vite-style minified code-split bundle in `v2/dist/`. Implemented `runtime/components.js` (node constructors with `highlightedWhen`/`onActivate` hooks, `ensureIds`), `runtime/appState.js`, renderer support for a host state store, app context and configurable asset base, `compiler/emit.js` (UI module per authored container, lazy action module per command, command-first state seeding, capability delegation or documented mock, README and LOOSE-ENDS), `build.mjs` (`app`/`dist`/`all`; esbuild code splitting, hashed names, minified, no comments, asset copy, config swap, manifest), `capabilities.js` (empty manifest), `package.json` scripts with esbuild as the single devDependency, `.gitignore` for `node_modules/` and `dist/`. The outline remains the development representation; `app.html`/`preview-app.html` remain dev views.

Verification: unit suite 95 passing (emit tests execute the generated modules in Node from a temp folder with an absolute library base); `app/` 15 files / 6 UI modules / 2 mock actions / 16 loose ends for the current YAML; `dist/` entry 22.8 KB + two chunks (217 and 191 bytes) + skin assets. Headless-Chrome run 18/18 against `app/index.html` (workbench server) and `dist/index.html` (static host rooted at dist/): no chrome, no outline/API/authoring requests, startup fetches exactly entry + assets, the action chunk loads on first Play only, no errors. Screenshots reviewed. Found while building: esbuild's metafile marks dynamically imported modules as entry points (chunk classification fixed); the rule-before-action binding order seeded `playback.status` as null (command values now win); Dropbox transiently locks freshly written files on Windows (folder removal retries; tests emit outside the repository). No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-10 — Spec 008: skin assets traced to source PNGs, authored component styles, Skin tab

User direction: trace every skin asset to its source PNG; load those single PNGs at development time and bundle them into one PNG plus a map file at build time, as before; make the canvas renderer's appearance editable from the workbench, with a component linked to a button type (e.g. `play`) in YAML and that type's per-state background artwork set in a separate section; link a type to its graphical representation description; and render every variety in the component library.

Allocated spec 008 and implemented all five phases. Artwork now lives as one PNG per asset in `v2/assets/skin/src/` (33 copied from the parent repository, with provenance); `project/specs/application/current/skin/default.yaml` says which asset draws which of seven visual states for `panel`, `button`, `radioGroup`, `radioItem` and `label` styles, with `extends`, insets, default size, label placement, focus colour and font metrics. A component links to a variant with `variant: play` (`style:` accepted as an alias) and a radio group's variant cascades to its items through `itemStyle`. An asset name *is* its PNG's base name, which is also its sprite name in the atlas, so the trace from a state to a file is one step. Development loads the individual PNGs (replace a file, reload, no build step); `node v2/build.mjs skin` packs exactly the referenced assets into `assets/skin/generated/atlas.{png,json}` with a new dependency-free packer (`tools/skinAtlas.mjs`, adapted from the parent `skin/generate.js`), and the generated app carries the skin as `app/skin.js` in atlas mode. The renderer no longer hardcodes a sprite name, a 9-slice inset or a font coordinate: `resolveStateAsset()` walks one documented fallback chain and applies the old tints only where a style has no artwork of its own, reporting which of the two happened. The workbench gained a Skin tab (styles by type and variant, per-state rows with the source PNG, the drawn result, provenance and a picker of every available PNG, the variant by state library, and the components each variant draws) plus a details-panel `style:` row linking a component to its graphical representation and back; the State gallery now draws fixtures with the selected node's own style.

Found and fixed three real bugs. `compiler/yaml.js` computed source offsets from the start of a line's content rather than the line, so every column on an indented line was short by its indentation: invisible while only line numbers were read, and immediately visible when the Skin tab spliced YAML at those offsets (`button_inlay_blueay_green`). Compiler 0.2.0 and outline schema 3 mark the change. `loadSkin`/`loadCanvasAssets` cached on the asset base alone, so the workbench kept the first generation's artwork after a save; they now take a cache key. And `build dist` could not clear its output folder in this environment (directory removal is denied, file deletion is not), so both builds clear the contents and report it instead of failing. Editing authored source deserves a guard rather than optimism, so a splice verifies that its range still holds the text it expects and the workbench refuses a dirty or stale buffer.

Verification: `node --test "v2/tests/*.test.js"` 125 tests, 123 passing, 2 pre-existing skips (was 97/95); the new suites cover the packer (including a pixel-identical check of every rect against its source PNG), the skin compiler and the buffer splices. Six headless-Chrome scenarios, 72 checks, all passing: development loads 12 individual PNGs and no sheet; activating Play repaints it with the authored green artwork and dispatches its command; the Skin tab shows and sets artwork, leaving the buffer unsaved until Ctrl+S, after which the regenerated outline and the tab show the new PNG; the generated app and the minified bundle request exactly the atlas pair and draw pixels identical to development; overwriting a source PNG changes the app on reload with no build step, and artwork with no file warns twice and draws a red dashed placeholder without breaking the interface; and on a plain static host the Skin tab is read-only while spec 003's `preview.html` still renders through the built-in fallback skin. Screenshots of all six reviewed. Every file the scenarios touched was restored byte for byte. The chrome-devtools MCP browser was unusable again, so the runs used scratchpad CDP scripts that are not part of the repository.

Noted while working: `components/controlBar.yaml` was authored further in parallel during this session (a spinBox, nested radio groups, `variant: big` and `variant: buttons`, and `- click: toggleRecording` / `- change: setPlaybackType` references). The `variant:` spelling was adopted from that authoring. Those three action references resolve to no file, so the record button and the playback-order group are inert and the compiler reports each one. Several tests that pinned exact counts of the live application's nodes and warnings were rewritten to locate their subjects by name with lower bounds, so authoring the application no longer breaks the suite. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-10 — Spec 009: the component registry

User direction after an assessment of what porting the v1 application to v2 would need: tackle the component registry first, because adding a component type meant editing six hardcoded lists that did not know about each other, and the port needs about thirty more types than the four v2 rendered.

`runtime/componentTypes.js` now declares every component type once: prose description, whether it is implemented, how its children are authored, whether it carries skin artwork, whether it is interactive, how selection affects its siblings, its layout direction, its draw kind and the properties it accepts. `UNIVERSAL_PROPERTIES` declares what every component accepts. The compiler, the layout engine, the canvas renderer, the emitter, the runtime factories and the workbench all read it, and `SUPPORTED_TYPES`, `RESERVED_KEYS`, `STYLED_TYPES` and both copies of `INTERACTIVE_TYPES` are gone. `renderers/canvas/drawKinds.js` is the new table where a kind owns both its drawing and its measurement, so those two cannot disagree; the emitter now calls one generic `component(type, props, children)` factory, which also puts the authored type name into the generated code as documentation.

Three things the registry made possible immediately. A key a component's type does not declare is reported as `unknown-component-property` at that key, listing what the type accepts, instead of silently becoming a child node with a misleading "unresolved component reference" (a mistyped `labell:` on a button was the motivating case). A type can be *declared without being implemented*, which is what `menu` and `spinBox` became: both are already authored in the current source, their properties and item actions are checked, the renderer draws its placeholder, and the diagnostic says "declared but not rendered yet" rather than treating them as typos. And the workbench gained a Components tab: every type with its status, contract, properties, a sample drawn by its own draw kind and the nodes using it, with links to the Skin tab and the State gallery, plus a `type:` row in the details panel. That closes the component-contract half of spec 002.

`checkbox` was added as the proof, through the registry alone: one entry, the shared `spriteLabel` draw kind (artwork at natural size with the label beside it), and two skin states over the `checkbox_on`/`checkbox_off` artwork already in the asset folder.

Found while building, all three by tests written to enforce the seam rather than by reading. `workbench/skinEditor.js` contained a NUL byte where a space was intended, which made the file read as binary to every text tool while working at runtime. Three surviving type-name branches turned out to be about something other than types and became declared properties: which containers get their own generated module (`container.ownModule`), and that a radio item's selection is exclusive within its parent (`selection`), which the emitter's loose-end report and the gallery's caption both keyed off by name. And two draft versions of the property check regressed `app.yaml`'s untyped notification lights, whose `color:` key became a bogus child, until the rule was made explicit: validate a node that has a contract, and leave a node without one exactly as before.

Verification: `node --test "v2/tests/*.test.js"` 138 tests, 136 passing, 2 pre-existing skips (was 125/123). Regenerating the outline after the compiler rewiring produced a byte-identical tree and the same thirteen diagnostics in the same order, with two messages reworded on purpose. Two scanning tests assert that no module outside the registry compares a type name, that the newly added type is named nowhere else, and that the four replaced lists stay gone; `renderers/canvas/mount.js` is excluded because it renders spec 003's fixed template over a different schema. One new browser scenario, 18 checks, covers the catalogue, the planned-type contract, the details-panel link and the new type's artwork per state; the six spec 008 scenarios were re-run at 72 checks and the app, generated app and bundle still draw the Play region at exactly the pre-009 pixel values.

Not done: property editing from the workbench (spec 002, still pending), implementing `menu` or `spinBox`, and the structural gaps they need first, namely an overlay and z-order layer and drag, wheel and key-repeat input. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-10 — Spec 010: the port roadmap

User request after spec 009 closed: write down the further steps for moving the v1 application onto the v2 system, since the earlier assessment was only a chat answer.

Allocated spec 010 as an architecture/research spec: twelve work packages plus a cross-cutting parity harness, each to become its own numbered spec when it starts, with a dependency graph, five milestones, the risks, and six open decisions that are the author's to make. It rests on the 2026-09-10 inventory of `script/src`, `script/plugins` and `player/src`, which is summarised in its requirements: 25,300 lines of v1 own code against 6,728 in v2, 26 reusable component types against 6 implemented, 10 fonts against 1, 113 spritesheet assets against 14, 63 events and 37 commands against 4 prose recipes, about 54 tracker effect branches, and no automated tests on the v1 side at all. About 8,800 of v1's 14,453 UI lines sit in surfaces that are not component trees and become specialized components rather than YAML.

The order is dependency first, then risk, then visible value. Playback moves to the front, ahead of the UI machinery it does not need: it is the largest unknown, it is independent of layout and interaction, `player/src` is a 5,593-line UI-free reuse candidate, and a transport of plain buttons already exists to drive it. Then layout (clipping, scroll viewports, an overlay layer with z-order, declared breakpoints), then interaction (drag with capture, wheel, text entry with a caret, double-click, a declared shortcut table), then the remaining twenty component types and the screens they unlock, then the document model with undo and selection, then the pattern grid as the proof of the specialized-component contract, then renderer performance measured against that workload, then persistence and providers, features and the plugin path, and the remaining specialized surfaces.

This supersedes steps 5 to 9 of `IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md`, and says why: the DOM renderer buys accessibility rather than parity, since v1 is one canvas and a 21-element HTML shell, so it becomes conditional; playback was last and is now first; and step 4's promised clipping bounds, coalesced invalidation and semantic DOM mirror were never delivered and are now packages of their own. Steps 7 and 8 survive intact as the feature registry and the pattern grid.

Also recorded the definition of "ported" as six programme-level gates covering screen, musical, editing, file and delivery parity plus no regression in v2's existing guarantees, and the constraint that the song document never enters the state store: v1's patterns are six to twenty thousand note objects and modules on disk reach 15 MB.

No code changed. Documentation only: the spec, the specs and documentation indexes, product steering, and the supersession note on the roadmap. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-10 — Spec 011: retiring the spec 003 pipeline

User question after spec 009 closed, then approval: can `renderers/canvas/mount.js` be removed? Traced every import first, which corrected two of my own assumptions: `runtime/hitTest.js` only mentions `normalize.js` in a comment and `mountGeneric.js` has its own `flatten`, so `normalize.js` was removable too, while `store.js` was not, because `runtime/appState.js` wraps it and the generated application therefore depends on it.

Removed the whole spec 003 runtime rather than part of it: `renderers/canvas/mount.js`, `runtime/{validate,normalize,layout,commands,uiCommands}.js`, `templates/editor-workspace.js`, `domain/mockTracker.js`, `preview.html`, `dev.html` and the schema-version-1 `outline.json`, plus the four test files whose subjects they were. About 920 lines of source and 280 of tests, 27 tests. Two follow-on pieces of dead code went with them: `assets.js`'s pre-skin `button`/`panel` fields, which existed only so the retired renderer could draw before skins were authored, and spec 009's seam-test exclusion, so that test now covers every module with no exception.

Two capabilities lived only in the retired code, and both are now recorded in spec 010, `runtime.md` and this spec rather than quietly lost. `runtime/commands.js` was the only implementation v2 has had of a command registry that answers whether a command *can* run, consulted by both the renderer, to draw a disabled control, and the dispatcher, to refuse; spec 010's interaction package owns rebuilding it. More importantly, `mount.js` positioned a real, visually transparent `<button>` over every control, which is where focus, accessible names, native Enter and Space activation and the disabled state came from. `mountGeneric.js` has never had one. So this deletion leaves v2 with no accessibility layer at all, and it also corrects spec 010, which had claimed the roadmap's promised semantic DOM mirror was never delivered: it was, for that renderer only.

Since no v2 file has ever been committed, the deletion had no commit to revert, so the removed files were copied to the session scratchpad and the manifest recorded in the spec. Committing v2 remains the durable fix and was left out of scope.

Verification: 111 tests, 109 passing, 2 pre-existing skips, down from 138 by exactly the 27 retired tests. The compiled outline is byte-identical. `node v2/build.mjs` runs all three targets and the bundle entry shrank from 41,327 to 41,144 bytes. Browser run, 8 checks: the three retired URLs return 404, and the application, the workbench and the generated app all load with nothing thrown, no failed request and the Play region drawn at exactly the same pixel values.

Left behind: `v2/templates/` and `v2/domain/` are empty directories, because directory removal is denied in this environment; `domain/` is wanted anyway, since spec 010's playback package fills it next. The historical specs 003, 005, 006 and 008 and this log were deliberately not rewritten; the corrections went only to documents describing the present system. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-10 — Spec 012: playback, the first real capability (roadmap P2)

User direction: implement spec 010, the port roadmap, unattended. Started with its first package, playback, which the roadmap puts first because it is the largest unknown and the most independent piece of work.

The generated application now plays real modules. `player/src` from the parent repository (16 files, 5,593 lines, the playback-only subset with no UI imports) was adapted into `v2/domain/playback/engine/` and wired through `capabilities.js`, so the `transport.toggle` command the prose recipe already generated stops being a mock and delegates to the engine. Nothing in the authored YAML changed to make that happen, and the authored rule that lit the mock now lights the button from the real engine, because both name the same state field. Only the generated app and the bundle reach the capability: the authoring preview keeps its mock, so opening the workbench never starts an audio device and never loads a 68 KB sequencer.

Three divergences from the original, each recorded in `domain/playback/README.md` next to the code. The engine no longer creates an audio context at module import or patches the global constructor name, so the caller supplies one, created on the click. The browserified WAAClock bundle is gone, replaced by `domain/playback/scheduler.js`, a v2 implementation of the four things the sequencer actually uses; that is safe because the clock decides when the scheduling loop runs, not when notes sound, and every note, ramp and envelope point inside the loop is scheduled at an absolute audio-clock time. Nothing else changed: the circular imports between tracker, audio and instrument are preserved exactly, because reordering them is what would break them.

Those two divergences also bought the port its first real test story. The engine is now importable in plain Node, so `tests/playback.test.js` loads three fixtures and asserts their musical facts (title, channels, pattern shape, song length, instruments, envelopes, the vibrato effect, tempo, and that playing schedules a batch of sources spanning the lookahead window with absolute times), and `tests/scheduler.test.js` pins the clock's semantics against a fake context. These are the first automated tests this engine has ever had on either side of the project; v1 has none. `domain/playback/silentContext.js` is the stub context they run against, recording every start, stop and parameter automation so a test can assert on the schedule rather than on a waveform.

One small addition to the authored language, because playback had to be observable: a `label` may name a state `field` instead of static text, and that field becomes part of the application's state so a capability has somewhere to publish to. The status bar now shows the song title and the playback status. It needed no new draw kind and no branch on a component name, and the compiler carries every declared property of a type onto its node generically now, which removed the last hardcoded property pair.

Found while building: the position cursor did not advance. The sequencer schedules ahead and records timestamped states, and in v1 the frame loop decides which one is "now" and pushes it back into the engine; the capability had been publishing to the store without doing that, so anything reading the engine's own position, including the future pattern grid, would have sat at row 0. Also recorded as observed behaviour rather than corrected: v1 converts 8-bit sample data by dividing by 127 rather than 128, so values reach 1.008 and Web Audio clamps the difference, which is visible as a peak of 1.05 at the master output.

Verification: 127 tests, 125 passing, 2 pre-existing skips, up from 111. One new browser scenario, 17 checks, all passing: the engine and the module are absent at startup, the first click loads and plays, the engine reports four tracks and the title "wizball", the lazy chunk and the module data arrive on that click, the audio context runs and the master output carries signal at 0.52 rms, the pattern row advances, the Play button draws its active artwork, the status bar shows the title and status, a second click stops while keeping the module loaded, and the authoring preview never loads the engine and dispatches the mock instead. The other seven scenarios and the full build were re-run unchanged.

Not done: the offline render comparison against v1's own engine, which is roadmap package P13 and now says so. This package establishes that the engine runs, is bounded and is testable, not yet that it renders identically to v1. Recording, MIDI, the write path and remote sources are later packages; MP3 export is dropped because it needs a CDN-fetched encoder. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-10 — Spec 013: layout foundations (roadmap P3)

The third package of the port roadmap, and the debt spec 003's step 4 left: nothing in v2 was clipped, there was no scroller, and there was no way to draw above the tree.

`runtime/genericLayout.js` now returns a clip for every node, a content extent and applied offset for every viewport, and the ids of out-of-flow nodes in paint order. A node's clip is its parent's clip intersected with its own rect if its type clips, and its parent's otherwise; only the new `viewport` type clips, which is deliberate and is what kept the application's rendering identical. A panel does not clip, because clipping the flow would hide the overflow an author needs to see. Hit-testing now requires a point to be inside both the rect and the clip, so a control scrolled out of sight cannot be clicked where it is not visible; the click falls through to whatever really is visible there.

The `viewport` type lays its children out at natural height, translates them by the offset its bound state field holds, clips them, and reports the content extent so the offset can be clamped inside the layout pass. That means a bad field value cannot lose the content, and one viewport replaces the three separate scrollers v1 hand-rolled in its list box, its pattern view and its arrangement view. Interactive scrolling is explicitly not here: wheel, drag and a scrollbar thumb need the input work in package P4, and the roadmap now says P4 owns them.

Two registry fields carry the overlay layer: `layer: "overlay"` takes a node out of flow, laid out against the viewport, painted after the tree in declared order and clipped only by the viewport; `modal: true` additionally makes it swallow input, so a hit outside its subtree is not a hit at all. That is v1's zIndex sort plus its single global modal slot, expressed as declared data rather than a sort key an author maintains, and it goes through one gate in front of every pointer path so Inspect and Interact route identically. No component type declares either field yet, because dropdown, menu, modalDialog and tooltip are package P5; the mechanism is verified by mounting a hand-built tree through the public renderer API, with a throwaway type declared the way P5 will declare one and removed again afterwards.

`layout.direction` is now authorable, ahead of the component type's declared direction and ahead of the pre-013 rule that a fixed-height container reads as a row. That last step stays, because the application relies on it for its top bar and control bar.

Verification: 135 tests, 133 passing, 2 pre-existing skips, up from 127. One new browser scenario, 9 checks: a 60-pixel viewport holding twelve rows draws 2,621 lit pixels inside itself and exactly zero below it where the clipped rows were laid out, the sibling after it still draws, changing the bound field changes the top row's pixels, an absurd offset is clamped rather than emptying the box, a visible row activates on click, a modal overlay draws centred over the tree, and a control behind it cannot be activated. The other eight scenarios re-run at 111 checks, all passing.

Found while doing it: three of the four problems this package surfaced were in my own test fixtures rather than the code, and each is now documented in the test. A viewport's content extent is its own inner width, not its parent's. A row that starts inside a 20-pixel box can still extend past it. And a panel's own 9-slice border reads as content to a pixel counter, one bright pixel down each edge for the full height, which is the difference between a real clipping check and one that always passes.

Also corrected while here: the Components tab scenario had pinned the registry's contents as a literal, so adding the viewport type broke it. It now derives the expectation from the registry itself. Not done in this package and recorded as such: align, minimum and maximum sizes, and the declared breakpoints that replace v1's hand-written cascade, all of which belong with the screens in P5. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-10 — Spec 014: pointer interaction (roadmap P4, pointer half)

Package P4 was split in two when it was started. The pointer work and the keyboard work share nothing but the renderer they land in, so each became its own spec and each could be verified alone; this is the pointer half, and spec 015 owes the rest.

Before this, v2's whole input model was hover, press, focus, Enter and Space. There was no pointer capture, so there could be no drag, and without a drag there is no slider, no scrollbar and no block selection. `runtime/pointerRouter.js` is now a pure state machine that takes positions and the id of whatever the renderer found under them and returns semantic inputs, each addressed to a node: capture, a three-pixel drag threshold, leave and re-enter while held, a 400-millisecond double-click window with five pixels of slop, and secondary clicks that take no capture at all. Three details are deliberate and are documented where they live. The first drag event reports its delta from the press point rather than from where the threshold was crossed, so a fast flick does not lose its first three pixels. Coming back onto a node while still holding re-presses it, which is what a native button does. And a double-click is emitted *after* the second release rather than instead of it, so promoting a type to double-click-aware cannot regress anything.

Which controller a node gets is now declared by its type. `runtime/behaviors.js` is the one table from a behavior name to a factory, exactly as the draw-kind table is the one table from a draw kind to pixels; `button`, `value` and `scroll` are the three. Every controller has the same three members, so the renderer still has one `feed()`, and controllers now report `handled`, which is how a wheel can be offered to each ancestor in turn until one takes it and how the renderer decides whether to suppress the browser default.

A value control does not hold its value: the bound state field does, and every input carries the current value while the controller returns a new one. Which field that is resolves in three steps — the authored `field:`, then the mock field of a `change` action bound to the control, then a field derived from the node id — and the renderer and the emitter call the same function, so the field a generated application declares is the field the renderer writes. Bounds come from the authored `min`, `max` and `step` and every result is clamped and snapped on every path, so no gesture can produce an out-of-range or fractional number. `slider` and `spinBox` differ by two options: absolute along x against relative along y, with a stepper column on the spin box.

Spec 013's viewport can now be scrolled three ways — the wheel, a drag on its background, a drag on its scrollbar — and all three compute a *requested* offset that the layout pass clamps, so the clamp stayed in one place and no path can lose the content. `runtime/scrollbar.js` holds the thumb arithmetic that the draw kind, the controller and the cursor logic all read, and it returns nothing when the content fits, which is how each of those three decides to do nothing. A viewport at either end, or with no bound field, does not consume the wheel, so an outer scroller can take over.

`activate(id)` became `dispatch(id, event, detail)`. `actions:` has always carried an arbitrary event key and only `click` had a consumer; `change`, `doubleClick` and `secondaryClick` now do too, a binding with no event is still a click, and a `change` carries its new value into the generated action module as `detail`. A radio group's choices are authored as items, so the group is where its `change` action lives and the chosen item's name is the value.

Also delivered: one declared cursor per type, with the dragged node keeping the cursor wherever the pointer goes; and file drag-and-drop, where a drop calls `appContext.openFiles(files)`. The generated application gets that hook through a **reserved host command** — a `capabilities.js` key beginning `host.` is not authored in YAML and is not compiled from a binding, it is a hook the renderer offers and the manifest says who takes it — so dropping a module file on the generated application loads and plays it, while the authoring preview passes no app context at all and a drop there is a trace and nothing else.

Verification: 161 tests, 159 passing, 2 pre-existing skips, up from 135. One new browser scenario driving real Chrome input, 23 checks: pressing a slider at a quarter of its track gives 25; dragging it and ending 260 pixels below the control gives 90, so capture held; the thumb moved from the left of the track to the right; the wheel steps a spin box up and down; pressing the stepper's top half moves one step; the wheel over a *row inside* a viewport scrolls the viewport; dragging its background 40 pixels moves the offset 40; dragging its thumb 10 pixels moves it 48, so proportionally further; dragging across a button activates nothing; a double-click reports click, click, doubleClick in that order; a right-click reports only secondaryClick; the four cursor cases are right including during a drag; a dropped file is acknowledged, kept from the browser and handed to the application; and every gesture appears in the Interaction log with the drag coalesced to 8 entries out of 134. A third part drives the real workbench, because a renderer emitting a trace is not the same as the log showing it: the log's filter set starts as exactly the kinds it knows, so all three new kinds would have been hidden rather than shown uncoloured, and a wheel over the *authored* spin box now reads there as a wheel, as `tracks.value: 0 to 1`, and as the `tracks.set` command it is bound to. All ten scenarios together: 148 checks, none failing. `node build.mjs` produces all three targets; the entry chunk grew from 46,248 to 63,553 bytes and the playback engine stays in a lazy chunk.

The authored application's compile went from 13 warnings to 9, and three of its controls started working. Two of those had never worked and the reason was not in the renderer: `- click: toggleRecording` named an action document that does not exist, so the record button had been inert since spec 005, and `action:` is documented as an alias of `actions:` but only `actions:` was ever parsed, so a third radio group's binding was dropped with no warning at all. A bare action reference is now read as prose with camelCase split into words, which reaches the `toggle recording` recipe that already existed, and the alias is honoured. The record button consequently reaches the red active artwork its skin has always described, which until now was only checkable in the workbench's variant library.

Found while doing it, and worth recording: **a state change did not re-lay out, only redraw.** Spec 013's viewport therefore did not move when its bound field was written from outside the renderer — the rows kept the rectangles the previous pass had given them — and spec 013's browser scenario had passed anyway, because its check rested on a pixel fingerprint of the top row that changed for an unrelated reason. The renderer now separates the canvas resize from the layout pass and re-lays out on any state change, since a state change can move things and not only recolour them; the scenario asserts the applied offset as well as the pixels; and spec 013's tasks document carries a dated amendment saying so. The general lesson is recorded there too: a pixel check needs a structural assertion beside it or it can pass over the absence of the thing it is checking.

Three smaller findings. Making `viewport` interactive made `interactive` read oddly, since a viewport needs a controller but is not a control; rather than add a second flag, `interactive` keeps its documented meaning of "the renderer gives it a behavior controller" and `behavior` says which, and the two tests that pinned the list of interactive types were rewritten to derive it from the registry and assert the rule instead. Two tests and one browser scenario used `spinBox` as their example of a declared-but-unimplemented type, so promoting it broke all three; they now use `menu` and additionally check an implemented type's declared property, covering both sides of the distinction rather than resting on which types happen to be finished. And a slider's `min: 1` arrived at the node as the string `"1"`, so the compiler now converts a property the registry declares as a number and warns when the value is not one.

Not done and recorded as such: keyboard input is still the pre-014 minimum, and the focus model, text entry, the shortcut table, tooltips, dialog keys and command enablement are spec 015, which completes P4. Redraw coalescing stays package P8, which wants measurement before it changes how every input redraws. Horizontal scrolling and cell-level hit-testing belong to the pattern grid in P7. `scrollbar`, `knob` and `rangeSlider` are not component types: a viewport draws its own thumb, and a standalone scrollbar needs the binding vocabulary P5 brings. Touch and pen are not handled, which matches v1. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-11 — Spec 010's six open decisions, resolved

Specs 001 through 014 were committed for the first time as `41a667f`, closing out the "no Git commit was created" note on every prior entry above. With the working tree clean, the author resolved all six open decisions recorded in `010-port-roadmap/requirements.md`, which had been blocking nothing yet but were about to start scoping P5, P9, P10, P11 and P12.

Decisions: (1) visual fidelity — a smaller authored skin, not v1's full 10 fonts and ~113 sprites, reducing P5's artwork scope from the exact-fidelity assumption every prior spec was built under; (2) the DOM renderer — deferred until after editing parity (P6/P7), confirming the prior assumption, P12 stays optional; (3) plugins — the sample editor and Nibbles port, the generator waits, widening P10/P11 beyond the prior sample-editor-only assumption; (4) the server API — kept as is, confirming the prior assumption, scopes P9; (5) MP3 export — a vendored, no-CDN encoder is accepted, reversing the prior "dropped" assumption, so MP3 export stays in P9's write path; (6) Amiga host integrations — out of scope, confirming the prior assumption.

`requirements.md`'s "Open decisions" section was rewritten in the past tense to record each resolution and which package it scopes. `design.md` was updated at P5 (fidelity), P9 (API kept, MP3 in scope), P10 (Nibbles added), and P12 (decision 2 resolved rather than pending). `tasks.md`'s open-decisions checklist is now checked off with each resolution inline.

Verification: documentation only, no runtime code changed. Next: spec 015, the keyboard half of P4.

## 2026-09-11 — Spec 015: keyboard interaction, completing P4

The keyboard half spec 014 left: the focused control now sees every key first (`runtime/textController.js`'s `handle()` reports `handled` exactly as the pointer controllers already did), a declared shortcut table (`runtime/shortcuts.js`) fires a component from anywhere by its `shortKey`, `disabledWhen` is synced automatically wherever `highlightedWhen` already is (the command-enablement mechanism spec 011 flagged as owed to this package), a `tooltip:` property shows after a hover delay with no new component type, and Escape/Enter route to whatever `modal: true` overlay is open — verified against a hand-built tree, since `modalDialog` is still P5's, the same call spec 013 made for the overlay layer itself. `inputbox` was promoted from undeclared to implemented, the same move spec 014 made for `spinBox`: a caret, a selection (keyboard- and drag-driven), and an in-app clipboard.

Two things were found only by testing in a real browser, not by the unit tests. First, `handlePointerDown` had never sent a `{ type: "focus" }` event at all — only Tab did — which stayed invisible until a control needed something to happen exactly at that moment (`textController` capturing the value Escape reverts to); every pure test had constructed its own `focus` input, which is exactly what hid the gap. Fixed by sending it from both paths that can newly focus a node. Second, the native `copy`/`cut`/`paste` bridge this spec's design started with — matching spec 014's precedent that a focused canvas gets ordinary DOM events — does not work: verified empirically (Playwright driving real Chromium, with and without clipboard permissions granted) that a plain canvas never receives one of these events for a keyboard shortcut, and the app's own context menu is already suppressed throughout Interact mode besides. The three listeners were removed rather than kept as dead code; `Ctrl+V` now reads the in-app clipboard directly at `keydown`. Both are recorded in spec 015's design.md and tasks.md.

Verification: 178 tests, 178 passing, up from 161 (`node --test "v2/tests/*.test.js"`). `node v2/build.mjs app` succeeds and the compiled skin picked up the new `inputbox` builtin style with no other change needed; `node v2/build.mjs dist` could not run because the installed `esbuild` binary is `win32-x64` on this machine, a pre-existing environment mismatch unrelated to this spec. Two hand-built-tree browser scenarios via Playwright/Chromium (`chromium-cli` was unavailable in this environment): 9 checks with no modal (shortcut, `disabledWhen`, tooltip timing, click-to-place-caret, Ctrl+A/C/V, Escape-revert), 7 checks with a modal (the pointer gate re-confirmed, Tab trapped inside it, Escape always cascading regardless of focus, Enter cascading only when nothing else claimed it). All 16 passed after the two fixes above.

Not done and recorded as such: tracker-specific keys (note entry, octave F-keys, hex/decimal step values) are P7/P6's, not this package's. No compiler vocabulary authors `disabledWhen` from YAML yet — nothing in `app.yaml` has state worth disabling a control on until P6. `modalDialog`, `tooltip` and `dropdown` remain undeclared component types (P5). No caret blink, no mouse-drag selection across a scrolled field, no OS clipboard integration. **Package P4 is now complete** (spec 014 + spec 015). No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-11 — Spec 016: the rest of the catalogue, declared (P5's first step)

P5, the last unallocated roadmap package, is roughly twenty types plus six authored screens — too large to split the way P4 split into a pointer half and a keyboard half, since there is no natural two-way seam here: every type is independent of every other. The roadmap's own design sketch already names the right seam instead: declare, then implement, in dependency order. This spec is only the declare half.

Fifteen remaining v1 types — `icon`, `image`, `numberDisplay`, `rangeSlider`, `knob`, `scrollbar`, `listbox`, `dropdown`, `submenu`, `modalDialog`, `tabPanel`, `tooltip`, `collapsePanel`, `animSprite`, `buttonGroup` — are now registered in `runtime/componentTypes.js`, `implemented: false`, each with a contract and a one-paragraph rationale reading its actual v1 source (`script/src/ui/components/*.js`) rather than guessing. Several contracts flag an open question rather than settle it: whether `numberDisplay` reuses `valueController`'s bounds or `textController`'s caret, that `knob`'s v1 version toggles its own `disabled` flag on a plain click, whether `collapsePanel` should actually collapse anything (nothing in its 33-line v1 version does), and what `buttonGroup` — which has no v1 source at all — is even for. `checkboxbutton` was read and found to be the same shape as the already-implemented `checkbox` (a `Button` subclass with an on/off glyph); it is not carried forward as a second entry, a documented decision rather than an oversight. `tooltip` as a component type is declared but cross-references, rather than duplicates, the plain-text hover mechanism spec 015 already shipped and verified.

Found while doing it: the existing spec-009 registry-consistency test caught two real mistakes in the first draft immediately. `icon`'s and `animSprite`'s obvious property name, `name`, already means "identity within the parent" as a universal property; renamed to `icon` and `sprite`. `knob`'s and `collapsePanel`'s obvious `label` property is already universal too; simply removed, since the universal one already says what was needed. Separately, `listbox`, `dropdown` and `tabPanel` were first drafted with a `childType` naming an unregistered `listItem`/`tab` sub-type (mirroring `radioGroup`/`radioItem`); changed to a bare `{ childrenIn: "items" }`, following `menu`'s own long-standing precedent for "the child shape is not settled yet" rather than inventing two more placeholder types with no source to justify them.

Verification: 180 tests, 180 passing, up from 178 — one confirming all fifteen are registered, unimplemented, placeholder-drawn and controller-less, one proving the compiler's unknown-property diagnostic fires for a new type (`dropdown`), not only historically for `menu`. `node v2/build.mjs app` succeeds with the same 24 loose ends as before, since nothing in `app.yaml` uses any of the fifteen yet. No browser verification: nothing here draws anything but the existing placeholder box.

Not done and recorded as such: no draw kind, skin style, State gallery fixture or authored use for any of the fifteen — that is each type's own later implementing spec, in the dependency order this entry's design.md lists. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-11 — Spec 017: icon, image and animSprite implemented (P5's first cluster)

The first implementing cluster from spec 016's dependency order, promoted to `implemented: true`: `image` and `icon` (one draw kind, since both are "draw a named skin asset" under two property names, `src` and `icon`) and `animSprite` (a frame strip stepped on a timer). `numberDisplay`, grouped with these as "simple" in spec 016's own design.md, turned out on inspection to be a real bounded, editable control rather than a leaf, and was moved to its own future spec instead of being implemented under a label that no longer fit it — a scope correction made before writing code, not a mid-spec discovery.

`animSprite` needed the one genuinely new piece of mechanism this cluster required: v2's renderer had never had a per-frame animation clock, since redraw has always been driven by state changes and input. `runtime/animationClock.js` is that clock, pure and DOM-free like every other timing module in `runtime/` (the pointer router's double-click window, the playback scheduler's lookahead): given elapsed time, it advances a sprite by whole frame steps and carries the remainder forward, so a slow frame or a backgrounded tab catches up correctly rather than drifting or skipping. The current frame lives in the node's own bound `field`, the same place a slider's value and a viewport's scroll offset already live, read through one new fully generic case in `visualStateOf()` — `state.fieldValue`, for any non-interactive type with a field, not `animSprite` by name. A new registry flag, `animated: true`, says which types the renderer's clock drives; it is independent of `interactive`, since a frame counter takes no input at all. The clock itself only runs (`requestAnimationFrame`) while at least one animated node is mounted, and stops on dispose — the same "costs nothing when unused" property spec 013 and 014 already held everywhere else in this renderer.

`measure()`'s three-argument signature grew an optional fourth, `node`, since `image`/`icon` need to resolve an asset to know their natural size and nothing else did; every other draw kind's `measure()` is unaffected.

Verification: 191 tests, all passing, up from 180 (11 new): the clock's stepping/wraparound/catch-up arithmetic, the registry facts for all three types, and — the first time a draw kind's actual pixel logic has been unit-tested in this project rather than only checked in a browser — `image`/`icon`/`animSprite`'s `draw()` and `measure()` exercised directly with a fake asset lookup and a fake canvas context that just records `drawImage` calls. `node v2/build.mjs app` succeeds with the same 24 loose ends, since nothing in `app.yaml` authors any of the three yet. One hand-built-tree browser scenario (Playwright/Chromium): an image, an icon and a 3-frame `animSprite` against the built-in fallback skin all drew real pixels (confirmed by screenshot), and the animated field visited all three frame values within 1.5 seconds of real time, never out of range, with no console errors.

Found while doing it: nothing needed correcting after the fact this time. The two things that looked like risks going in — a property name colliding with a universal one, and changing a signature every draw kind shares — were designed around from the start rather than discovered by a failing test.

Not done and recorded as such: `compiler/skin.js` does not yet register an authored `src`/`icon`/`sprite` reference into a real skin's asset list (it only walks font declarations and a style's `states:` block today); consequence-free until a screen authors one of these three, which none does. `numberDisplay` remains declared, not implemented. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-11 — Spec 018: rangeSlider and knob implemented (P5's second cluster)

The second implementing cluster spec 016 grouped as "value-like controls": `rangeSlider` and `knob`. Both turned out to need no new controller at all - `rangeSlider` is `slider`'s own absolute-mode behavior, `knob` is `spinBox`'s own relative-mode behavior (a vertical drag moves the value, the wheel steps it, no stepper) - so `runtime/valueController.js` was not touched, only two new draw kinds. `scrollbar`, the third type in that original grouping, was left declared-only: its own contract already said it is more likely a waveform or arrangement view's own territory (P7/P11) than something worth implementing against no real consumer, the same reasoning that pulled `numberDisplay` out of spec 017's cluster.

The one new generic mechanism: `rangeSlider`'s `vertical` property has to reach both the controller (to compute the value from the right axis) and the draw kind (to lay out top-to-bottom), and both read it the same way, by property name, in one place each - `if (node.vertical) options.axis = "y"` where controllers are built, and the same check in the draw kind. Nothing checks `node.type === "rangeSlider"` anywhere, which the project's own registry-consistency test would have caught.

Found only by mounting a hand-built tree in a real browser, not by the unit tests: the first draft's built-in fallback style copied `slider`'s `width: 120, height: 20` verbatim, and the vertical instance's `measure()` read `style.height || 120` as its on-screen length - landing on `20`, the wrong dimension's value, because one shared style cannot express two different lengths for two different travel axes. A vertical `rangeSlider` measured 20px tall, indistinguishable from the horizontal one above it in the screenshot. The first draft's unit tests never exercised this because each test case happened to set only one axis's style dimension at a time. Fixed with a plain constant (`RANGE_SLIDER_LENGTH`) for the vertical length instead of reading the style, removing the style's now-misleading fixed height, and a new regression test that mounts a style with both dimensions set and asserts the vertical instance is not collapsed by it.

Verification: 199 tests, all passing, up from 191 (8 new). `node v2/build.mjs app` succeeds, 24 loose ends unchanged, `slider`/`spinBox`'s own generated code untouched. One hand-built-tree browser scenario, 7 checks: a horizontal drag/click, a vertical one (confirming bottom-to-top), the knob's drag and wheel, and both types' declared cursors (`ew-resize`, `ns-resize`) - all passing, no console errors, with a screenshot confirming the corrected vertical geometry and a visibly rotating knob pointer.

Not done and recorded as such: `scrollbar` remains declared-only. No authored vocabulary exists yet for a per-orientation `rangeSlider` length. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-11 — Spec 019: listbox implemented (P5's third cluster)

The third implementing cluster from spec 016's dependency order, and the roadmap's own "first real container-with-selection": `listbox` (`script/src/ui/components/listbox.js`, 399 lines) reuses `genericLayout.js`'s clip/scroll/overflow mechanism through the same generic `clips` flag a `viewport` already sets - no change to that file at all - but needed a controller of its own (`runtime/listController.js`, behavior `"list"`), because its authored `items` are ordinary child nodes of any type (spec 016 deliberately left the shape unconstrained), unlike `radioGroup`'s items, which are always the interactive `radioItem` type. Selection and scroll position are two separate fields: `field` holds the selected index, resolved exactly as a value control's is, and the scroll offset lives at `scrollFieldOf(node)` - a name derived from the node's own id, never authored - so that dragging the list to scroll it can never silently overwrite what is selected, or the reverse. `compiler/emit.js` declares both fields, seeded to `0`, for a generated application.

The one piece of new mechanism this cluster needed, beyond a controller and a draw kind: since most of a listbox's items have no controller of their own, the pre-019 pointer routing - feed a gesture to whatever the hit-test found, only if that exact node had a controller - silently did nothing for a click on a bare `label` item. `renderers/canvas/mountGeneric.js` gained `controllableTarget(hitId)`, which walks the hit node's ancestry for the nearest id (including itself) that has a controller; every pointer-down/move site now routes through it instead of the raw hit id. This is a no-op for every type that predates this spec (a hit node with its own controller, or with no controllable ancestor at all, is routed exactly as before) and only changes behaviour for the new case: an item with no controller inside a listbox that has one. `itemIndexAt()` then resolves which authored child the point landed inside by a direct rect scan over the listbox's own children, not another full-tree hit-test. `renderers/canvas/drawKinds.js` gained a `listbox` draw kind (an unstyled dark backing, the selected row's tint clipped to the listbox's own box, and a scrollbar thumb) and a shared `drawScrollThumb()` helper factored out of `background`'s existing inline viewport-thumb code, with no behavioural change to `background` itself.

Deliberately not carried forward, each flagged rather than silently dropped: drag-to-reorder (the `reorderable` property stays declared and validated, per spec 016, but is not wired to a behavior - populating a listbox from real data is P6's job, and reordering ahead of that would be shaping the mechanism to a guess), dedicated up/down buttons (redundant once wheel/drag/thumb all scroll the same box), and v1's custom per-row rendering, `centerSelection` mode and hover-row tint (the selected row's own tint is implemented; hover's is not).

Verification: `node --test "v2/tests/*.test.js"` - 210 tests, all passing, up from 199 (11 new, `tests/listbox.test.js`: registry facts, a direct `computeGenericLayout()` check that a listbox clips/scrolls its items exactly as a viewport clips its content, the controller's click-vs-drag/wheel/disabled cases, and the draw kind's measure/backing/selected-tint/scrollbar-track behaviour against fake canvas/asset stubs). `node v2/build.mjs app` succeeds, 24 loose ends, unchanged, since nothing in `app.yaml` authors a listbox yet.

No browser verification occurred, unlike every P5 spec before it. This environment has no Playwright/Chromium install - checked directly (`node -e "require.resolve('playwright')"` fails; no `chromium`/`chromium-browser` on `PATH`) rather than assumed. Specs 013 through 018 each verified their interaction mechanism against a real, rendered browser, including catching real bugs that way (spec 013's redraw-without-relayout gap, spec 018's collapsed vertical `rangeSlider`); this spec's coverage is the unit suite plus a layout-level integration test and controller-level input-sequence tests, which is real coverage of the logic but not the same claim as "a real click in a real browser selected the right item." This is recorded as this spec's most significant open item, not glossed over, and should be the first thing re-checked once a Playwright-capable environment is available.

Not done and recorded as such: drag-to-reorder, up/down buttons, custom row rendering, `centerSelection`, hover-row tint (see above); any authored use in `app.yaml`; and real-browser verification (see above). No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-11 — Spec 020: dropdown, submenu and modalDialog implemented (P5's fourth cluster)

The fourth implementing cluster from spec 016's dependency order, and the one spec 016 called "the overlay-dependent trio": `dropdown`, `submenu` and `modalDialog`, promoted to `implemented: true`. Spec 013's layer:"overlay"/modal:true mechanism and spec 015's Escape/Enter cascade had only ever been verified against a hand-built throwaway type that was *always* present in the tree - the one real gap this cluster closes is that nothing could show or hide an overlay at runtime. `runtime/genericLayout.js`'s `computeGenericLayout` gained an `overlayOpen` option (default: always open, so every existing caller, including spec 013/015's own hand-built-tree tests, is unaffected) and anchored positioning (`node.anchor = { targetId, edge }`, with edge-flip and cross-axis clamping) alongside the existing centred-by-default behavior.

A dropdown's popup and a modal dialog's synthesized OK/Cancel/input are not authored - v1's own constructors build them, and the v2 equivalent is `runtime/overlaySynthesis.js`, a pure function run once at mount time (so both the authoring preview and a generated application, which share `mountGeneric.js`, get them the same way). `renderers/canvas/mountGeneric.js` gained `openOverlay`/`closeOverlay`/`toggleOverlay` (on the mounted instance's public API, since nothing authors a way to open a dialog from YAML yet - an application calls it directly, exactly as nothing authors listbox's `reorderable`) and three small, registry-fact-gated generic rules: a `"disclose"`-behavior click (a dropdown's own, a second name for the plain button controller) toggles its popup instead of dispatching a command; resolving an input inside any `.modal` overlay closes it, which is what makes both the popup (behavior `"list"`, reused from listbox unchanged) and a submenu (a new, small `"flyout"` behavior, `runtime/submenuController.js`) close correctly with zero submenu/dropdown-specific code in `feed()`; and a behavior may name which child it activated by index, which is how a submenu's own item (not the submenu itself) receives its `dispatch()`.

`submenu`'s contract drops spec 016's placeholder-only `itemActions: true` (that shape existed only so a *declared-but-undrawn* type's item actions could be validated without inventing real nodes - still exactly right for `menu`, which stays a placeholder) in favour of the same bare `{ childrenIn: "items" }` shape `listbox` already uses, since a real, drawn submenu needs real per-item nodes. `menu` itself is not promoted; `submenu` is verified against a hand-built tree that opens it directly, the same way spec 013/015 verified the modal mechanism itself, since nothing else can trigger one yet.

Found while designing, before any code: reusing `dropdown` as both a type name and a behavior name would have tripped spec 009's own registry-consistency test (no module outside the registry may branch on a component type name by string) the moment `mountGeneric.js` checked `behaviorNames.get(id) === "dropdown"`, since that check is textually indistinguishable from a type-name branch. Both new controllers are registered under names that are not component type names (`disclose`, `flyout`), and `runtime/overlaySynthesis.js` dispatches on a new registry fact (`synthesizes: "popup"|"dialogParts"`) rather than `node.type`, for the same reason.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 244 tests, all passing, up from 210 (34 new: `overlaySynthesis.test.js`, `submenuController.test.js`, `overlayLayout.test.js`, `overlayTypes.test.js`, plus one added to `compiler.test.js` proving submenu's items compile as real per-item nodes - an unresolved action reference on one is attributed to the item's own id, not the submenu's), and three existing tests updated (`compiler.test.js`'s contract-checking test moved from `dropdown` to `numberDisplay` as its still-placeholder example; `componentTypes.test.js`'s "still placeholders" list and fallback-style check updated for the three promotions). `node build.mjs app` succeeds, 24 loose ends unchanged, since nothing in `app.yaml` authors any of the three. `node build.mjs dist` could not run - a pre-existing environment mismatch (installed esbuild binary is a different version than the host expects), unrelated to this spec, matching prior specs' own notes about this same machine.

Not done and recorded as such: `menu` remains a placeholder; nested submenus; any compiler-side vocabulary for opening a `modalDialog` from an authored action; arrow-key/Enter keyboard operation of an open dropdown/submenu beyond Tab/Escape (matching listbox's own already-recorded gap); a generated (non-preview) application's popup scroll offset and dialog input value are not compiler-declared fields, so they fall back to `setField()`'s existing undeclared-field tolerance there (consequence-free today, since nothing authors any of the three). No browser verification - this environment still has no Playwright/Chromium install (checked again), matching spec 019's own record; verification here is the unit suite plus direct `computeGenericLayout()`/controller-level integration tests. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-11 — Spec 021: tabPanel, collapsePanel and buttonGroup implemented (P5's fifth and last implementing cluster)

The fifth implementing cluster from spec 016's dependency order - "composition" - and, per that same dependency order, the last one: once these three exist, package P5's own remaining work is the screens themselves, not another type. Two of the three needed a real decision spec 016 deliberately left open, re-read against the v1 source rather than guessed: `collapsePanel` (`script/src/ui/components/collapsePanel.js`, 33 lines) genuinely does not collapse anything in v1, and this spec decided it should, since a header a user cannot click is worse than a decorative label and the mechanism spec 020 built for showing/hiding an overlay generalizes cheaply to an in-flow container's content too; `buttonGroup` (no v1 source at all) had its spec-016-guessed `childType: "button"` read again against what `radioGroup`'s own `childType` shape actually does (`compiler/convert.js`'s `convertItems()`) and found to be `radioGroup`'s own "the group dispatches, not the item" contract - exactly wrong for independent buttons, so it was dropped in favour of the bare `{ childrenIn: "items" }` shape `listbox`/`dropdown`/`submenu` already use.

The one new mechanism: a container's children can be conditionally absent from layout without being an overlay. `runtime/genericLayout.js` gained two options alongside `overlayOpen`, both defaulting to their pre-021 behaviour so no existing caller is affected: `activeTab(node)` (only a tabPanel-like node's active item gets a rect at all; every other item is treated exactly as a closed overlay already is - no rect, no clip, no draw, no hit) and `collapsed(node)` (a collapsible node's children get no rect at all when true). A new per-type registry fact carries the geometry each mechanism needs generically: `tabHeader: { height }` for the reserved tab-strip height (read by both the layout pass and the draw kind, so the two can never disagree about where the header ends) and `collapsible: true` for the toggle.

Both new behaviors are aliases, following spec 020's own `disclose`/`flyout` precedent of a second name for an existing controller factory when only the *meaning* of an activation differs: `"tabs"` is a second name for `listController.js`'s existing controller (a press-and-release on a resolved tab index is exactly its own "click an item to select it" case, with `mountGeneric.js`'s new `tabIndexAt()` supplying `scrollable: false` so nothing about dragging or the wheel ever fires), and `"collapsible"` is a third name for the plain button controller (its own click writes the *negation* of its bound field through the same `applyValue()` every value-like control already uses, rather than dispatching a command - one small, behavior-name-gated branch in `feed()`, the same shape spec 020's dropdown-toggle branch already has). Because a tab switch resolves to the identical `{ changed: true, value: index }` shape a slider's drag or a listbox's click already produces, `feed()`'s existing generic branch handles it with no new code there at all.

`tabIndexAt()` deliberately does not reuse `listbox`'s own `itemIndexAt()`: a tab button is not a real per-child rect the way a listbox row is (only the *active* item ever has one), so it instead divides the tabPanel's own rect's header strip into equal-width slots - a documented simplification over v1's own per-label-width tab buttons, consistent with spec 010's decision to reduce visual fidelity, and correct because a synthetic strip position has no font access anywhere near where the pure geometry lives.

Found while designing, before any code: `buttonGroup`'s spec-016 contract would have made every authored button's own `actions:` vanish silently, exactly the class of bug the registry was built to catch generically - `convertItems()` (the function `childType` selects) only carries `name`/`label`/`rule`/`style` onto an item, nothing else, which is correct for `radioGroup` (whose group, not its items, owns the dispatch) and would have been silently wrong for a cluster of otherwise-ordinary buttons.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 262 tests, all passing, up from 244 (18 new: `compositionLayout.test.js`, `compositionTypes.test.js`, plus one added to `compiler.test.js` proving `buttonGroup`'s items are independent, full-treatment nodes - each keeps its own type and its own `actions:`, unlike `radioGroup`'s forced-childType shape), and one existing test updated (`componentTypes.test.js`'s "still placeholders" list, now down to `numberDisplay`, `scrollbar` and `tooltip`). `node build.mjs app` succeeds, 24 loose ends unchanged, since nothing in `app.yaml` authors any of the three.

Not done and recorded as such: `runtime/genericLayout.js`'s `measureIntrinsic()` cannot see `activeTab`/`collapsed`, so an `"auto"`-sized tabPanel or collapsePanel would measure the sum of *all* its children rather than only the ones actually shown - consequence-free today (nothing authors either with `layout: auto` yet), recorded rather than silently accepted, and would need every one of `measureIntrinsic`'s many call sites to also carry the layout options object to fix properly; v1's tabPanel footer strip and its `Layout.trackControlHeight` constant (an authored screen's concern, not this generic type's); per-tab/per-row hover highlighting (matching listbox's own already-recorded scope cut); variable tab-button widths sized to each label's own text; any authored use in `app.yaml`. No browser verification - this environment still has no Playwright/Chromium install (checked again), matching specs 019/020's own record; verification here is the unit suite plus direct `computeGenericLayout()`/controller-level integration tests. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-11 — Spec 022: four of the six screens authored (info bar, edit pad, options panel, song and pattern list)

The screens step of P5, following spec 021's composition cluster. Six v1 surfaces were named as this step's scope: the menu bar, the control panel, the options panel, the info bar, the edit pad, and the song and pattern lists. Each one's actual v1 source was read rather than assumed (recorded as an inventory table in requirements.md) before authoring anything.
