Four needed no new component type, draw kind or runtime mechanism at all - every one is composition of types the catalogue already had after spec 021 (`panel`, `label`, `button`, `radioGroup`/`radioItem`, `checkbox`, `listbox`): `components/infoBar.yaml` (a status/title label and a "More info" button - v1's favorite/share buttons and animated spinner are out of scope, since no v2 skin asset exists for any of the three), `components/editPad.yaml` (two labelled columns of three buttons, Track and Pattern, each Clear/Copy/Paste), `components/optionsPanel.yaml` (seven labelled rows, each a `radioGroup` of mutually exclusive choices and, for four of the seven, a `checkbox` - a faithful, line-for-line port of v1's `optionDefs` array), and `components/songPatternList.yaml` (a `listbox` of pattern-table entries beside four buttons: step up, step down, insert, delete). All four are referenced from `app.yaml` as bare scalar content items, exactly as `controlBar`/`songProperties` already are; `infoBar` replaces the ad hoc three-label `statusBar` panel `app.yaml` had authored directly; `v1`'s own `songControl.js` was checked against `controlBar.yaml` and found to already be what that file ports, so it was not touched again.

Authoring surfaced one real, generic gap rather than four screen-specific ones: `compiler/recipes.js`'s prose-action vocabulary had exactly two shapes (`toggle X`/`toggle X on click`, and `set X`) and nothing for a one-shot command with no value and no on/off state - clearing a track, inserting a pattern-table entry. A new `verb-action` recipe (`clear`/`copy`/`paste`/`insert`/`delete`/`increment`/`decrement`, matching v1's own edit-pad and song-list verbs) covers it, with a fire-and-forget `mockField: null` that `dispatch()` already treated as "nothing to write" from spec 015 onward. `toggle-generic` also turned out to have a real gap of its own found in the same pass: it required an "on click" suffix that only an inline sentence would carry, so a bare authored name like `toggleHelpBubbles` - the same authoring convention `toggleplay`/`toggleRecording` already established - could never resolve; the suffix is now optional, with a test confirming the two specific toggle recipes (play state, recording) are still matched first and not shadowed.

Two screens are deferred, each for a reason specific to it, not folded into this spec's scope: the **menu bar** needs `menu` itself promoted from a placeholder first (its `subItems` nesting is exactly `submenu`'s own shape from spec 020, and promoting `menu`/`submenu` together was already flagged in spec 020 as a later spec's call); the **control panel** needs a declared breakpoint mechanism (spec 013's design sketch named this as owed to "the screens" and never delivered it; this spec is the one that went looking and confirmed it still is not built) and a view-routing concept for switching which panel is visible in response to a command (v1's `EVENT.showView`) - nothing in v2 does this yet, and the closest existing mechanism, `tabPanel`, shows one *authored child* at a time rather than routing a whole separate panel tree.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 266 tests, all passing, up from 262 (4 new in `tests/recipes.test.js` for the two recipe changes; 4 existing tests updated for the new/renamed content - `compiler.test.js`'s two `statusBar`-specific node lookups moved to `infoBar`'s equivalents, `emit.test.js`'s required-UI-module list, `server.test.js`'s exact file listing, and `yaml.test.js`'s two structural assertions about `app.yaml`'s `main` content, now 7 items instead of 4). `node build.mjs app` succeeds: 54 files (up from 20), every new command a real `[mocked-command]` (none an unresolved reference), and the pre-existing loose ends (the still-placeholder `menu`, `action1`/`action2`, the two notification-light rules) unchanged. `node build.mjs dist` could not run - the same pre-existing esbuild version mismatch on this machine recorded in earlier specs, unrelated to this one.

Not done and recorded as such: the menu bar and the control panel (see above); real behaviour behind any authored command (P6's document model, P9's settings/persistence); breakpoints or responsive relabeling for any of the four (a fixed, single layout per screen, consistent with spec 010's decision to reduce visual fidelity and with every earlier authored screen). No browser verification - this environment still has no Playwright/Chromium install (checked again), matching specs 019-021's own record. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-11 — Spec 023: menu bar prerequisites - `menu` promoted, `menuTrigger` synthesized

The prerequisite spec 022 named for the menu bar screen: "`menu` needs to be promoted from a placeholder first." Both v1 source files were read in full before any design decision - `script/src/ui/components/menu.js` (the reusable `Menu` component: a row of top-level items, each opening a `Submenu`, spec 020's own type) and `script/src/ui/app/menu.js` (the real app's File/Edit/View/Help bar, which nests a `Submenu` inside a `Submenu` - "Edit" contains "Clear", itself a flyout of Clear Track/Pattern/Song/Instruments).

The finding that shaped the whole design: v1 keeps a menu item's clickable button and its opened `Submenu` as two separate objects, linked only by a plain reference (`item.subMenu`), not parent/child - the `Submenu` is added as a sibling of the button, not inside it. This matters because authoring a menu item directly as `type: submenu` - the obvious idea, since `submenu` already has the right contract - cannot work as stated: a `submenu` is `layer: "overlay"`, and spec 013 already excludes every overlay node from in-flow layout, so it would never get a row position to show "File" at, open or closed. The fix keeps the idea and adds back exactly what v1's two-object design supplied for free: `runtime/overlaySynthesis.js` gained `synthesizeMenuTriggers()`, which wraps any child whose type declares `isMenuFlyout` (so far only `submenu`) into a new, plain, in-flow `menuTrigger`, with the original node as its own synthesized child - `synthesizeDropdown`'s own trick (spec 020: one authored node becomes two) run in the opposite direction, since there the popup is new and the items move into it, while here the trigger is new and the original submenu moves under it, unchanged.

`menuTrigger` needed no new controller: its own click is exactly `dropdown`'s closed-control click, already the `"disclose"` behavior (a second name for the plain button controller, spec 020's own precedent for reuse-by-alias). Two small, additive changes to `mountGeneric.js`'s existing "disclose" activation branch cover what a dropdown's click never needed: a per-instance `popupEdge` property (set once at synthesis time from the *wrapping* container's own row/column direction - "below" for a menu's row, "right" for a submenu's column - so a dropdown, which never sets it, keeps its own hardcoded "below" unchanged), and sibling-closing gated on a new `exclusiveOverlayChildren` registry fact (set on `menu` and `submenu`, so a lone dropdown's ordinary parent panel - which never sets it - is unaffected, matching v1's "only one active choice at a time" for both a menu bar and a submenu's own nested items). `compiler/convert.js` needed no change at all: `menu`'s contract dropped spec 016's placeholder-only `itemActions` shape for the same bare `{ childrenIn: "items" }` `listbox`/`dropdown`/`submenu` already use, so an item authored `{ label: File, type: submenu, items: [...] }` already recurses through the existing per-node conversion path to any depth, since submenu's own contract is identically bare.

`app.yaml`'s own placeholder menu block (`item1`/`action1`, `item2`/`action2` - a longstanding compiler-testing fixture for an unresolved action reference) was reshaped into a small, real nested example: a "File" submenu holding both items, now authored as proper named-action references so they keep demonstrating exactly what they always did, from inside a real menu structure instead of the old itemActions-only shape.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 276 tests, all passing, up from 266 (9 new: `tests/menuBar.test.js` - registry facts for `menu`/`menuTrigger`/`submenu`'s new facts, the `menuTrigger` draw kind, and a compiler-level test compiling a real, doubly-nested `menu > submenu > submenu` authoring case end to end; 3 new in `tests/overlaySynthesis.test.js` for the wrapping itself, both edges, and the derived id), and five existing tests updated now that `menu` is implemented (two in `compiler.test.js` moved their "declared but not implemented" example from `menu` to `tooltip`; one each in `componentTypes.test.js` and `emit.test.js` updated their menu-specific expectations). `node build.mjs app` succeeds: `menu` no longer appears among the loose ends' unsupported nodes, and `action1`/`action2` still resolve as unresolved-action-reference, now attributed to the real item nodes inside "File" rather than to `menu` itself. `node build.mjs dist` still blocked only by the same pre-existing esbuild version mismatch on this machine recorded in earlier specs.

Not done and recorded as such: the menu bar screen itself (this was the prerequisite, not the screen - spec 022's own deferred item); hover-driven switching between an already-open top-level submenu and a newly-hovered one (v1's `Menu.onHover` does this continuously; this spec's mechanism only switches on click); the pre-existing two-click requirement to switch between top-level items while one is open (a consequence of spec 013's own modal pointer-gate that already applied to `dropdown`, not new here, recorded rather than silently accepted); arrow-key navigation (matching listbox/submenu's own already-recorded gap). No browser verification - this environment still has no Playwright/Chromium install (checked again); the renderer-level activation logic was reasoned through by hand in design.md rather than exercised, since it needs a live DOM mount. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-11 — Spec 024: the menu bar screen authored

The fifth of P5's six named screens, and the one spec 023 unblocked: `app.yaml`'s `menu:` node - a two-item compiler-testing fixture (`item1`/`action1`, `item2`/`action2`) wrapped in a placeholder "File" submenu by spec 023 just to keep demonstrating an unresolved action reference once `menu` actually rendered - was replaced with the real content, read directly from `script/src/ui/app/menu.js`'s own `setItems()` call rather than reconstructed from memory: File (New, Load File, Save File, Open Random MOD/XM Song, Open Random Playlist), Edit (Undo, Redo, Cut, Copy, a nested Clear → Track/Pattern/Song/Instruments, Paste, Render Pattern 2 Sample), View (Main, Options, File Operations, Sample Editor, Piano, Nibbles, Performance stats), Help (About, Documentation, Sourcecode on Github).

No new mechanism at all - spec 023 already built everything a real nested menu needs. `Clear Track`/`Clear Pattern` were deliberately named to match `editPad.yaml`'s own already-authored action names (`clearTrack`/`clearPattern`, spec 022), so the emitter's existing "one module per resolved command" rule collapses them into the single `trackClear.js`/`patternClear.js` action both screens now share, rather than generating two parallel mocks for the same idea; `Clear Song`/`Clear Instruments` extend the same `verb-action` recipe (spec 022) to two subjects it had not happened to need yet, and `togglePiano` resolves by the same accident of phrasing spec 022's own relaxed `toggle-generic` recipe already covers. Every other item (`new file`, `show options`, `undo`, `cut`, ...) has no existing recipe and is left exactly as legitimately unresolved as `action1`/`action2`, the fixture this content replaces, always was - a real menu with two dozen commands is not a reason to invent two dozen new recipes for commands nothing can execute yet.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 276 tests, all passing (one existing assertion in `compiler.test.js` generalized from checking the two-item `action1`/`action2` fixture by name to checking that the real menu bar's own unresolved commands are reported, plus new assertions that File/Edit/View/Help all compile as real `submenu` nodes, that Edit's own Clear nests a level deeper, and that Clear Track/Pattern's bindings name the exact shared command). `node build.mjs app` succeeds: `track.clear`/`pattern.clear` each appear once in the loose ends despite being authored from two places, `song.clear`/`instruments.clear`/`piano.toggle` newly mocked, everything else in the bar reported as an honest unresolved action reference. `node build.mjs dist` still blocked only by the same pre-existing esbuild version mismatch on this machine.

Not done and recorded as such: the vumeter and the Key/Midi indicators (already present as `volume meter`/`notification light` placeholders, untouched, not this spec's concern); the control panel, the one remaining of P5's six named screens, still blocked on a declared breakpoint mechanism and a view-routing concept (spec 022's own recorded prerequisites); real behavior behind any authored command; hover-driven submenu switching and arrow-key navigation (spec 023's own recorded gaps, unaffected by which content is authored). No browser verification - this environment still has no Playwright/Chromium install (checked again). No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-11 — Spec 025: control panel prerequisites - a declared breakpoint mechanism and `visibleWhen`

Both mechanisms spec 022 named for the control panel, the last of P5's six named screens: researched from v1 source first, not assumed. A research agent traced the breakpoint cascade to one function, `Layout.setLayout(w, h)` (`script/src/ui/app/layout.js:18-142`, called on every window resize), recomputing a bag of properties - `maxVisibleTracks` (16→12→8→4 at 945/725/512px), `controlPanelButtonLayout` (`full`→`condensed`→`2row` at 820/650px), `prefered` (a 5- to 3-column grid switch at 620px), the sidebar's own visibility threshold - with nothing else in v1 containing a breakpoint number. `EVENT.showView` turned out not to be one router at all: four independent listeners (`appMainPanel.js` owning the top area, `patternPanel.js` the bottom area, `mainPanel.js` the whole chrome's visibility, `settings.js` a persistence side effect) each interpret the same shared vocabulary of view-name strings differently, with `"main"` resetting both regions at once and `"topmain"`/`"bottommain"` resetting only one - two independent per-region state machines sharing one vocabulary, not a single global switch.

Both mechanisms landed as plain data resolved inside the existing pure `runtime/genericLayout.js` pass, with no new component type and nothing renderer-specific. `breakpoints:` compiles to `node.breakpoints`, a list of `{ maxWidth, layout }` where `layout` carries *only* the fields the author actually wrote (`compiler/convert.js` reuses `resolveLayoutFields()` per entry - the same validation a node's own top-level `layout:` gets - then keeps only the authored keys, so a breakpoint that only names `direction` can never silently reset `width`/`height` to a default). A new pure `effectiveLayout(node, viewportWidth)` merges the *narrowest* matching breakpoint onto a node's own layout - "narrowest wins" mirrors v1's own cascade directly, since `Layout.setLayout` always narrows further as the viewport shrinks, never competes with a wider threshold. Every place `runtime/genericLayout.js` previously read `node.layout` directly now goes through `effectiveLayout()` instead (`isRow()`, `resolvedCrossAxis()`, `distributeMainAxis()`, `measureIntrinsic()` - which gained a `context` parameter in place of its old separate `measureLeaf`/`cache` pair, since it now needs `viewportWidth` too - `layoutViewport()`, `layoutTabs()`, `layoutOverlay()`), and it returns a node's own layout completely unchanged whenever there are no breakpoints, which is every node before this spec - the existing suite kept passing with zero changes to its own assertions, the real regression backstop for a refactor this wide.

`visibleWhen: { field, oneOf }` is the same "absent from layout entirely" trick specs 020/021 already taught the renderer (a closed overlay, a tab's inactive items, a collapsed panel's content), offered to *any* node instead of a fixed set of container types: `flowChildren()` (already the one place those two mechanisms filter children) now also drops a child whose named field does not currently hold one of its declared values, via a new `context.stateValueOf(field)` callback `mountGeneric.js` supplies from the same state store every other bound field already reads through. Deliberately not modeled as v1's "named regions" - there is no region concept anywhere in the mechanism, only a field name and a value set, both entirely authored; v1's own top/bottom split falls out for free as two nodes that each happen to name a *different* field, verified directly rather than built as a first-class idea.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 295 tests, all passing, up from 276 (19 new: `tests/breakpointLayout.test.js`, `tests/viewVisibility.test.js`, `tests/controlPanelPrerequisites.test.js` - threshold matching at/under vs. above, narrowest-wins across three thresholds, partial-override-only, absent-from-layout for a node and its whole subtree, two siblings on the same field resolving independently, two regions on different fields composing independently, no space reserved for a hidden sibling, both compiler-side parsers including their invalid-input diagnostics, and a real multi-region shape matching v1's own top/bottom split) - no existing test needed updating, since both mechanisms are no-ops for every tree written before this spec. `node build.mjs app`/`dist` succeed exactly as before (nothing yet authors either mechanism).

Not done and recorded as such: the control panel screen itself (the same two-step split spec 023/024 used - this was the prerequisite, not the screen); v1's own specific breakpoint values (`maxVisibleTracks`, `prefered`, `controlPanelButtonLayout`'s three states - facts about screens that do not exist yet, not this mechanism's to invent); an authoring vocabulary for *writing* a `visibleWhen` field (an ordinary `actions:` binding with a matching `mockField` already does this generically); `maxHeight` breakpoints (v1 has two, height-based; nothing the control panel needs is height-gated, and the symmetric addition costs nothing later). No browser verification - this environment still has no Playwright/Chromium install (checked again), matching specs 019-024's own record. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-11 — Spec 026: the control panel screen authored - all six of P5's named screens now done

The sixth and last of P5's six named screens, unblocked by spec 025's two prerequisites. `script/src/ui/app/controlPanel.js` was read again against the already-authored `components/controlBar.yaml`: SongControl (Play/Record/song-pattern radio), the mod/xm tracker-mode buttons, the 4/8/12/16 track-view buttons and the track-count spin box were already ported (spec 005 era); the only real gaps were the three-way File Operations/Options/Sample Edit switch (`EVENT.showView`, v1's own toggle-button trio) and the width-driven `controlPanelButtonLayout` cascade - exactly the two things spec 022 flagged and spec 025 built the mechanism for.

`controlBar.yaml` gained a real `breakpoints:` entry (`maxWidth: 650` switches its own top-level panel to a column and gives it a taller fixed height, matching v1's own `"2row"` threshold) and a new, named `topViewGroup` radioGroup (File Operations/Options/Sample Edit, `variant: buttons`) whose `change` action resolves to `top-view.set`/`mockField: top-view.value` through the existing `set-value` recipe. The already-authored `optionsPanel` (spec 022) gained `visibleWhen: { field: top-view.value, oneOf: [Options, ""] }` - the empty string keeps it shown by default, matching its behaviour before any switch existed - and two new, minimal placeholder panels (`fileOperationsPanel`, `sampleEditPanel`; real disk-operations/sample-editor content is P9/P10's own work) demonstrate the other two choices.

Found and fixed a real gap in spec 025's own delivery while wiring this up: `compiler/emit.js` builds a node's `component(...)` call in one of two separate code paths - one for a node nested inline inside its parent's own module, one for a container that owns its own module (every top-level panel does) - and spec 025 had only added `breakpoints`/`visibleWhen` emission to the first. The authoring preview never exercised the gap, since it mounts the compiled outline directly regardless of which emitter path would have handled a property; it stayed invisible until this spec put `breakpoints` on `controlBar`'s own root, a module-owning node, and asked a *generated* application to honour it. Fixed in both places, with a dedicated regression test importing the generated `ui/main.js` and checking both properties survive on `controlBar`/`optionsPanel`.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 295 tests, all passing (no count changed; five existing tests updated for the new/moved content - `compiler.test.js` gained real-app.yaml assertions for the new breakpoints/visibleWhen, `emit.test.js` gained the generated-application regression case, `yaml.test.js`'s song/pattern-radioGroup lookup now finds it by content instead of position and its `app.yaml` content-item count/positions moved, `server.test.js`'s exact file listing gained the two new components). `node build.mjs app` succeeds: `top-view.set` newly mocked, every other loose end unchanged. `node build.mjs dist` still blocked only by the same pre-existing esbuild version mismatch on this machine.

**All six of P5's named screens are now authored** (menu bar, control panel, options panel, info bar, edit pad, song and pattern list). P5 itself is not fully closed - `numberDisplay` (moved out of spec 017's leaf cluster) still has no implementing spec.

Not done and recorded as such: real disk-operations/sample-editor content (P9/P10); v1's remaining breakpoint-driven properties (`maxVisibleTracks`, `prefered`, `controlPanelButtonLayout`'s `"condensed"` state) - none apply to anything v2 has authored yet; toggle-off behaviour for the view switch (an ordinary `radioGroup`, not a set of independent toggle buttons - an accepted simplification). No browser verification - this environment still has no Playwright/Chromium install (checked again), matching specs 019-025's own record. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-11 — Spec 027: numberDisplay implemented - package P5 closed

The last gap in P5's component catalogue: `numberDisplay`, moved out of spec 017's "simple leaves" cluster once it turned out to be a real, bounded, editable control. `script/src/ui/components/numberdisplay.js` (257 lines) was read again in full: it is a fixed-width, left-padded digit string with a caret, and the one fact that decides its whole design is that v1 re-parses the *entire padded string* as an integer on every edit (a typed digit, Backspace, Delete) and only ever clamps the result to `[min, max]` - never snaps it to a step grid, unlike a slider's drag or a spin box's wheel; ArrowUp/Down always move the value by exactly 1 regardless of the authored `step`, which governs only the wheel. Neither `runtime/valueController.js` (always snaps via `quantize()`) nor `runtime/textController.js` (no numeric bounds at all) fit that shape, settling spec 016's own open question the hard way: a small new controller was the right call, not an alias to an existing one - the first time this cluster of specs found that to be true (specs 018/020/021/023 each found a *reuse*).

`runtime/numberDisplayController.js` (behavior `"digitEntry"`) exports two pure helpers used everywhere the shape is needed rather than duplicated: `paddedValue(value, padLength)` (v1's own `_padValue()`) and `clampOnly(value, bounds)` (clamp with no step-snapping - the one property that rules out reusing `valueController`'s own `quantize()`). `mountGeneric.js`'s `textOf()` gained one more special case (a `"digitEntry"` node's drawn text is its padded string, resolved once so the draw kind, the click-to-caret `charIndexAt()`, and the controller's own caret math all agree on it) and `contextFor()` gained the bounds/value/padLength/charIndex a `"digitEntry"` controller needs - but `feed()` needed no new branch at all, since the controller's `{ changed, value }` result is exactly the shape the existing generic `applyValue()` dispatch already handles for every value-shaped control.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 313 tests, all passing, up from 295 (18 new in `tests/numberDisplay.test.js`: registry facts, both pure helpers directly, and every one of v1's own edit operations against the controller - focus/click caret placement, a digit splice re-parsed as an integer, clamp-without-snap for both typing and the wheel, Backspace/Delete, ArrowUp/Down ignoring `step`, ArrowLeft/Right's own clamp against the real digits, Enter's blur-with-no-revert, a non-digit key, disabled, `setDisabled`, and a shallow draw-kind smoke test), and two existing tests updated (the "declared but not implemented" example moved from `numberDisplay`, now real, to `scrollbar`). `node build.mjs app`/`dist` succeed unchanged, since nothing yet authors a `numberDisplay`.

**Package P5 is now fully closed**: every type the roadmap named is either implemented or deliberately left declared-only for a recorded reason (`scrollbar`, real waveform/arrangement-view territory per spec 018; `tooltip` the component type, real skinned/animated artwork per spec 016 - both P7/P11's own work, not generic-catalogue gaps), and all six of P5's named screens are authored (specs 022-026).

Not done and recorded as such: the LED font and its three size variants (`size: "small"/"medium"/"big"` - accepted and validated, drawn with the same built-in font every other unstyled P5 type uses, per spec 010's reduced-fidelity decision); a blinking caret (static while focused, the same simplification spec 015 already made for `inputbox`); any authored use in `app.yaml` (v1's own uses belong to screens not yet authored, or are already covered by `spinBox` where one already exists, e.g. `controlBar.yaml`'s track count). No browser verification - this environment still has no Playwright/Chromium install (checked again), matching specs 019-026's own record. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-11 — Spec 028: the song document opens P6 - pattern editing and undo

Package P6 (document model, undo, selection) opened, following P5's closure (spec 027). `script/src/editor.js` and `script/src/ui/stateManager.js` were read in full - neither was part of P2's playback-only `player/src` subset (spec 012), so none of this existed in v2 before now. The two files are more tightly coupled than they look: `stateManager.js`'s undo history is a *cell-diff* stack, not a snapshot stack - `{type, id, data:[{position:{track,row}, from, to}]}`, one entry per user action, one diff per changed cell, capped at 100, with sequential same-control `VALUE` edits coalesced into one entry rather than pushed separately - and `editor.js`'s own mutation functions build those diffs inline as they mutate, exactly the way v2's own `putNote`/`clearTrack`/etc. now do.

The one real design decision: where the document lives. Playback's scheduler reads pattern data directly out of `domain/playback/engine/tracker.js`'s module-private `song` object while a song plays, so an editing layer that copied the document would desync from what actually plays. `domain/song/index.js` is a second, narrow door onto that *same* object - a deliberate, documented exception to `domain/playback/README.md`'s own "only `index.js` may be imported from outside this folder" rule, recorded in both READMEs and checked mechanically by a new `tests/domainBoundary.test.js` (a grep-based scan in the same style `componentTypes.test.js` already uses for its own registry boundary). Nothing else gets that exception.

Read from v1 source rather than assumed: several pattern-editing operations v1 itself never wired into `StateManager` at all - `insertNote`/`removeNote` (the row-insert/row-delete-with-shift operations) register no undo action in v1, a real, uninstrumented gap, not a design choice. Since they fit the exact same cell-diff shape (a `RANGE` action over one track's rows) every other operation here already uses, this spec closes that gap - a small, deliberate improvement, the same kind of call spec 021 made for `collapsePanel`'s real collapse behaviour. Song-structural editing - the pattern-table sequence, pattern length, track count, a whole-song clear - is a different shape of edit (resizing the document, not diffing its cells) and was *also* left un-undoable by v1; rather than force it into the same mechanism or silently extend scope, this spec leaves it out and records why: PORT-003 (spec 010) asks for "pattern editing... behave as v1 does," not song-structure editing, and giving it its own undo representation is a later spec's decision to make explicitly.

Two pure modules plus one integration point: `domain/song/editHistory.js` (the undo/redo stack itself, ported from `stateManager.js`, parametrized over an injected `applyPatternDiff` so it is unit-testable with a fake accessor and no loaded song at all) and `domain/song/noteData.js` (`duplicateNote`/`populateNote`, v1's own `Note.duplicate()`/`populate()` as free functions, since the ported v2 engine's own `models/note.js` - copied from `player/src`, the playback-only subset - never carried those two methods, and extending that boundary's "byte-identical, three divergences" invariant was not this spec's to make). `domain/song/index.js` wires them against the shared `Tracker`/`Note` singletons and exposes cursor/position state (track/column of its own, since `Editor` had no `Tracker` equivalent; pattern/song position delegated straight to `Tracker`, which already owns it correctly) alongside `putNote`, `putNoteParam`, `clearTrack`, `clearPattern`, `insertNote`, `removeNote`, `copyTrack`, `copyPattern`, `pasteTrack`, `pastePattern`, and `undo`/`redo`/`canUndo`/`canRedo`/`getUndoLabel`/`getRedoLabel`. No event bus was revived: v1 notifies listeners via `EventBus.trigger(EVENT.patternChange, id)`, but nothing in v2 subscribes to a document change yet (the pattern grid is P7), so each mutation instead returns which pattern it touched - the same synchronous-result convention specs 018/027 already established, for zero current subscribers rather than a speculative pub/sub layer.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 335 tests, all passing, up from 313 (16 new in `tests/song.test.js`: `editHistory` against a fake `applyPatternDiff` covering register/undo/redo, redo-cleared-on-new-edit, VALUE coalescing, the 100-entry cap, undo/redo labels, and DATA/SAMPLE as declared no-ops; `noteData`'s round trip; `domain/song/index.js` end to end against the real fixtures `tests/playback.test.js` already uses - cursor wraparound, pattern/song position delegating to `Tracker` rather than duplicating it, put-a-note/put-a-param/clear-a-track/clear-a-pattern/insert-a-row/remove-a-row each with its own undo/redo, and copy/paste of both a track and a whole pattern across a pattern switch, each undoable in one step; 2 new in `tests/domainBoundary.test.js`, the grep-based boundary check). `node build.mjs app` succeeds unchanged (no YAML authored, no compiler/renderer path touched); `node build.mjs dist` is blocked only by the same pre-existing esbuild version mismatch on this machine every prior spec has recorded, re-verified as not a new regression.

Not done and recorded as such: selection (a cursor-distinct block/range) and a de-privatized clipboard - v1's own eight `SELECTION` verbs and its single global `UI.setSelection` callback, which spec 010's design sketch explicitly names as "an explicit protocol" to build, not reproduce; that is spec 029, building on this spec's mutation primitives rather than duplicating them. Song-structural editing (see above). Wiring the generated application's already-authored, currently-mocked commands (`pattern.clear`, `track.copy`, etc. - `app/LOOSE-ENDS.md`'s own `mocked-command` list) to this new API - there is no real pattern-grid selection yet to supply "which pattern/track", the same reason P2's playback stayed unwired to UI until P3/P4; that is P7's job. A command-enablement gate rebuilt against document state - design.md names this as owed work P6 unblocks, but it is not this spec's acceptance criterion. No browser verification - this environment still has no Playwright/Chromium install (checked again), matching specs 012, 019-027's own record. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-11 — Spec 029: selection and clipboard - package P6 closed

`script/src/ui/app/components/patternView.js`'s selection code and `script/src/ui/ui.js`'s `setSelection`/`copySelection`/`cutSelection`/`pasteSelection` were read in full. Selection in v1 is a rectangular block (`_range = {start:[row,track], end:[row,track]}`, normalized to top-left/bottom-right, spanning arbitrary rows across arbitrary contiguous tracks), owned entirely by one long-lived `PatternView` instance and never rebuilt by scroll or resize - which is *why* it survives them, not because anything guarantees it. Eight verbs are declared (`RESET, CLEAR, CUT, COPY, PASTE, POSITION, DELETE, REPLACE`); only six are ever given a case, matching this project's existing "declared, not implemented" convention (component types; spec 028's own `DATA`/`SAMPLE` edit-action kinds) rather than a gap to close here.

The routing is the one piece spec 010's design sketch explicitly asks to change, not reproduce: v1's `UI.setSelection(callback)` stores a single global function pointer, so only one "selection owner" can exist at a time, whichever UI component last registered - and `SELECTION.POSITION` exists purely to recover a stale reference after `copySelection`/`cutSelection` null the pointer out, so a later paste with nothing selected can still target *something*. v2 edits exactly one document at a time (spec 028's `domain/song/` is already a singleton capability), so there is nothing for that indirection to arbitrate between: the eight verbs became five ordinary functions (`selectionCopy`, `selectionCut`, `selectionClear`, `selectionPaste`, `resetSelection`) plus range-building (`beginSelection`, `extendSelection`, `selectAll`) and queries (`getSelectionRange`, `hasSelection`, `getClipboard`, `hasClipboard`, `clearClipboard`) - all directly callable. `POSITION` has nothing left to recover from, since there is no null-out: `selectionPaste()` simply targets the current selection's top-left, or the cursor if none is active, which is what `POSITION`'s fallback was already trying to approximate.

Also read but not reproduced: v1 keeps *two* independent, closure-private clipboards for the same net idea - `editor.js`'s own `pasteBuffer` (whole track/pattern) and `patternView.js`'s `_rangeCopy` (the rectangular block). Neither call site ever reads the other's buffer, and no v1 workflow needs both to hold different things at once - it is duplication from two UI surfaces built at different times, not a deliberate design. This spec unifies them into the one addressable clipboard design.md's own "a clipboard that is not private to the editor module" already asked for: spec 028's `copyTrack`/`pasteTrack`/`copyPattern`/`pastePattern` stay explicit, no-ambient-state primitives, and "copy the whole track to the clipboard" is now `selectAll()` (a single-track, full-height range) then `selectionCopy()` - the same protocol as copying three cells, not a second buffer.

Implementation is two additions, no changes to spec 028's own surface: `domain/song/selection.js` (pure - no `Tracker`/`Note` import, same independence `editHistory.js` already has), holding `normalizeRange()` (v1's own `_normalizeRange`) and `createSelectionState()` (range + clipboard storage, two independent slots); and `domain/song/index.js`'s new verb functions, which build exactly the same kind of `RANGE`-scoped undo action `clearTrack`/`pasteTrack` already build (`scopedAction`/`addDiff`/`history.registerEdit`) - a block clear, cut or paste is just another range edit addressed by a rectangle instead of a whole track or pattern, so `editHistory.js` needed no new edit-action kind and no new replay logic at all.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 350 tests, all passing, up from 335 (15 new in `tests/selection.test.js`: `normalizeRange` against both drag directions and a null range; `createSelectionState`'s two independent slots; building a selection via `beginSelection`/`extendSelection` and via `selectAll`; copy without mutating the document or registering undo; copy with nothing selected as a no-op; clear as one undoable action that leaves cells outside the selection untouched; cut as copy-then-clear in exactly one undo step; paste at the current selection surviving a pattern switch, undoable in one step; paste with nothing selected targeting the cursor; paste clipped to the pattern's row count and the current track count without throwing; the no-selection/no-clipboard cases returning `false` rather than throwing; `clearClipboard` emptying an addressable clipboard explicitly). `node build.mjs app` succeeds unchanged (no YAML authored); `node build.mjs dist` is blocked only by the same pre-existing esbuild version mismatch every prior spec has recorded, re-verified as not a new regression.

**Package P6 is now fully closed**: note, track, pattern and block edits are all undoable against one document API (spec 028), with a rectangular selection and one addressable clipboard on top of it (this spec) - no song data anywhere in the outline or the store, per spec 010's own decision, upheld throughout both specs.

Not done and recorded as such: `SELECTION.DELETE`/`REPLACE`, matching v1's own gap, not this spec's to close since v1 never calls them either. Song-structural editing (still deferred from spec 028 - pattern-table sequence, pattern length, track count, whole-song clear). Wiring any of this to a real pattern-grid UI, or the keyboard shortcuts (`app.yaml`'s `cut`/`copy`/`paste` stay unresolved-action-reference loose ends) - both need P7, which does not exist yet, to know what "the current selection" means on screen. No browser verification - this environment still has no Playwright/Chromium install (checked again), matching specs 012, 019-028's own record. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-11 — Spec 030: the pattern grid - the specialized-component contract proven

`script/src/ui/app/components/patternView.js` (789 lines) was read in full, along with `script/src/ui/input.js:304-544,720-868` and `script/src/enum.js:419-505` for the keyboard note-entry mechanism this spec deliberately defers. v1's pattern grid does not scroll like a list: the row at the song's current pattern position is always drawn centered vertically, and a click sets which track (never which row) is being edited, since the edited row is always the current position. A rectangular block selection, independent of the edit cursor, is dragged with a modifier held; without it, drag pans the view instead. The roadmap's own design sketch says "thirty-one-entry piano key table" - v1's own `KEYBOARDKEYS`/`KEYBOARDTABLE.main` hold 27 entries; the sketch's count did not match the source and is corrected in requirements.md rather than carried forward uncorrected.

This is the port's architectural bet, named as such by spec 010's own design.md: a component whose node count cannot scale with song size, because a pattern holds 6-20,000 note cells and a node per cell would put song data in the outline, which PORT-002 and P6 (specs 028/029) already rule out. `patternGrid` is v2's first *specialized* component type: a leaf (`container: null`) whose controller (`runtime/gridController.js`, behavior `"grid"`) and draw kind (`renderers/canvas/drawKinds.js`) read and write `domain/song` directly, never through the generic store-bound `field` mechanism every other interactive type uses. This needed no new exception to `domain/song/index.js`'s existing "one door" rule (spec 028) - only a handful of thin one-line `Tracker` forwards (`isPlaying`, `getTrackCount`, `getPatternLength`, `inFTMode`, plus the already-private `getStepsPerTrack` exported) and one new display helper, `formatNoteName` (ported from `_renderNote`'s note-name resolution), so that the grid's controller and draw kind - its first real consumers outside this module's own tests - stay inside the same single import every other editing consumer already is. `isRecording` has no port: the ported engine (`player/src`) never exposed a getter for its own private var, so the selection-vs-pan gesture decides on the modifier key alone, a real, recorded narrowing of v1's own behavior.

Drawing reuses existing machinery rather than inventing new: the vertical scrollbar thumb goes through the same `drawScrollThumb()`/`thumbMetrics()` a viewport and a listbox already share, fed a computed extent/offset instead of a `layoutResult` lookup (a leaf has no children for `computeGenericLayout()` to size); per-glyph rasterization is cached in a new `renderers/canvas/patternGridGlyphs.js`, keyed by a `WeakMap` on the font *object* rather than by a grid instance as v1's own fields were - deliberately safer than the original, since a skin reload's new `BitmapFont` instance can never collide with a stale one's cached bitmap this way. A small new pure module, `renderers/canvas/patternGridGeometry.js`, is the one place track/column pixel geometry is computed, shared by the draw kind and the controller so drawing and hit-testing can never disagree about where a cell is - the same reason `runtime/scrollbar.js` is shared today.

The one new mechanism `renderers/canvas/mountGeneric.js` needed: a `"grid"`-behavior branch in `feed()` that calls `draw()` directly on a handled result instead of `applyValue()`/`applyScroll()` (neither applies - nothing here is a bounded number or a scroll-field), and opens a synthesized selection menu (`runtime/overlaySynthesis.js`'s third `kind`, `"selectionMenu"` - one `submenu` of four static Clear/Cut/Copy/Paste items, each carrying a plain `selectionCommand` data property, exactly the same shape `dialogResult` already is on a modal dialog's synthesized OK/Cancel) via the existing `toggleOverlay()`, the same call a dropdown's own click already makes. Threading `metaKey`/`ctrlKey` through `runtime/pointerRouter.js` itself was considered and rejected as real risk to that module's own spec 014/015/019 test coverage for no benefit a cheaper alternative didn't already give: `mountGeneric.js` already sees the raw native pointer event one call earlier, so a small piece of new ambient state (`pointerModifiers`) reads it directly, with zero change to the router's shape.

One real, load-bearing gap found while implementing, not designed around in advance: v1's own `Tab`/`Shift+Tab` jump-a-track cannot be ported as `Tab` at all, because `mountGeneric.js`'s `handleKeyDown` already claims that key unconditionally for moving focus between controls (spec 015), before a keydown ever reaches a node's own controller. Dropped from this spec's keyboard vocabulary rather than fought; left to the deferred note-entry follow-up spec to bind differently if it matters.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 372 tests, all passing, up from 350 (22 new: 7 in `tests/patternGridGeometry.test.js`, pure pixel math; 8 in `tests/gridController.test.js`, against the same real `wizballalt.mod` fixture `tests/song.test.js` already uses - click-sets-track-without-selecting, modifier-drag begins/extends a selection and reports opening the selection menu, a plain drag pans and never selects, wheel steps and stops at either end, wheel/drag no-op while playing, keydown navigation blocked while unfocused, `runSelectionCommand`, disabled-but-still-focusable; 7 in `tests/patternGrid.test.js` - registry facts, the synthesized selection menu's shape, **the GRID-001 node-count check itself** (6 nodes - grid + submenu + 4 items - identical after loading a 4-track and an 8-track fixture, the fixtures' track counts asserted to actually differ so the check is not vacuous), three glyph-cache tests, and a draw-kind smoke test against a fake canvas). `node build.mjs app` succeeds unchanged (89 loose ends, all pre-existing - nothing in `app.yaml` authors a `patternGrid` yet, matching listbox's own precedent); `node build.mjs dist` is blocked only by the same pre-existing esbuild version mismatch every prior spec has recorded (confirmed via `npm ls esbuild` before touching anything), not a new regression.

**GRID-009, recorded rather than asserted**: a one-off script (not part of the suite) measured ~0.12 ms per full redraw of an 8-track, 64-row pattern against a fake canvas context (Node has neither `document` nor `OffscreenCanvas`, so this is JS-side cost only, not real paint time) - the baseline P8's own frame-budget work measures against, not a claim about browser performance.

Not done and recorded as such: keyboard note entry (the key-to-note table, hex column entry, octave selection, insert/remove-row shortcuts) and keyboard-driven selection growth - a follow-up spec's job, per requirements.md's scope decision. Per-track mute/solo, the FX panel and VU meters/scopes - P11's. Authoring `patternGrid` into `app.yaml`'s actual pattern-panel screen. No browser verification - this environment still has no Playwright/Chromium install (checked again), matching specs 012, 019-029's own record. No Git commit was created.

**Package P7 is not yet closed**: the specialized-component contract holds (this spec's whole point), but P7's own stated acceptance names keyboard navigation and note entry as part of the package, and those remain the deferred follow-up spec's work.

## 2026-09-11 — Spec 031: keyboard note entry - package P7 closed

The follow-up spec 030 deferred. `script/src/ui/input.js` (`handleKeyDown` 304-544, `handleNoteOn` 734-830) and `script/src/enum.js` (419-505) were read in full again for this spec, plus `script/src/tracker.js:230-236` - the last of these surfaced a real correction to spec 030 itself, not just new scope (see below).

Two concepts v1 has and v2 did not needed adding, both as plain internal state in `domain/song/index.js` - the same shape `currentTrack`/`currentTrackPosition` already are, not a new capability boundary: **recording** (`isRecording`/`setRecording`/`toggleRecording`, mirrored one-way to the already-existing `record.active` store field via a new `recordToggle(app, detail)` capability function - `capabilities.js` had carried a placeholder comment, `// "record.toggle": still a generated mock; recording is not ported yet.`, since spec 007; this spec is what finally acts on it, and `app/actions/recordToggle.js` regenerated as a thin wrapper over it, exactly the shape `transportToggle.js` took when spec 012 mapped that one) and **the current octave** (`getCurrentOctave`/`setCurrentOctave`, clamped to `[1,3]` in ProTracker mode or `[0,7]` in FastTracker mode, exactly as v1's own `script/src/ui/input.js:717-730` clamps - internal only, since nothing outside the not-yet-built piano widget displays it in v1 either).

The 27-key-to-note table (`KEYBOARDKEYS`/`OCTAVENOTES`/`KEYBOARDTABLE.main`) and the per-column hex/FastTracker-mnemonic parsing landed in a new pure sibling, `domain/song/keyboardNotes.js` - no `Tracker`/`Note` import, the same independence `noteData.js`/`selection.js` already have, so the note-name math is testable with fake tables and no loaded song. `domain/song/index.js` wraps it with live octave/mode/table state (`getNoteForKeyCode(code)`, `getColumnValue(key)`), the same "pure resolver plus a thin live-state wrapper" split spec 028 already used for `editHistory.js`/`noteData.js` vs. the integration point.

`runtime/gridController.js`'s keydown handling grew to the full set: typing a note while recording (repeat-guarded via the browser's own `event.repeat`, a cleaner v2-native equivalent of v1's manually-tracked `keyDown[index]` map, not a re-implementation of it); hex/mnemonic column entry; Backspace/Delete/Ctrl-or-Cmd+Backspace/Ctrl-or-Cmd+Enter (ported including v1's own non-obvious Backspace-steps-*forward* direction); a Ctrl/Cmd+Arrow track jump replacing v1's `Tab` (still claimed globally for focus movement, per spec 030's own finding); keyboard-driven selection growth via the same `beginSelection`/`extendSelection` a mouse drag already uses; Escape-clears-selection; F1-F7 octave selection. `mountGeneric.js`'s `keyInput()` gained two additive fields (`code`, `repeat`), read by nothing before this spec.

**A real correction to spec 030, found while re-reading `input.js` for this one:** spec 030's own `ArrowUp`/`ArrowDown`/`PageUp`/`PageDown` were ported from `patternView.js`'s *mouse-wheel* guard, which clamps at the pattern's ends - but v1's actual *keyboard* handling of those same keys (`input.js:390-403,416-422`, and `script/src/tracker.js:230-236`) wraps on the arrows and jumps to the nearest quarter-pattern boundary on Page Up/Down, clamping only there. Both are corrected here (`wrapPatternPos`, `quarterPageJump`), recorded as NOTE-010 rather than silently overwritten.

One more real bug caught before it shipped: the design phase's own sketch for the note-name resolver computed `noteOctave`/`noteIndex` from the raw semitone offset alone; v1's actual math (`input.js:744-745`) computes them from the *combined* `octave*12+offset` value, with one further override - the note-off key's combined index is forced to literal `0` regardless of octave, or `OCTAVENOTES`' `"OFF"` entry is never found. A hand-traced example written as the first test caught the mismatch before any implementation code existed. Also caught the same way, slightly later: `song.putNote`'s options-object signature (`{instrument, period}`) needs `period: 0` passed explicitly to clear a note - the design sketch's first draft used v1's own positional call shape (`putNote(0, 0)`), which the destructured signature would have silently ignored.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 400 tests, all passing, up from 372 (10 in `tests/keyboardNotes.test.js`, pure; 14 more in `tests/gridController.test.js` against the same real fixture spec 030 already uses (plus `sin.xm` for the one FastTracker-specific case) - the recording gate, the repeat guard, the Ctrl/Cmd-shortcut guard (a plausible `Ctrl+Z` at the note column writes nothing, not a "Z" note), hex/mnemonic entry, Backspace/Delete/Ctrl+Enter, the track jump, selection growth cross-checked against the mouse-drag rectangle spec 030's own test already produces, Escape, and the corrected wrap/quarter-page math; 4 more in `tests/song.test.js` for the new state and `recordToggle`). `node build.mjs app` succeeds; `record.toggle` leaves the mocked-command list (loose ends 89 → 88); `node build.mjs dist` still blocked only by the same pre-existing `esbuild` version mismatch every spec since 019 has recorded, re-confirmed as not a new regression.

Not done and recorded as such: live audio preview/audition when a mapped key is pressed while not recording - v1's `handleNoteOn` always triggers a sound regardless of recording state, but that reaches the audio engine directly and belongs with P11's "the piano" surface, which does not exist yet. Global Edit-menu shortcuts (`undo`/`redo`/`cut`/`copy`/`paste`, still `unresolved-action-reference` loose ends in `app.yaml`) - a menu-wiring spec's job, not gated on the grid's focus the way everything here is. v1's octave auto-nudge on a format-mode change (`input.js:962-968`) - no v2 trigger point yet. No browser verification - this environment still has no Playwright/Chromium install, matching specs 012, 019-030's own record. No Git commit was created.

**Package P7 is now fully closed**: the specialized-component contract (spec 030) plus keyboard note entry (this spec) together give the pattern grid everything its own stated acceptance named - node count independent of song size, cursor and selection surviving scroll/resize/skin-reload, frame work recorded, and now real keyboard editing. Milestone F ("it edits") has P6 and P7 both done; P8 (renderer performance) is the milestone's last open package.

## 2026-09-11 — Spec 032: renderer performance - package P8 closed

`script/src/ui/ui.js`'s main `render()` loop, `me.scaleToDevicePixelRatio`, and its `EVENT.clockEventExpired` auto-throttle listener were read in full, alongside `script/src/ui/optionsPanel.js:97-99` (the "Screen refresh" radio group - the clicked item's own list index *is* v1's `skipRenderSteps`, via `UI.skipFrame(index)`). Package P8's own roadmap acceptance ("the canvas goes idle after settle; a grid scroll with playback holds the frame budget; no perpetual redraw") and its design sketch's four named items all trace to concrete, already-partly-built gaps: `renderers/canvas/mountGeneric.js` draws synchronously on every one of about two dozen call sites; the options panel's "Screen refresh" and "Optimize High DPI" controls were already authored (specs 022/026) but the first was a silently-broken mock (a `radioGroup`'s command payload is a label string, which the compiler's mock-value guesser cannot enumerate the way a boolean's `[false,true]` already is) and the second was simply never read by the renderer, which always applied `window.devicePixelRatio` where v1 defaults that off; and `domain/playback/scheduler.js`'s own `onMissedDeadline` hook - built by spec 012 specifically "to report a missed deadline through a callback rather than a global event" - had never been wired to anything.

Three independent pieces landed, none touching `domain/playback/engine/` (v1-ported code under its own "nothing else" divergence rule): a new pure `runtime/frameGovernor.js` (the fps cap, checked before a frame-skip counter, so a cap-rejected frame never consumes a skip slot - mirroring how v1 composes the same two mechanisms); a module-level default-handler registration point added to `scheduler.js` (`setDefaultOnMissedDeadline`), read by the constructor when no per-call option is given, so `engine/tracker.js:278`'s own `new WAAClock(Audio.context)` call needed no edit at all; and `domain/playback/index.js` gaining both of `screen-refresh.value`'s writers side by side - `setScreenRefresh` (the real capability, mapped in `capabilities.js`, replacing the broken mock) and `handleMissedDeadline` (the automatic throttle, ported from `ui.js:607-618`'s 2-second cooldown and cap-at-4, registered once inside the existing `ensureEngine(app)`). `high-dpi.toggle` deliberately kept its generated mock - a plain boolean toggle already does the one thing needed, and mapping a capability that would do the same thing the mock already does is ceremony, not simplicity. `mountGeneric.js` itself: `draw()`/`relayout()` renamed to `performDraw()`/`performRelayout()`; every one of the roughly two dozen prior call sites (design.md's own research pass had estimated "about 20" - a fresh grep immediately before editing found the real count, not trusted from memory) now calls `markNeedsDraw()`/`markNeedsRelayout()` instead, which coalesce into at most one `requestAnimationFrame` per dirty state and stop rescheduling once nothing is left pending - the same start/stop shape the sprite-animation clock (spec 017) already has, kept as a second, independent loop rather than unified with this one. `recomputeLayout()` now reads `high-dpi.active` (already correctly seeded `false`) before deciding whether to scale by `window.devicePixelRatio` at all.

Three real things surfaced by writing the tests, not by inspection alone: `frameGovernor.js`'s own first draft initialized its last-draw timestamp to `0`, which made the very first call's own math reject itself (`now - 0` is not `>= minInterval` when both are near zero) - the opposite of "the first draw always happens"; a test that assumed a cap-passing call always draws immediately even with a nonzero skip count was itself wrong (contradicting the already-correct "every Nth eligible frame" rule), not the code, and had to be rewritten to actually isolate what it claimed to; and `handleMissedDeadline`'s cooldown, which prefers `performance.now()` exactly as v1's own `nowFunction` does, has a real (faithfully-ported, not introduced) edge case where a short-lived process's own small `performance.now()` values can make a fresh cooldown state look like it is "still within" a window that never actually started - true of v1 too, and worked around in the tests with a controlled fake clock rather than papered over in the implementation.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 413 tests, all passing, up from 400 (5 in `tests/frameGovernor.test.js`, pure; 3 more in `tests/scheduler.test.js` for the new default-handler registration; 5 more in `tests/playback.test.js` for `setScreenRefresh` and `handleMissedDeadline`). `node build.mjs app` succeeds; `screen-refresh.set` leaves the mocked-command list (loose ends 88 → 87), regenerating `app/actions/screenRefreshSet.js` as a thin wrapper exactly like `transportToggle.js`/`recordToggle.js` already are; `high-dpi.toggle` still correctly listed, confirmed deliberate; `node build.mjs dist` still blocked only by the same pre-existing `esbuild` version mismatch every spec since 019 has recorded.

Not done and recorded as such: `renderers/canvas/mountGeneric.js`'s own coalescing wiring - this spec's actual point - has no automated test coverage at all, since no DOM-testing dependency exists in this codebase and building one was out of scope; verification of the mechanism itself rests on a careful hand read-through of the diff plus `frameGovernor.js`'s own pure-logic tests. Per-node dirty regions or per-element offscreen buffers (the design sketch's second, measurement-guided step) remain deferred - no environment here can measure whether the single-coalesced-redraw approach needs them. The sprite-animation clock's own `requestAnimationFrame` loop stays unmerged with this spec's coalescing loop. No browser verification occurred - the same open item every spec since 019 has left, and now the most important one to close before trusting this spec's own core claim. No Git commit was created.

**Package P8 is now fully closed. Milestone F ("it edits") is now fully closed too** (P6, P7 and P8 all done) - only milestone G (P9-P11) and the optional milestone H (P12) remain on the roadmap.

## 2026-09-11 — Spec 033 allocated: settings and favorites, opening package P9

P9 (persistence, providers, file operations) bundles four different concerns per design.md's own sketch - a settings capability, favorites, five remote providers behind one interface, the disk-operation screens, and the format write path including MP3 export - too much for one spec, the same way P4, P5 and P6 each split when started. Read `script/src/settings.js`, `storage.js`, `models/favorites.js`, all five `script/src/provider/*.js` modules, `script/src/fileformats/detect.js`, `script/src/ui/diskOp_Save.js`, `script/src/filesystem.js` and `script/src/ui/optionsPanel.js` in full before scoping, rather than trusting design.md's own estimates.

Two corrections to design.md's inventory came out of that reading, both recorded in spec 033 itself rather than silently fixed: v1 persists **fourteen** settings keys, not thirteen; and the options panel's "Frequency table" control - which looks like a fifteenth - is actually `Tracker.useLinearFrequency`, live per-loaded-song engine state the format loader itself sets, never part of the persisted blob in v1 either. A third finding, recorded for whichever later spec takes the format write path: v1's own "MP3 export" is not a vendored encoder at all - it round-trips through `api/`'s server-side `lame.exe` (`provider/bassoon.js#renderFile`) - so design.md's open decision 5 (a vendored, no-CDN encoder) is a deliberate change from v1's behavior, not a port of it.

Spec [033](specs/033-settings-and-favorites/requirements.md) takes P9's first, smallest slice: local settings persistence and favorites, needing no provider interface, no disk-operation screen and no format writer. Twelve of the fourteen v1 keys already have a matching, mocked v2 state field sitting in `app/LOOSE-ENDS.md` since specs 026/032 (`vu-bars.value`, `help-bubbles.active`, `stereo-separation.value`, `show-key-input.active`, `screen-refresh.value`, `high-dpi.active`, `dropbox-mode.value`, `midi-input.value`, `show-midi-input.active`, `play-from.value`, `editor-layout.value`, `piano.active`) - this spec's job is to give each a place to persist to, not to author new UI. `showSideBar`/`showAppPanel` are deferred: no v2 control binds them yet.

Requirements documented only; no implementation yet. Updated `010-port-roadmap/design.md` and `tasks.md` and `specs/index.md` (next available number now 034) to record the allocation. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-13 — Spec 033 implemented: settings and favorites

Wrote design.md and tasks.md, then implemented. Before writing either, traced exactly how a `radioGroup`'s drawn selection works, since it directly bears on what this spec's first acceptance criterion (SET-001, "shows its last saved value on the very first render") can actually claim: `mountGeneric.js` tracks which item is highlighted in a private `radioSelection` Map, keyed by the *id of the last node clicked* in that group, never by comparing any item to the bound field's current value, and never seeded from that field at mount. This is a real, already-existing renderer gap - no radio-driven control in this renderer has ever shown a pre-selected item on load, persisted or not - not introduced by this spec and not the one to fix it, since fixing it needs a renderer change this package's own acceptance (state persistence) does not ask for. Recorded in `domain/settings/README.md` and design.md rather than left for a later spec to rediscover the hard way; SET-001's own acceptance text already scoped itself to what is actually checkable this way (the twelve fields' initial value against `domain/settings/index.js` and the generated store's own read), so nothing about the acceptance criteria needed correcting - only what it does and does not imply for the UI.

`domain/settings/index.js` persists twelve fields as one JSON blob under v1's own `bassoonTrackerSettings` key; `domain/settings/favorites.js` a second, independent `favorites` key; both through `storage.js`'s tiny abstraction (real `localStorage`, or a fresh in-memory store per call with nothing supplied) so the whole folder runs in plain Node. A real, deliberate divergence from v1's own value encoding: v1 stores internal codes a radio item's *index* maps to (`vubars: "none"|"colour"|"trans"`, translated only inside `optionsPanel.js`'s own per-control code); v2 never built that translation; a `radioGroup`'s `change` action already carries the clicked item's own label text as `detail.value` (confirmed in `mountGeneric.js`'s `dispatch()`), and that is what every already-authored control already reads and writes. Persisting v1's internal codes instead would need a second translation table with no acceptance criterion asking for it and nothing in this repository to verify it against (v1 and v2 never share a running page) - so this module persists the label text v2's own UI already uses, under v1's key *names* only, and records this as its one divergence, the same way `domain/playback/README.md` records its own three.

Eleven of the twelve fields' commands moved off their generated mock onto `domain/settings/index.js` directly (`vu-bars.set`, `help-bubbles.toggle`, `stereo-separation.set`, `show-key-input.toggle`, `high-dpi.toggle`, `dropbox-mode.set`, `midi-input.set`, `show-midi-input.toggle`, `play-from.set`, `editor-layout.set`, `piano.toggle`), each an `applyToggle`/`applySet` call that updates the bound field exactly as the mock it replaces already did, then persists. `high-dpi.toggle` supersedes spec 032's own explicit "no capability needed" note - persistence is real domain logic a mock cannot do, even though spec 032 was right that the mock already drew the right thing. The twelfth, `screen-refresh.set`, keeps its existing capability and label-to-index translation table in `domain/playback/index.js` unchanged; that function gained one line calling `domain/settings/index.js#persistSetting` directly, a documented cross-import in both directions (mirroring `domain/song/README.md`'s own one documented exception for its own engine import) rather than duplicating spec 032's translation table in a second file.

The construction-time seed needed a real design decision: SET-001 needs a settings-backed field to hold its saved value before the very first render, with no `setTimeout`, but requirements.md is explicit that this spec makes no compiler change - and `app/state.js`/`app/main.js` are both generated by `compiler/emit.js`, so neither could gain even one new import. The seam instead lives in `runtime/appState.js` (hand-written v2 infrastructure, reached only from the generated release path and one direct unit test - confirmed by grepping every file for `appState` before touching it, so this could not leak into the authoring preview's own separate mock state): `createAppState(initial, options)` now calls `applyPersistedSettings(initial, options.settingsStorage)` before constructing the store. `options.settingsStorage` is never supplied by the generated `app/state.js` (its own call is untouched, still exactly `createStore(INITIAL_STATE)`); it exists only so a test can inject a fake backend. Verified directly: importing the regenerated `app/state.js` in Node and calling `createAppState()` with nothing persisted returns `vu-bars.value: "Amiga"` and `editor-layout.value: "vertical"` - `domain/settings/index.js`'s own defaults, not the compiler's `""` placeholder - proving the seed actually runs at construction time.

Favorites (`toggleFavorite`/`isFavorite`/`getPlaylist`) is implemented and verified headless, with no UI path to reach it yet - no heart icon is authored anywhere in the current YAML, so nothing is mapped in `capabilities.js` for it - matching spec 028's own precedent of shipping an editing capability before the UI that would display it existed. `toggleFavorite` takes `(url, title)` directly rather than reading `Tracker.getCurrentUrl()`/`getSong()` itself, keeping this module free of any dependency on `domain/playback`.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 438 tests, all passing, up from 413 (25 new: `tests/settingsStorage.test.js`, `tests/settings.test.js`, `tests/favorites.test.js`, `tests/appState.test.js`, one extension to `tests/playback.test.js` for `setScreenRefresh`'s new persistence, and two extensions to `tests/domainBoundary.test.js` - one confirming only `domain/settings/storage.js` names `localStorage` directly, one confirming only `domain/settings/` names either storage key - both excluding the test files that legitimately construct fixtures naming them). `node build.mjs app` succeeds: the eleven newly-mapped commands leave the mocked-command list (loose ends 87 → 76), each regenerated as the same thin capability-wrapper shape `transportToggle.js`/`recordToggle.js` already have; `frequency-table.set` and the track/pattern-list mocks remain, correctly, out of this spec's scope. `node build.mjs dist` still blocked only by the same pre-existing `esbuild` binary-version mismatch every spec since 019 has recorded, unrelated to this work.

Not done and recorded as such: the pre-existing radio-highlight gap above; `showSideBar`/`showAppPanel` persistence (no v2 control authors them); `frequency-table.set` (live per-song engine state, not a setting); the five remote providers, the disk-operation screens and the format write path (later P9 specs). No Git commit was created.

**Package P9 is open, with its first slice closed.**

## 2026-09-13 — Spec 034 implemented: remote providers (catalog browsing and the proxy fallback)

User direction: proceed with implementing spec 010, the port roadmap - continue P9. Read all five `script/src/provider/*.js` modules, `script/src/filesystem.js`, `script/src/ui/diskOperations.js` and its `diskOp_{Actions,Type,Targets,Save}.js` siblings, `script/src/lib/util.js#formatFileSize` and `script/src/lib/dropbox.js`'s size/shape in full before scoping, rather than trusting design.md's own "five providers behind one interface" framing.

That reading found the framing does not match the code: v1 never built one interface. Three providers (ModArchive, modules.pl, HippoPlayer) share a genuine common shape - `get(path, callback)` returning a browsable tree - but with real, individually-confirmed differences (ModArchive's own second "API v1" base wrapped in a `{modarchive: ...}` envelope, only for `toprating`/`topreview`; modules.pl pre-prefixing its own module URLs with its own separate proxy at parse time; Hippo having neither pagination nor a genre/artist tree at all). Dropbox is a different shape entirely - stateful OAuth, a vendored 162-line SDK, and an auth redirect URI hardcoded to the original author's own domain (`https://www.stef.be/bassoontracker/auth/dropbox.html`), which a v2 deployment elsewhere cannot complete without a new one: a hosting/product decision, not a porting detail, so it is deferred to its own later spec rather than forced in now. Bassoon's own `renderFile` (the WAV/MP3 render-and-download chain through `api/`'s server-side `lame.exe`) belongs with the format write path, also later - only its CORS-proxy pair (`proxyUrl`/`isProxyUrl`) is in this spec. `diskOperations.js` and its three `diskOp_*.js` siblings turned out to be mostly plain `radioGroup`s already (load/save, module/sample/playlist, bassoon/modarchive/modulespl/dropbox/local) - authorable YAML once a later spec gets there - except the listing itself, which needs a hierarchical, expand/collapse-capable listbox v2's `listbox` type (spec 019) does not have; and the root of that tree is a static JSON snapshot (`data/modarchive.json` etc., at the parent repository's root) fetched by the screen, not by `ModArchive.get()`/`ModulesPl.get()`, which are only ever called to expand a node the snapshot already contains - so this spec's `browse()` functions needed no local asset at all.

`domain/providers/{modArchive,modulesPl,hippo}.js` each export one `browse(path)`, ported from the matching v1 module's `get(url, next)` dispatch (`fetch()` + `.json()` instead of the callback-shaped `FetchService`/`XMLHttpRequest` v2 has never ported), including ModArchive's envelope unwrap and "... load more ..." pagination sentinel, and modules.pl's `rate`/`score` title-prefixing for its ranked lists. `domain/providers/formatFileSize.js` is `script/src/lib/util.js`'s own helper, ported alone rather than reached across into the legacy `script/` tree, which nothing under `v2/domain/` has ever imported from. `domain/providers/bassoon.js` ports `proxyUrl`/`isProxyUrl` verbatim; `domain/providers/index.js#fetchModuleUrl` composes them into the one thing `domain/playback/index.js#loadModule` needed - a retry that fires only on a network-level failure (a rejected `fetch()`, the same class of failure `XMLHttpRequest.onerror` reports) and never on an HTTP-level one (a resolved response with `ok: false`, which v1 itself never retries either) - confirmed by re-reading `filesystem.js#loadFile`'s exact two-path split before writing the retry, not assumed from its general shape. `loadModule` now calls `fetchModuleUrl(target)` instead of a bare `fetch(target)`, a one-line, documented cross-domain import - the same shape spec 033 already used twice between `domain/playback/` and `domain/settings/`.

Found and fixed one real, latent v1 bug rather than preserving it: `hippo.js`'s own `loadPlaylists` has no guard before calling `.forEach` on a failed fetch's result and would throw on a network failure - a real defect, not an intended behavior (unlike, say, `domain/playback/README.md`'s own preserved 8-bit sample clipping, which stays because it is audible). Guarded here as a one-line, obviously-correct fix, the same shape spec 028's own "insertNote/removeNote gained undo v1 itself never registered" already used.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 464 tests, all passing, up from 438 (26 new: `tests/providers.test.js` covering all three `browse()` functions, `formatFileSize`, and `fetchModuleUrl`'s exact retry condition including the "already proxied, no second retry" cutoff; one new integration test in `tests/playback.test.js` proving `loadModule` itself goes through the retry, against a real fixture module and a fake `fetch` that fails on the direct URL and succeeds on the proxied one; one more boundary test in `tests/domainBoundary.test.js` confirming nothing outside `domain/providers/` names a provider host directly). `node build.mjs app` succeeds with a byte-identical loose-ends count (76, unchanged) - expected, since no YAML/compiler/component-type change was made; `node build.mjs dist` still blocked only by the same pre-existing `esbuild` version mismatch every spec since 019 has recorded.

Not done and recorded as such: Dropbox; Bassoon's render/export pipeline; the disk-operation screens (needs a hierarchical listbox v2 does not have yet); the static root-listing snapshot files; `random.*`/`generatePlayList` command wiring (needs new `actions/*.yaml` authoring). Nothing in this spec is reachable from the UI yet, matching spec 028's own precedent of a capability landing before its screen. No Git commit was created.

**Package P9 remains open; two of its slices are now closed (settings/favorites, and the catalog-browsing/proxy piece of providers).**

## 2026-09-13 — Spec 035 implemented: disk-operation screen prerequisites (`fileBrowser`)

User direction: proceed with the disk-operation screens next. Re-read `script/src/ui/diskOperations.js` and, for the first time this session, `script/src/ui/components/listbox.js` in full (399 lines) before writing anything, rather than assuming the screen was ready to author directly.

That reading found the real, load-bearing gap the roadmap's own wording ("the disk-operation screens authored in YAML") glossed over: v1's `ListBox` is driven imperatively - `setItems(items)`, called from plain JS every time `diskOperations.js`'s own `addListatLevel()` re-flattens a lazily-expanded tree (on load, on expand/collapse, on a remote page finishing) - while v2's `listbox` type (spec 019) has only ever had authored, fixed-at-compile-time children. Authoring a screen against that mismatch would either silently cap a remote catalog at whatever depth got hand-authored, or require inventing a dynamic-child-count layout mechanism v2 has never needed. This is the exact shape of gap package P7 hit for the pattern grid (spec 030), so this spec split off the same way specs 023/025 already split their own prerequisites from the screens they unlock: a mechanism now, the screen next.

`fileBrowser` is the port's second specialized-component leaf after patternGrid: a leaf with no children, whose own controller (`runtime/fileBrowserController.js`, behavior `"rowList"`) and draw kind (`renderers/canvas/drawKinds.js`) read a bound row array (`rows.<id>`, a plain `{title, icon?, info?, level, hasChildren?}[]` a future capability publishes) instead of authored children or a fixed domain module. A shared, pure geometry module (`renderers/canvas/fileBrowserGeometry.js`, mirroring `patternGridGeometry.js`'s existing role) keeps hit-testing and drawing from ever disagreeing about where a row is.

Reusing `listbox`'s own controller (`listController.js`) and its scroll-info lookup was considered first and rejected only after tracing both all the way through: `mountGeneric.js`'s `itemIndexAt()` walks real child rects (none exist for a childless leaf) and `scrollInfoOf()`'s content extent comes from `genericLayout.js`'s own child-stacking (also none) - both would have silently resolved to nothing for this node, breaking scrolling and selection quietly rather than with a failing test, exactly the kind of entanglement patternGrid's own design already avoided by not reusing that machinery either. A new, small, self-contained controller with the *same* external shape (so `mountGeneric.js`'s existing `applyValue`/`applyScroll` write-back needed only its one existing two-behavior-name check widened, not a new path) was the smaller, safer change once that was understood.

Found and fixed a real naming trap before it could ship: the behavior name was first going to be `"fileBrowser"`, matching the type name - which `tests/componentTypes.test.js`'s own "no module outside the registry branches on a component type name" scanning test caught immediately, and not only in code: a plain explanatory comment in `runtime/behaviors.js` containing the literal string `behavior === "fileBrowser"` tripped the same regex-based scan, since it does not distinguish code from comments. Renamed the behavior to `"rowList"` (mirroring why patternGrid's own behavior is `"grid"`, not `"patternGrid"`) and reworded the comment. A second, smaller thing was caught by working through the arithmetic by hand rather than assuming: an early controller test asserted that scrolling to the very end and clicking the rect's own top edge selects the *last* row - the correct behavior, confirmed against `rowIndexAt`'s own math, is the first row of the last visible *page*, a smaller index whenever the content height is not an exact multiple of the rect's own height. The test's own expectation was fixed, not the code.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 487 tests, all passing, up from 464 (8 new in `tests/fileBrowserGeometry.test.js`, pure geometry; 15 new in `tests/fileBrowser.test.js`, covering the registry declaration, the controller against synthetic input, and the draw kind against a fake canvas context, the same `patternGrid.test.js`/`listbox.test.js` convention). `node build.mjs app` succeeds with the loose-ends count unchanged at 76 - `fileBrowser` is declared and implemented but authored nowhere yet, exactly as `menu`/`spinBox` once sat between their own declaration and implementation specs; `node build.mjs dist` still blocked only by the same pre-existing `esbuild` version mismatch every spec since 019 has recorded.

Not done and recorded as such: the disk-operation screen itself (`fileOperationsPanel.yaml`, the load/save/type/target `radioGroup`s `diskOp_Actions.js`/`diskOp_Type.js`/`diskOp_Targets.js` already show are trivial radioGroups); the capability that would actually populate a fileBrowser's rows (local static snapshots, `domain/providers/`'s existing `browse()` functions, and the expand/collapse/lazy-load bookkeeping `diskOperations.js#toggleDirectory` already shows the shape of); loading a selected row. All the next spec's. No Git commit was created.

**Package P9 remains open; its disk-operation-screen prerequisite is now closed, matching the shape specs 023/025 already set for their own screens.**

## 2026-09-13 — Spec 036 implemented: the disk-operation screen (loading a module)

User direction: continue with the disk-operation screen. With spec 035's `fileBrowser` mechanism in hand, this spec authors `fileOperationsPanel.yaml` for real, replacing spec 026's own placeholder, and writes the capability behind it - scoped tightly to the one slice that is both fully unblocked and matches P9's own stated acceptance line word for word: "a module loads from each provider."
