Authored a target radioGroup (Bassoon/ModArchive/Modules.pl - Dropbox and local excluded, the first deferred since spec 034, the second already reachable by dropping a file on the canvas since spec 014) and one `fileBrowser`. Rather than re-derive `identity.js`'s naming rules to predict the fileBrowser's own runtime id, the YAML was authored first and `node compiler/generate.js`'s own output read back to confirm it directly: `main.fileOperationsPanel.browser`, and the two commands' own recipe-derived names, `disk-target.set`/`browser-row.set`. `domain/diskOperations/index.js` owns one in-memory tree per target (cached for the application's lifetime, never reset on switching back and forth, matching v1's own per-target caching in `diskOperations.js`), flattening whichever subtree is currently expanded into `fileBrowser`'s own bound row array. A branch's children are fetched lazily, exactly once, through `domain/providers/{modArchive,modulesPl}.js#browse()` (spec 034) unchanged; a leaf loads through `domain/playback/index.js#loadModule` (spec 012) unchanged in its own logic, but now reached through a URL resolved against v2's own asset base first.

That last part was a real bug this spec's own test caught, not something planned for: `loadModule`'s own URL resolution falls back to `globalThis.location`, which does not exist in Node (an immediate `Invalid URL` throw) and would be the *wrong* base even in a real browser - a local snapshot's own relative URL (`"demo/wizballalt.mod"`) is relative to `v2/assets/`, not to wherever the generated application's own page happens to be hosted. Fixed by resolving a leaf's `url` through the same asset-base helper the local snapshot's own fetch already uses, before ever calling `loadModule` - one code path, since a remote catalog's own already-absolute `url` passes through it unchanged.

"Bassoon" needed a decision v1's own `data/modules.json` could not answer directly: sixteen of its twenty-seven entries point at a `demomods/` folder this port never copied (vendoring dozens of tracker files was never this spec's job), so `v2/assets/data/modules.json` is a smaller, curated nine-entry snapshot instead - the one demo module already bundled since spec 012, plus the eight entries whose URL was already absolute and therefore genuinely fetchable from any deployment, unchanged. Recorded as a deliberate scope reduction in requirements.md, not folded in silently. This is the second folder a capability loads from at runtime (the first being spec 012's own demo module), so `build.mjs`'s dist asset-copy step - previously one hardcoded `assets/demo/` block - was generalized to a short list; verified directly with a standalone script against a throwaway directory, since the pre-existing `esbuild` version mismatch in this environment blocks a real `node build.mjs dist` run from ever reaching that code.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 495 tests, all passing, up from 487 (8 new in `tests/diskOperations.test.js`, covering both capabilities against a fake `fetch` and the real `wizballalt.mod` fixture for the actual "loads as the current module" case; one more file added to `tests/domainBoundary.test.js`'s existing provider-host exclusion list). `node build.mjs app` succeeds; loose ends move from 76 to 79 (exactly the three new radio items' own inert-control entries, the same shape every other radioGroup's items already have - no new mocked-command entries, since both `disk-target.set` and `browser-row.set` are real). Hand-read the regenerated `app/ui/fileOperationsPanel.js`, `app/ui/diskTargetRow.js` and both new `app/actions/*.js` wrappers to confirm the emitted code matches design.md's own prediction, not just that the unit suite passes in isolation.

Not done and recorded as such: samples, playlists, save/export, Dropbox, local-file browsing (the format write path and Dropbox's own later specs); `random.*`/`generatePlayList` command wiring and the File menu's own `showFileOperations` item (the panel is already reachable through the control bar's existing radio button, unchanged since spec 026). No Git commit was created.

**Package P9's own stated acceptance is now partly met: settings survive a reload (spec 033), and a module loads from three of its providers (this spec). Exports readable by v1 - the format write path - remains unallocated, the one piece of P9 left.**

## 2026-09-13 — Spec 037 implemented: module export (MOD), the format write path's first slice

User direction: continue with the format write path, the last piece of P9's own stated acceptance. Launched a dedicated research pass (read `editor.js`'s `buildBinary`/`save`, `fileformats/protracker.js`'s `write`, `fasttracker.js`'s `write`/`writeXI`, `audio/riffWave.js`, `models/playlist.js`, and `filesystem.js`'s full `BinaryStream` in full) before writing anything, since P9's own design sketch describes this piece only as "XI, WAV, the playlist formats, and the container formats" - wording that, read against the actual source, turned out to need two corrections rather than a straightforward pickup.

First correction: module export (`Editor.save`/`buildBinary`, dispatching to `ProTracker().write()` or `FastTracker().write()` depending on `Tracker.inFTMode()`) is a real, fully-working, currently-unaddressed v1 feature - not already "out" of anything, and the single most direct way to check P9's own "exports are readable by v1" clause. Second: "the container formats" the sketch names have no write-side referent at all - `zip`/`gzip` in v1 (`fileformats/detect.js`, `tracker.js`'s own `processFile`) are used exclusively to *open* an archive on load, never to produce one. Both corrections are recorded in requirements.md rather than carried forward into a later spec's scope by mistake.

Scoped this spec to MOD export alone - the smallest correct, complete, already-fully-unblocked piece (`domain/song`/`domain/playback` already expose every field `ProTracker().write()` reads), deferring XM (roughly twice the size: delta-encoded sample data, full envelope/vibrato instrument blocks), sample export (WAV/RAW/XI - depends on an instrument/sample-slot UI this port doesn't have), playlist export (depends on a playlist model that doesn't exist at all yet, the same reason spec 036 deferred *loading* one) and MP3 export (the vendored-encoder decision, already recorded, still unimplemented - and a second, dead, CDN-fetching `editor.js#renderToMp3` path was found alongside the real, already-documented server-side one, worth flagging so a later spec doesn't mistake it for something to port).

`domain/moduleExport/binaryWriter.js` is new v2 code, not a divergence of `domain/playback/engine/filesystem.js`'s read-only port: `player/src` never wrote anything, so there was nothing there to diverge from. It ports only the write methods `ProTracker().write()` actually calls, big-endian only (MOD's own multi-byte fields) - a little-endian mode is left for whichever later spec needs one, not built ahead of that need. `domain/moduleExport/protracker.js#write()` ports v1's own MOD writer field-for-field (title, 31 fixed instrument headers, song length/pattern table, format magic, packed pattern data, raw 8-bit sample data), reading `domain/playback/engine/tracker.js`'s live `Tracker` singleton directly - a second documented exception to that folder's own "index.js only" rule, alongside `domain/song/index.js`'s (both READMEs updated to say so, and the domain-boundary test now checks both by name instead of one).

Wiring it needed the one thing every P9 spec since 033 had avoided: a real compiler-adjacent change. The File menu's own `saveFile` item has been an inert `unresolved-action-reference` since the application's very first cumulative spec, and nothing in `compiler/recipes.js`'s existing vocabulary could express a one-shot "save" command - so its `verb-action` recipe (already handling `clear`/`copy`/`paste`/`insert`/`delete`/`increment`/`decrement`) gained `save` as a recognized verb, resolving `"save file"` to command `file.save`, mapped in `capabilities.js` to the new capability. Recorded explicitly as this spec's one exception, not folded in silently.

Verified with a real round trip, not a raw-byte comparison: a fixture module (`wizballalt.mod`) is written back out by the new writer, then reloaded through the *same, already-tested* read path (`Tracker.processFile`), and the two in-memory songs are compared - title, every instrument's name/volume/finetune/loop points/sample length, the pattern table, and every pattern cell's instrument/period/effect/param - all matching exactly on the first real fixture tried. A truncation test (more than 31 instrument slots) and an XM-mode test (a clean, logged no-op rather than a silently-wrong `.mod`) round out the acceptance list.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 505 tests, all passing, up from 495 (7 new in `tests/binaryWriter.test.js`; 3 new in `tests/moduleExport.test.js`; one case added to `tests/recipes.test.js`'s existing one-shot-verb table; the domain-boundary test's own exception list extended). `node compiler/generate.js` - 26 warnings, down from 27 (`saveFile` no longer unresolved). `node build.mjs app` succeeds; loose ends move from 79 to 78; the generated `app/actions/fileSave.js` was read by hand and confirmed to import `domain/moduleExport/index.js#exportModule`. `node build.mjs dist` still blocked only by the same pre-existing `esbuild` version mismatch every spec since 019 has recorded.

Not done and recorded as such: XM export, sample export (WAV/RAW/XI), playlist export, MP3 export, a save/export screen (format/target pickers), Dropbox/local save targets. No browser verification occurred in this environment; the actual `Blob`/`URL.createObjectURL`/`<a download>` trigger has never been exercised outside a real browser. No Git commit was created.

**Package P9's own full stated acceptance is now met for the slices this port has actually built: settings survive a reload, a module loads from three providers, and a module export is readable by v1 (MOD; XM, samples, playlists and MP3 remain later slices of the same package).**

## 2026-09-13 — Spec 038 implemented: XM export

User direction: continue with XM export next - the gap spec 037 deliberately left open (a clean, logged no-op rather than a silently-wrong `.mod`). Read `script/src/fileformats/fasttracker.js#write` (311-553) in full before writing anything, beyond the `writeXI`-only reading spec 037's own research pass already did.

Two real prerequisites surfaced by that reading, neither assumed in advance. First: XM's own multi-byte fields are little-endian, the opposite of MOD's - `domain/moduleExport/binaryWriter.js` (spec 037) had only ever written big-endian, since MOD was its only consumer, so it gained a `bigEndian` constructor flag (`createBinaryWriter(arrayBuffer, bigEndian = true)`), the same one-flag choice v1's own `BinaryStream` constructor already has, not a new concept. Second: the writer needs the song's current tempo and speed, and the playback-only ported `Tracker` (spec 012) never exposed either - `getBPM()`/`getAmigaSpeed()` exist only in `script/src/tracker.js`, one line each, reading private state (`bpm`/`ticksPerStep`) `player/src/tracker.js` already tracks for its own scheduling but never needed to read back out. Added both, verbatim, and recorded them in `domain/playback/README.md` as an *addition*, not a fourth divergence - nothing about existing behavior changes, only a new accessor for a new consumer.

`domain/moduleExport/fasttracker.js#write()` ports v1's own XM writer field-for-field: the identifier/title/tracker-name header block (reading `window.versionNumber`, which has no v2 equivalent, so this falls back to `"dev"`, exactly as spec 037's own `writeXI` research already found), a fixed 256-slot pattern table, uncompressed 5-byte note quintuples per cell (XM's own bit-packed "compressed" encoding is never produced, matching v1, which never writes it either), and per-instrument envelope/vibrato/sample blocks. That last part is where v1 hand-duplicates itself: `write()` and `writeXI()` both carry the identical envelope/vibrato/sample-header/delta-encoding logic, copied rather than shared. This port factors it into one internal `writeInstrumentSamples()` helper instead - not exported yet, since nothing in this spec calls it a second time, but positioned for a later sample-export spec's own `writeXI` port to reuse rather than hand-duplicate a third time.

`domain/moduleExport/index.js#exportModule` now dispatches to whichever writer matches the loaded song's own format (`Tracker.inFTMode()`), exactly mirroring `Editor.buildBinary`'s own branch - the capability's own shape is unchanged, only what it calls. One real, easily-avoidable v1 defect was fixed along the way rather than preserved: `getFileName()`'s own no-filename fallback hardcodes a `.mod` extension regardless of format, so a brand-new XM song saved before ever being named would download as `"<title>.mod"` despite being XM content - the same kind of call spec 034 made fixing `hippo.js`'s own crash, not a behavior worth matching.

Found and fixed one real gap the moment the XM writer tried to use it: `binaryWriter.js` had no `writeDWord`/`writeLong` alias at all (spec 037's own MOD writer only ever called `writeUint` directly, so the gap stayed invisible until `fasttracker.js`, which calls `writeDWord` throughout like v1's own source, hit it immediately as a real `TypeError`). Fixed by adding the same alias v1's own `BinaryStream` already has for reads (`readLong = readDWord = readUint`), mirrored for writes.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 507 tests, all passing, up from 505 (one new little-endian case in `tests/binaryWriter.test.js`; `tests/moduleExport.test.js`'s own former "XM is a no-op" test replaced with two - a full round trip against a real XM fixture, comparing title, every instrument's envelope points/counts/sustain/loop settings/vibrato, every sample's metadata, and every pattern cell's index/instrument/volumeEffect/effect/param, all exactly; and `exportModule`'s own dispatch/extension-naming behavior, the first test in this suite to reach all the way through the DOM-touching download trigger, stubbed minimally rather than skipped). `node build.mjs app` succeeds with loose ends unchanged at 78 (no YAML/capabilities.js touched - `file.save` already dispatched here since spec 037). `node build.mjs dist` still blocked only by the same pre-existing `esbuild` version mismatch every spec since 019 has recorded.

Not done and recorded as such: `writeXI` (sample/instrument export - the real second caller `writeInstrumentSamples()` is now positioned for), WAV/RAW sample export, playlist export, MP3 export, a save/export screen, pingpong loops, XM's own bit-packed pattern encoding. No browser verification occurred in this environment. No Git commit was created.

**Module export is now complete for both formats v2 loads (MOD and XM). Package P9's remaining, unallocated pieces are all sample/playlist/MP3 export and a save/export screen - genuinely new UI/domain territory, not further slices of the writer this and the previous spec already built.**

## 2026-09-13 — Spec 039 implemented: the feature registry, opening package P10

User direction: leave the rest of P9's exports (sample/playlist/MP3, a save screen) - not the interesting part - and move on to P10 ("features and the plugin path").

Read `script/plugins/registry.js` (a flat, static manifest map) and `script/plugins/loader.js#Plugin.load` in full, plus `script/src/app.js`'s own three `Plugin.load("SampleEditor"/"Nibbles"/"Generator", callback)` call sites (73-92, 159-186, 187-200), before writing anything - the roadmap's own design sketch describes P10's acceptance criteria (a cold trace, deduplication, close-during-load, retry) in the abstract, and tracing v1's actual loader line by line found something genuinely useful: it already gives in-flight deduplication and retry-after-failure "for free," not by deliberate design - both fall out of the same two `if` checks (`loaded`/`loading`) simply failing to match once a load has failed, so a third call falls through and starts over. What v1's loader has never had, at all, is any "closed" concept: `app.js`'s own call sites decide what to do with a loaded module with no guard against a user closing a feature's own panel while its import is still in flight.

Scoped this spec to the mechanism alone - `domain/features/index.js` (metadata-only manifests, the same `idle`/`loading`/`ready`/`failed` state machine v1's loader already has, made explicit rather than accidental, plus the one genuinely new piece: an independent `open`/`closed` flag, so closing during load leaves a feature closed once its import resolves rather than popping open anyway) - the same two-step shape every prior package in this roadmap has used: a mechanism lands before its tenants (P7's pattern grid before its screen, P9's providers and `fileBrowser` before the disk-operation screen). Verified against hand-built mock features with a controllable, manually-resolved load promise - no sample editor, no Nibbles, ported here.

A second real finding shaped what this spec deliberately does *not* do: P10's design sketch says v2 should give plugins "one documented host interface" instead of v1's differently-shaped ones, but reading all three of `app.js`'s own dependency bags in full found each genuinely different (SampleEditor's own `core`/font/Tracker/Assets bag; Nibbles' own bag of raw v1 UI element constructors, since it builds its UI directly; Generator's simpler third shape) - and none of the three resembles anything v2 already has (no `EventBus`/`EVENT`/`COMMAND` constants, no `Y`/yascal sprite system, no raw element constructors exist in this codebase at all). Inventing a consolidated interface now would be guessing at what a real tenant needs before one exists; this spec's own registry accepts any manifest shape and hands back whatever a feature's `load()` resolves to, leaving the actual interface design to whichever spec ports the first real tenant.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 515 tests, all passing, up from 507 (8 new in `tests/features.test.js`, covering every acceptance criterion plus an unregistered name, closing an already-ready feature, an idempotent reopen, and a bare no-`default` module). `node build.mjs app`/`dist` confirmed unaffected (loose ends unchanged at 78) - this spec touches no YAML, no compiler, no `capabilities.js` mapping at all.

Not done and recorded as such: the sample editor and Nibbles (P10's own two named tenants, ~4,300 and ~1,300+ lines of v1 code respectively - each a separate, later spec); the generator (excluded by the roadmap's own resolved open decision 3); any screen or command wiring to open a feature (`showSampleEditor`/`nibbles` stay unresolved in `app/LOOSE-ENDS.md`); the real host interface a feature module receives. No Git commit was created.

**Package P10 is open, with its own mechanism now closed - the same shape P9's `fileBrowser` and P7's pattern grid already set before their own screens landed.**

Scoped this spec to `sampleProcessing.js` alone - the smallest, most self-contained, most valuable real slice, matching every other package's own "capability before screen" precedent throughout this roadmap.

Independently confirmed a real, load-bearing finding before writing any code: `domain/song/editHistory.js` (spec 028) already had an `EDITACTION.SAMPLE` constant and an explicit `// DATA/SAMPLE: declared, not implemented here - P10's own territory` comment - planted on purpose, two specs before P10 was ever allocated, for exactly this moment. `domain/song/index.js`'s own `history = createEditHistory({ applyPatternDiff })` construction had no sample hook wired in yet, and its exported surface had no sample-data read/write or edit-registration entry point either - confirming the seam was real, not just a comment. This spec fills it in: `domain/song/index.js` gained `getCurrentInstrument()`, `registerSampleEdit()`, and an internal `applySampleDiff` wired into `editHistory.js`'s now-implemented `SAMPLE` branch, unifying sample-edit undo into the exact same shared `Ctrl+Z` stack pattern-edit undo already uses - a deliberate improvement over v1's own two separate undo domains (v1's own sample-undo replay, driven by an `EVENT.commandProcessSample` listener, silently does nothing unless the sample editor's own view happens to be visible at the moment of undo - a real limitation, not a design worth reproducing).

`domain/sampleEditor/processing.js` ports the four operations as pure transforms (`normalize`, `fadeIn`, `fadeOut`, `adjustVolumePercent` with its own soft-knee compressor above 0.9 peak, `reverseRange`, `invertRange`, `trimBounds`); `domain/sampleEditor/index.js` is the capability - a `{start, length}` selection defaulting to "the whole sample," and the four operations, each registering one undo entry through `domain/song`'s new surface. It never imports `domain/playback/engine/` directly - reaching the current instrument only through `domain/song/index.js`'s own public surface, an ordinary inter-module dependency, not a new documented exception (unlike `domain/song/`'s and `domain/moduleExport/`'s own, which do reach the engine directly for a real, load-bearing reason each).

Found and fixed two real, small bugs in v1's own algorithms, rather than porting them faithfully: `adjustVolume("max")` on an entirely silent (all-zero) sample divides by zero (`scale = Infinity`), corrupting every sample to `NaN`; `adjustVolume("fadein"/"fadeout")` on a single-sample selection divides `i/last` with `last = 0`, the same failure. Neither is a deliberate v1 behavior worth preserving (unlike, say, `domain/playback/README.md`'s own audible 8-bit sample clipping, kept because it is audible) - both guarded here, the same kind of call spec 034 made fixing `hippo.js`'s own crash. A third, more subtle correctness point was worked out by hand before writing any test: `applySampleDiff` has to use `action.data`/`.dataTo`'s own array lengths, not `action.to` (the selection length *at edit time*, which `trim()`'s own length-changing edit makes stale) - getting this wrong would have silently corrupted data on undo/redo specifically for `trim()`, while every same-length operation would have looked fine regardless.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 543 tests, all passing, up from 515 (16 new in `tests/sampleProcessing.test.js`, pure array math against known expected output; 11 new in `tests/sampleEditor.test.js`, against a real loaded fixture - every operation's own undo/redo, a selected-range-only edit leaving the rest of the sample untouched, `trim()`'s own length-changing round trip including the sample's own `length` field, and a cross-instrument undo test confirming the engine switches back to the instrument an edit belongs to before restoring it; 1 more case added to `tests/song.test.js`, since the prior "SAMPLE is a declared no-op" test's own description was no longer accurate once this spec implemented it for real). `node build.mjs app`/`dist` confirmed unaffected (loose ends unchanged at 78) - this spec touches no YAML, no compiler, no `capabilities.js` mapping, matching spec 039's own shape exactly.

Not done and recorded as such: the waveform view, the rest of the sample-editor screen, `loopCreator.js`/`resamplePanel.js`/`hissReductionPanel.js`, `resample`/`resampleToRate` (`waveform.js`'s own, not `sampleProcessing.js`'s), 8-bit/16-bit conversion (`sampleView.js`'s own `changeSampleBit`, notably not undoable in v1 either - recorded, not fixed), and where the eventual screen mounts (a real, undecided architectural question intersecting with `top-view.value`'s existing screen-switching). No Git commit was created.

**Package P10's first named tenant is started - its core DSP operations exist and are undoable, unified with the rest of the application's own undo stack. The waveform view and the rest of the screen remain, each substantial enough to be its own later spec.**

## 2026-09-13 — Spec 041 implemented: the waveform view, the port's third specialized-component leaf

User direction: continue with the waveform view next. Re-read `script/plugins/sampleeditor/waveform.js` (1540 lines) closely, beyond spec 040's own high-level pass, focusing on `render`, `zoom`/`getZoomState`/`setZoomState`, and the range-only paths of `onDragStart`/`onDrag`/`onTouchUp` - deliberately leaving loop-marker dragging, the range-volume fade gesture, draw-mode freehand editing, individual-sample dragging, playback preview, the slice overlay, and the pinch-zoom scrollbar out of scope, recorded rather than silently dropped (see requirements.md's Scope boundaries).

Traced which of the two established specialized-leaf contracts this fits before designing, the same way spec 035 traced why `fileBrowser` could not reuse `listbox`'s own plumbing: a waveform view is tied to exactly one thing - the current instrument's sample - the same way `patternGrid` is tied to exactly one thing (the current pattern), not a generic, data-source-agnostic container the way `fileBrowser` deliberately is. So `runtime/waveformController.js` mirrors `gridController.js`'s own contract exactly: no bound `field`, reads/writes `domain/song` (the current instrument) and `domain/sampleEditor` (the selection, spec 040) directly as a side effect of `handle()`, always returns `changed: false, value: null, scrollTo: null` since nothing here goes through the generic `applyValue`/`applyScroll` write-back. The behavior is registered as `"waveform"`, deliberately not `"waveformView"` - the same type-name/behavior-name collision `tests/componentTypes.test.js`'s scanning test would otherwise flag, learned during spec 035's own `fileBrowser`/`rowList` naming.

`renderers/canvas/waveformGeometry.js` is the new pure layer underneath both the controller and the draw kind: `clampZoom` (a `MIN_ZOOM_LENGTH` floor so zooming in can never collapse the view to nothing, a ceiling at the sample's own length, and a start clamp keeping the window inside `[0, sampleLength - zoomLength]`), `pixelToSampleIndex`/`sampleIndexToPixel` (exact inverses of each other, so hit-testing and drawing can never disagree about where an edge is), and `edgeAt` (the same 5px hit-tolerance `waveform.js` itself uses for its own marker dragging). The controller drives zoom with the mouse wheel (plain wheel pans, ctrl+wheel zooms, both through the same `clampZoom`) and range selection with click-drag - a plain drag creates a new, direction-normalized selection; dragging within 5px of an existing selection's own start or end edge resizes that edge instead, clamped so it can never cross the other edge (at least 2 samples of range always remain, mirroring `waveform.js`'s own minimum-range guard). `renderers/canvas/drawKinds.js` gained the matching `waveformView` draw kind: background fill, three guide lines (center + two quarter-amplitude lines), a selection tint drawn *underneath* the waveform line (a v2-appropriate simplification, not a v1 behavior to match exactly - v1 draws separate marker lines, not a filled tint, the same convention `listbox`/`fileBrowser` already use for their own selection highlight), then one sample per pixel column with no min/max envelope downsampling - a faithfully-ported v1 limitation (`waveform.js:840-915`), not fixed here since nothing in this spec's scope calls for it.

Registration was purely additive, the same shape every specialized leaf before it has taken: `runtime/behaviors.js` (`waveform` -> `createWaveformController`), `runtime/componentTypes.js` (`waveformView`, a leaf with `container: null`, no authored `properties`), `renderers/canvas/mountGeneric.js` (one new `contextFor()` branch resolving only the `ctrlKey` modifier - no `visualStateOf` branch at all, since the draw kind reads `domain/song`/`domain/sampleEditor` directly, exactly as `patternGrid`'s own draw kind does).

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 565 tests, all passing, up from 543 (7 new in `tests/waveformGeometry.test.js`, pure geometry against known values; 9 new in `tests/waveformController.test.js`, against the real `wizballalt.mod` fixture, mirroring `gridController.test.js`'s own convention - new-selection creation, both-edge resizing without crossing, wheel pan/zoom clamping at both ends, and disabled blocking every gesture; 6 new in `tests/waveformView.test.js`, a `patternGrid.test.js`-style fake-canvas test covering the registry declaration, `measure()`'s floor size, draw-never-throws for no instrument/a real sample/an out-of-window selection, and the selection tint appearing exactly once when a selection exists). `node build.mjs app` confirmed unaffected - loose ends unchanged at 78, since this spec touches no YAML and no compiler. `node build.mjs dist` still blocked only by the same pre-existing `esbuild` version mismatch every spec since 019 has recorded.

One design-sketch-vs-implementation discrepancy worth recording: design.md's own `pointerdown` sketch computes `blocked` with a ternary folded into one combined guard clause, which is `null` whenever `state.disabled` is false regardless of the other conditions - functionally fine, but the version actually written separates the "disabled" and "nothing to hit" guards into two clauses for clarity, with identical behavior. Separately, `wheel` deliberately never reports `blocked: "disabled"` the way `pointerdown` does - matching both design.md's own sketch and `gridController.js`'s own `handleWheel` (which does not check `disabled` at all) - so "disabled blocks every gesture" is verified via `handled: false` and a confirmed no-op on state, not a `blocked` reason for every gesture kind.

Not done and recorded as such: loop-marker dragging, the range-volume fade gesture, draw-mode freehand editing, individual-sample dragging, playback preview, the slice overlay, the pinch-zoom scrollbar gesture, and no screen authored yet (the waveform view has no component tree location to mount at until the rest of the sample-editor screen is authored). No browser verification occurred in this environment. No Git commit was created.

**Package P10 now has both of its DSP/view mechanisms in place - sample processing (spec 040) and the waveform view (this spec) - each undoable/interactive on its own, real domain modules underneath, with no screen yet to host either. The remaining work is the sample-editor screen itself (menu, sliders, transport, envelope panels) and the three still-unstarted sub-features (`loopCreator.js`, `resamplePanel.js`, `hissReductionPanel.js`).**

## 2026-09-14 — Spec 042 implemented: the sample-editor screen

User direction: continue with the sample-editor screen next. Re-read `script/plugins/sampleeditor/sampleView.js` in full (1070 lines, beyond spec 040's own high-level pass over the whole plugin) before scoping anything - it is one screen with far more surface than P10's remaining mechanisms cover: a loop creator, a hiss-reduction panel, a resample panel, two envelope panels, vibrato controls, an 8/16-bit toggle, playback-preview transport buttons, and a menu mixing disk load/save with the four operations spec 040 already ported. Scoped this spec to exactly what was real and already backed by an existing capability: the waveform view (spec 041) and the four sample-processing operations (spec 040), mounted for the first time, plus the instrument-selection/name/volume/loop fields those two mechanisms implicitly depend on - nothing in v2 had ever represented "which instrument is current" before this spec.

This is the first spec to author a slider/spinBox/inputbox's own `field:` property in real YAML at all - the mechanism has existed since spec 018 but nothing had exercised it. It surfaced a real, easily-made mistake the moment it was tried: the authored action name `setVolume` derives (via `actionNameAsProse` + the `set-value` recipe) to command `volume.set`/field `volume.value` - a single-word subject - not `sample-volume.*` as first assumed by analogy with the other five fields. Caught immediately by reading `app-outline.json` back after compiling, the same empirical-verification habit spec 036 established for exactly this kind of derived-name mismatch; fixed by renaming the authored action to `setSampleVolume` rather than bending the domain module's own field name to match an accident.

Two small, additive-only extensions outside the screen's own YAML: `domain/song/index.js` gained `getMaxInstruments`/`setCurrentInstrumentIndex` (thin re-exports of methods the ported engine already has, the same footing `getCurrentInstrumentIndex`/`getCurrentInstrument` already stand on); `runtime/waveformController.js`'s `ensureZoom` now resets the zoom window to "the whole sample" on every instrument switch, not only the very first call - matching `waveform.js#setInstrument`'s own reset (593-620), unexercised until this spec gave the controller its first real tenant that actually switches instruments.

The loop-enabled checkbox needed one genuinely new piece of compiler vocabulary: `compiler/recipes.js`'s `CONDITION_FIELD_ALIASES` list (designed in spec 014, populated only for `play`/`record` since) gained a `"loop enabled"` entry, because a rule's own `deriveMockFieldName` (dot-joining a multi-word subject) and `toggle-generic`'s own derived field (hyphen-joining, with a `.active` suffix) can only ever agree by coincidence for a multi-word subject - without the alias, the checkbox would silently never show as checked. `verb-action` gained six verbs (`normalize`/`trim`/`reverse`/`invert`, plus the first two-word verbs it has ever matched, `"fade in"`/`"fade out"`) to resolve the six operation buttons - each just a fire-and-forget wrapper around an operation spec 040 already built.

Two authoring mistakes were caught by the compiler's own warnings before any test ran, not silently: `buttonGroup`'s container shape is `items:`, not `content:` (an `unknown-component-property` warning named exactly which properties are accepted); two unnamed sibling `spinBox` nodes collided on identity (an `ambiguous-duplicate-name` warning), fixed by naming both explicitly.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 575 tests, all passing, up from 567 (6 new in `tests/recipes.test.js` for the compiler vocabulary additions; 8 new in `tests/sampleEditor.test.js`, against the real `wizballalt.mod` fixture, covering every new capability function including the instrument-index clamp/desync-prevention case and the real-sample-length loop clamp). `node compiler/generate.js`: 26 warnings, 0 net new (the two authoring mistakes above were fixed before this count). `node build.mjs app`: loose ends unchanged at 78 - all twelve new commands are mapped in `capabilities.js`, none left as a generated mock; `app/ui/sampleEditPanel.js`/`app/actions/sample*.js` read by hand to confirm each imports the real domain module, not a mock. `node build.mjs dist` still blocked only by the same pre-existing `esbuild` version mismatch every spec since 019 has recorded.

Not done and recorded as such: every FT-only field (finetune, panning, fadeout, relative note, vibrato) - blocked on two still-missing mechanisms, not merely skipped: `disabledWhen` has no YAML authoring vocabulary yet (spec 015's own recorded gap), and nothing lets an authored control's `min`/`max` vary by tracker mode the way v1's own dynamic `setMin`/`setMax` calls do. Also not done: envelope editing, the loop creator/hiss-reduction/resample panels, draw mode, 8/16-bit conversion, playback preview, sample load/save, maximize/slices, the percentage volume buttons (each needs a parameter no zero-argument command shape can carry), the Select button group (its "None" has no distinct representation from "the whole sample" in the current selection model), and instrument-property undo (v1's own mechanism replays through a live UI control object, a shape nothing in v2 has ever registered against). The screen's own fields also display blank/zero values until the instrument-index control is first touched - there is no proactive "publish on song load" hook, since adding one would mean `domain/playback` importing `domain/sampleEditor`, inverting this port's own established module layering. No browser verification occurred in this environment. No Git commit was created.

**P10's own two mechanisms (spec 040's DSP operations, spec 041's waveform view) are both reachable from a real screen for the first time. What remains of the sample editor is the FT-only side panel (blocked on two recorded mechanism gaps), envelope editing, and three separable sub-features (loop creator, hiss reduction, resample) - each substantial enough to be its own later spec, the same way this one was.**

## 2026-09-14 — Spec 043 implemented: the loop creator

User direction: continue with the loop creator next - the first of P10's three separable sub-features spec 040 recorded. Read `script/plugins/sampleeditor/loopCreator.js` in full (848 lines) before scoping anything. It is a self-contained crossfade loop-creation editor: three independently-sized regions blended across a chosen loop's seam (tail audio into the loop start, head audio into the loop end, the loop's own trailing audio into the pre-loop head), each with its own two blend-volume controls, plus three presets and snap-to-zero. Scoped this spec to the state model and DSP alone - `computeLayers`/`applyPreview`/`recomputeMaxes`/`nearestZeroCrossing`/the preset table/open/save/cancel - deferring the interactive drag-based waveform overlay this needs (its own eight independent drag targets: two loop markers, three blend-size handles, three blend-volume lines, three "original volume" lines) to a later spec, matching the exact "mechanism before its interactive screen" shape spec 040/041/042 already established.

Found and fixed a genuine, confirmed bug in v1's own undo path, not merely ported around: `saveLoop()` sets `editAction.loopStart`/`.loopLength` expecting undo/redo to restore the loop bounds, but the replay handler for a `REPLACE`-kind sample action (`waveform.js`'s own `EVENT.commandProcessSample` listener, read in full to confirm this, not assumed) only ever reads those two fields for its `CUT`/`PASTE` branches - never `REPLACE`, the one kind "Create Loop" itself uses. Undoing "Create Loop" in v1 reverts the sample data but leaves the loop bounds exactly where the session left them, out of sync with the reverted audio underneath. Fixed by extending `domain/song/index.js#applySampleDiff` (spec 040) with one small, optional, fully backward-compatible field - a registered SAMPLE action may now carry `loop: {from, to}`, restored alongside the data when present; every existing caller (spec 040's own four operations) omits it and is unaffected.

Also found, and deliberately not ported: `snapMarkersToZero()` (`loopCreator.js:590-601`) is fully implemented but genuinely dead code in v1 - confirmed by grepping the whole file for its own name, it is wired to nothing (not the "Snap to zero" checkbox, not any button). A related, real asymmetry was traced and preserved rather than "fixed": v1's own spinbox-driven `setLoopStart`/`setLoopLength` never apply snap-to-zero, only a mouse-drag on a loop marker does - both call sites were read before concluding this is deliberate (a typed-in exact value should never silently snap; an imprecise drag benefits from it), so this port's own `setLoopStart`/`setLoopLength` keep the same asymmetry.

`domain/sampleEditor/loopCreator.js` (new) holds the whole subsystem's module-level state (loop bounds, three region sizes and their maxes, six blend values, a plain-array snapshot of the original data for cancel/undo, snap-to-zero) and its public surface: `openLoopCreator`/`isLoopCreatorOpen`/`getLoopCreatorState`, eleven absolute setters (the domain-level equivalent of v1's own delta-driven drag handlers - a later drag controller will compute the delta and call these, the same split `runtime/waveformController.js`/`domain/sampleEditor#setSampleSelection` already established), `applyLoopPreset`, `saveLoopCreator`, `cancelLoopCreator`. Kept as its own internal module within the `domain/sampleEditor/` folder rather than folded into `index.js` - mirroring `domain/moduleExport/{protracker,fasttracker}.js`'s own precedent of a directly-testable sibling module, imported by tests now and by a real screen's own controller later, never re-exported through `index.js` under a redundant second name.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 596 tests, all passing, up from 575 (19 new in `tests/loopCreator.test.js` - most against small, hand-computable synthetic instrument objects rather than a real fixture, since exact numbers matter for the DSP/preset assertions in a way a real MOD sample's own arbitrary data cannot give; a hand-traced `computeLayers`/`applyPreview` case asserts the exact blended value at each of three affected sample indices, plus an idempotence check; `saveLoopCreator`'s own undo/redo round trip runs against the real engine, since it registers against `domain/song`'s shared history. 2 new in `tests/song.test.js`, exercising the `action.loop` mechanism directly, with and without the field present). `node build.mjs app`/`dist` confirmed unaffected (loose ends unchanged at 78) - this spec touches no YAML, no compiler, no `capabilities.js` mapping, matching spec 039/040's own precedent exactly.

Not done and recorded as such: the interactive drag-based waveform overlay (a fourth specialized-component leaf, at least as complex as the waveform view was), the menu-bar controls (title, Apply/Cancel, snap checkbox, presets dropdown) and the screen wiring into spec 042's own panel, `snapMarkersToZero` (confirmed dead code, not ported), zoom sync with the waveform view, and reactive instrument-change handling (nothing reaches this module from any UI yet to react to). No browser verification occurred in this environment. No Git commit was created.

**P10 now has three of its four sample-editor mechanisms in place (DSP operations, the waveform view, the loop creator's own state/DSP), each headless and fully tested, plus the real screen hosting the first two. The loop creator's own interactive overlay, the hiss-reduction and resample panels, and the FT-only side panel remain - each substantial enough to be its own later spec.**

## 2026-09-14 — Spec 044 implemented: the interactive loop-creator overlay

User direction: continue with the interactive loop-creator overlay next - giving spec 043's own headless state model and DSP their front end, the port's fourth specialized-component leaf after `patternGrid`, `fileBrowser` and `waveformView`. Re-read `loopCreator.js`'s own `waveDisplay` (119-274, 379-537) closely, beyond spec 043's own high-level pass - eleven independent drag targets (two loop markers, three blend-size handles, three blend-volume lines, three "original volume" lines), each with its own hit-testing priority and drawing.

Scoped this spec to the mechanism alone - the controller and draw kind - deferring the menu-bar controls (title, Apply/Cancel, snap checkbox, presets dropdown) and the screen wiring into spec 042's own panel to a later spec, the exact same split spec 041 established for the plain waveform view (mechanism, then spec 042's own screen). Traced the contract this leaf should follow before building: like `waveformView`, it reads/writes its own domain module directly (`domain/sampleEditor/loopCreator.js`), owns its own independent zoom window rather than syncing with the plain waveform view's (a coupling between two specialized leaves this port has never needed anywhere else), and reuses `renderers/canvas/waveformGeometry.js`'s own pixel/sample-index math directly rather than duplicating it - `renderers/canvas/loopCreatorGeometry.js` adds only the eleven-target hit test and the vertical blend-line-to-pixel mapping v1's own loop creator uniquely needs.

Two small, additive-only extensions to spec 043's own module gave this spec exactly the two things it had deliberately left private: `nearestZeroCrossing` (exported so a marker drag can honor `snapToZero` the same way v1's own `onDrag` does inline - spec 043's own doc comment had already flagged this as "read by a later drag controller only," and this is that controller) and `getLoopCreatorLayers` (the three computed blend arrays, needed so the draw kind can plot each one's own waveform shape in its own color, matching v1's own `drawLayer` - notably, v1's loop creator never draws the sample's *overall* waveform at all, only these three limited-range layers plus the loop markers/handles/blend lines).

Every one of the eleven drag targets calls the exact domain setter spec 043 already built and clamps against (`setLoopStart`/`setTailSize`/`setBlendTail`/etc.) - the controller itself adds no new clamping logic, only the delta math (horizontal for markers/handles, vertical and inverted for blend/orig lines) translating a drag into the candidate value each setter already knows how to bound.

Two real mistakes were caught and fixed before any test ran, neither by a failing assertion: first, `drawLoopBlendLine`'s own first draft always drew a filled triangle's wide end toward `sampleStart`, on the wrong assumption that this matched every caller - re-checking v1's own six `drawBlendLine` call sites (429-449) line by line found the wide-end side actually alternates per target, fixed by threading a real `wideAtStart` parameter through instead of hard-coding one direction. Second, an early draft of the eleven-target geometry test picked orig/blend values whose own pixel positions were only 4px apart - inside each other's own 6px hit tolerance - so a point meant for one target was quietly also matching another (checked first in priority); fixed by spacing every orig line's own Y position 36px from every blend line's own, past the tolerance on either side, for every region.

Not done and recorded as such: the hiss-reduction and resample panels, Nibbles, and the FT-only side panel (finetune, panning, fadeout, relative note, vibrato) - each its own later spec, as spec 042 already recorded. The presets `radioGroup`'s own selected item is never synced back to reflect live state once an individual size/blend value is dragged away from an exact preset's configuration - a deliberate, recorded limit, not an oversight. No browser verification occurred in this environment. No Git commit was created.

**P10's own four sample-editor mechanisms (DSP operations, waveform view, loop creator state/DSP, loop creator overlay) are now all reachable from one real screen, each with its own menu controls where v1 has them. What remains of P10 is the hiss-reduction and resample panels, Nibbles, and the FT-only side panel - each substantial enough to be its own later spec, matching every prior estimate in this log.**

## 2026-09-14 — Spec 046 implemented: the resample panel

User direction: continue with spec 010, skipping the hiss-reduction panel for now and picking the next unallocated P10 piece - the resample panel, the second of spec 040's three named sub-features. Read `script/plugins/sampleeditor/resamplePanel.js` (116 lines) and `waveform.js#resampleToRate`/`#resample` in full before scoping. Unlike the loop creator, `resamplePanel.js` needs no specialized-component leaf - it is a small static dialog (labels, a checkbox, two buttons, one number) with no drag surfaces, so it is authored entirely from the existing catalogue.

`domain/sampleEditor/resample.js` (new sibling module, mirroring `loopCreator.js`'s own precedent) ports the DSP: a whole-sample linear-or-nearest resample to a target note's own rate, scaling the loop's own start/length by the same ratio, one undoable edit. Nothing is mutated until Apply, matching v1's own panel exactly, so - unlike the loop creator - no live-preview session or cancel-time restore is needed. Two small additive exports feed it: `domain/song/index.js#getNoteForResampleIndex` (folding `resamplePanel.js`'s own `modeNote`/`noteName`/`targetPeriod` into one pure function) and `domain/playback/index.js#getSampleRateForPeriod` (a thin forward to the engine's own pure rate math) - deliberately placed in `domain/playback`, not `domain/song`, since it is playback-engine math with no song-document concern, avoiding growing `domain/song`'s surface for something unrelated to editing. The target note is driven by an authored spinBox (1-96) rather than v1's own captured-physical-key-press gesture, and `resample("up"/"down")` (the Edit menu's separate Upsample/DownSample quick actions, a different, separately-named v1 action) stays out of scope - both recorded, deliberate boundaries.

Found and fixed a real design gap **before writing any YAML**, by re-reading `runtime/genericLayout.js#isVisible` rather than assuming spec 045's own `loop-creator.mode` shape would simply extend to a second field: `visibleWhen` reads exactly one field per node, so two independent panel-switch fields (this spec's own planned `resample.mode` alongside spec 045's `loop-creator.mode`) could never mutually exclude a third sub-view - both `sampleEditNormalView` and the resample panel would have rendered simultaneously whenever `resample.mode` was `"open"`. Fixed by consolidating both into one shared `sample-edit-view.value` (`""`/`"loop-creator"`/`"resample"`) read by all three sub-views - a rename of spec 045's own delivered field, applied to `domain/sampleEditor/index.js`'s `openLoopCreator`/`saveLoopCreator`/`cancelLoopCreator` and their own six pre-existing tests, not a bolt-on alongside it.

Picking up the implementation then found and fixed two more real regressions before any new test ran, neither in v1's own code: two `ambiguous-duplicate-name` collisions (`tests/compiler.test.js`'s own fixed-count assertion caught them) from unnamed field-bound `label` nodes falling back to the bare type `"label"` as their identity, colliding with siblings in the same row - fixed by naming each of the five explicitly, the same class of mistake spec 042's own unnamed-spinBox collision already illustrated. And a domain-boundary violation in this spec's own first test draft: `tests/sampleEditor.test.js` had imported `TRACKERMODE` directly from `domain/playback/engine/enum.js` to force FastTracker mode for a test case - `tests/domainBoundary.test.js` mechanically forbids exactly this, even for test files. Fixed by loading the real `sin.xm` fixture instead (confirmed by grep first that `tests/moduleExport.test.js`/`tests/playback.test.js` already use this exact convention for FT-mode tests, rather than assuming a fix), which sets FastTracker mode and `useLinearFrequency` through the real XM loader; the non-linear-period branch is reached afterward by toggling `Tracker.useLinearFrequency` directly, a plain property write needing no import. A genuine measurement mistake in this spec's own ad hoc verification (not the shipped code) came from the same root cause in miniature: an early manual check of the non-linear-FT-mode branch was run without ever loading a module, so the engine's own unconditional construction-time default for `useLinearFrequency` (`true`) silently made the "linear" and "non-linear" checks identical - caught when the real test's loaded-fixture assertion produced a different, genuine value (856, not the wrongly hand-assumed 5376).

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 648 tests, all passing, up from 628 at this session's start (9 of that gain filled in spec 045's own missing coverage discovered earlier this session; this spec added 2 in `tests/recipes.test.js` and 9 in `tests/sampleEditor.test.js`, covering `getNoteForResampleIndex` across ProTracker/FastTracker/linear-frequency modes and all five new capability wrappers against real fixtures). `node compiler/generate.js`: 25 warnings, 0 errors, none new. Read back `app-outline.json`: all five bindings' `command`/`mockField` strings match design.md's table exactly. `node build.mjs app`: loose ends unchanged at 82 (spec 045's own count - no new `radioGroup`, so no new `inert-control` entries this time); none of the five new commands are mocked. `node build.mjs dist` still blocked only by the same pre-existing `esbuild` version mismatch every spec since 019 has recorded.

Not done and recorded as such: the hiss-reduction panel (deferred per user direction), Nibbles, `resample("up"/"down")`, the FT-only side panel, and keyboard-driven target-note entry (a spinBox is used instead). No browser verification occurred in this environment. No Git commit was created.

**P10 now has two of its three sample-editor sub-features (loop creator, resample) reachable from the real screen, alongside the DSP operations and waveform view from specs 040/041. The hiss-reduction panel, Nibbles, and the FT-only side panel remain - each substantial enough to be its own later spec.**

## 2026-09-14 — Spec 047 implemented: Nibbles, the game engine and grid

User direction: continue with Nibbles next, P10's second named tenant. Read `script/plugins/games/nibbles/nibbles.js` (1315 lines) in full - a complete, self-contained arcade game built directly against v1's raw UI element factories with its own hand-rolled per-breakpoint layout, sharing nothing with the tracker's own song/pattern data. Scoped this spec mechanism-first, the same split every prior specialized surface used: the headless engine and the specialized leaf, deferring the screen (config panel, score/lives display, buttons, the high-score table's own visual list, the menu action and feature-registry wiring), dynamic grid sizing, the high-score dialog and cheat codes to a later spec.

The single largest deliberate departure from v1: the grid is fixed at 40x15 cells (the level layouts' own native size), not derived from the browser window's own pixel dimensions the way v1's `game.resize()` computes it. This removes an entire axis of complexity (margin math, a resize-triggered map rebuild) for a real but secondary piece of fidelity, the same class of call open decision 1 already made for skin completeness.

`tools/nibblesLevels.mjs` (new, reusing `tools/skinAtlas.mjs`'s own dependency-free PNG decoder from spec 008) decodes the real 206x97 indexed level-sprite PNG once, offline, into `domain/nibbles/levels.js` - a plain, checked-in ES data module of 30 levels (a start point, a small per-level color palette, and a sparse wall-cell list), replacing v1's own per-level runtime canvas decode entirely. Verified against three independently hand-checked levels (1: empty; 3: two vertical bars; 30: a dense multi-color maze) before being trusted for anything else - decoding pixel data correctly the first time, on the first real test.

`domain/nibbles/index.js` is the port's first domain folder with no dependency on the tracker's own document model at all - grid/snake/collision/scoring/level progression/lives, ported from `Game()`'s own `update`/`won`/`lost`/`onClick`. Unlike v1's own single array conflating walls (10+, indexed into a session-wide accumulating color table), food (1-9) and the snake's own body (1000+, doubling as age), this keeps each level's own wall layout as static data (read directly, never copied into a mutable structure) and only the food/snake state as dynamic. `domain/nibbles/highscores.js` mirrors `domain/settings/favorites.js`'s exact shape and keeps v1's own storage key verbatim. `nibblesGrid` (`runtime/nibblesController.js`, `renderers/canvas/drawKinds.js`'s new entry, `runtime/componentTypes.js`'s new type) is the port's fifth specialized-component leaf, after `patternGrid`/`fileBrowser`/`waveformView`/`loopCreatorView`.

The one new renderer mechanism: `renderers/canvas/mountGeneric.js` gained a second, independent "tick" clock (a `ticks: true` registry flag, calling a mounted node's controller's own `onFrame(deltaMs)` every animation frame) so the game keeps advancing without further input. Re-reading the existing sprite-animation clock (spec 017) before writing anything found it the wrong shape - it advances a *bound field's own frame count*, not a whole simulation step, and nibblesGrid has no field to bind at all - so a second clock was added alongside it rather than force-fitting the sprite clock, mirroring spec 032's own explicit choice to keep its own coalesced-draw loop separate from the sprite clock for the identical reason ("the same start/stop shape ... applied here to everything else instead of unifying the two").

Found and fixed a real, confirmed v1 bug: `checkHighScore`'s own `index>0` guard silently refuses to ever record a genuine first-place high score, since `findIndex` returning `0` (beating the very best entry) fails that check; `domain/nibbles/highscores.js#qualifies` uses `index !== -1` instead, with a regression test naming the bug.

The hardest part of this spec turned out to be its own test suite, not the domain code. Proving a full, real 9-catch win needs the snake to reach ~109 segments (`snakeLength()`'s own formula) on a 600-cell grid by the last catch, and two successive heuristic walkers built to prove it - first "shortest path to the food," then "shortest path, but only through cells with enough open space around them" - each occasionally let the snake trap itself, failing roughly 1 run in 15 and then 1 in 40 respectively, discovered only by deliberately stress-running the test file dozens of times in a row rather than trusting a single green run. Rather than keep tuning an unprovable heuristic, the walker was rewritten to follow a hand-verified Hamiltonian cycle over all 40x15 cells (a fixed route visiting every cell exactly once before repeating - real Snake-solving bots use the identical technique), which makes self-collision structurally impossible by construction, regardless of where food lands, with no heuristic and no safety margin needed at all. Fixing that surfaced a second, deeper bug in the same test helper, hiding underneath the first: "was the food eaten" had been detected by checking whether `score` changed - but v1's own `update()` decrements score by 1 on *every* tick regardless of eating, so the very first tick after starting to hunt for food always changed the score, and the helper had been falsely declaring victory after a single step the entire time, meaning 7 of the win test's intended 8 ordinary catches had never actually happened at all, silently, since the test's own per-catch assertion only checked that the game was still running (true either way). Fixed by detecting the food cell's own position changing instead. Stress-tested 100 consecutive runs of the affected file and 15 of the full suite after both fixes landed together, with zero failures either way.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 690 tests, all passing, up from 648 (6 in `tests/nibblesLevels.test.js`, 15 in `tests/nibbles.test.js`, 6 in `tests/nibblesHighscores.test.js`, 9 in `tests/nibblesController.test.js`, 6 in `tests/nibblesGridView.test.js`). `node compiler/generate.js`/`node build.mjs app`: confirmed unaffected - this spec authors no YAML, adds no recipe, and maps no command in `capabilities.js`; loose ends unchanged at 82, the same "mechanism with no tenant screen yet" footing specs 039/040/043 already established.

Not done and recorded as such: the screen, dynamic window-size-derived grid dimensions (fixed 40x15 instead), the high-score name-entry dialog and its table's own visual rendering, the "SKIP"/"TRITON" cheat codes, and the decorative player-1/logo sprites. No browser verification occurred in this environment. No Git commit was created.

**P10's second named tenant now has a real, independently-domained engine and its own specialized-component leaf, the same "mechanism before screen" shape every other specialized surface in this port has used. What remains of Nibbles is entirely its screen and secondary UI (the high-score table, cheat codes) - a later spec's own; the hiss-reduction panel and the FT-only side panel are the sample editor's own remaining pieces.**

## 2026-09-14 — Spec 048 implemented: Nibbles screen prerequisites

Continuing directly from spec 047, researching where `nibblesGrid` should actually mount surfaced a real gap before any YAML was written: re-reading `script/src/ui/app/appMainPanel.js`'s own `_hideMain()`/`_showMain()` bodies in full (not just the `pluginRenderHook` handler that calls them) showed Nibbles takes over v1's *entire* `AppMainPanel` - the instrument list, the song list, both pattern panels, the title/BPM/repeat controls - not the smaller region `top-view.value` (spec 025/026) already swaps between Options/File Operations/Sample Edit. `top-view.value` gates only three of the nine regions under `main`; `editPad`/`songPatternList`/`songProperties` are always-visible siblings it never touches at all.

Bolting a second condition onto those six already-gated nodes was not an option - `visibleWhen` reads exactly one field per node, the identical limitation spec 046 already found and fixed for the sample-editor panel's own third sub-view. Following that same precedent, a new field, `main-view.value`, wraps all six regions (`fileOperationsPanel`/`optionsPanel`/`sampleEditPanel`/`editPad`/`songPatternList`/`songProperties`) in a new `normalMainView` panel, unchanged inside it, alongside a new `nibblesPanel` sibling - a placeholder only, since the real game screen is spec 049's own, the same "mechanism, then screen" split every prior specialized surface in this port has used (specs 023/024, 025/026, 041/042, 043-045).

`compiler/recipes.js` gained one more `verb-action` verb, `show`, resolving the View menu's own already-authored `showMain` for the first time - it had never resolved at all before this spec, confirmed by running `interpretAction` directly rather than assumed. As a recorded side effect, three more already-authored, still-unmapped View-menu siblings (`showOptions`/`showFileOperations`/`showSampleEditor`) now resolve too, each simply moving from an `unresolved-action-reference` loose end to a `mocked-command` one - not this spec's job to map, and not silently hidden either. `domain/nibbles/index.js` gained two thin view-switch capabilities (`openNibblesView`, `showMain`), kept there rather than in a new "app view routing" module, since nothing else needs a more general one yet.

Verified with `computeGenericLayout()` directly against the real, live `app.yaml` (no Playwright in this environment, the same reason every spec since 019 has recorded) - and the test itself caught two of its own mistakes before either result could be trusted. First: mocking an unset field as JS's own `undefined`, rather than the real running application's own seeded initial value `""`, silently hid every one of the six wrapped regions, since `[""].includes(undefined)` is `false` - an early draft's test failure pointed straight at the mock, not the mechanism. Second: an early draft assumed all six regions would appear together whenever `main-view.value` is `""`, forgetting that three of them (`fileOperationsPanel`/`optionsPanel`/`sampleEditPanel`) keep their own, older `top-view.value` condition, entirely untouched by this spec - fixed by asserting only what each region's own pre-existing condition actually allows, plus one more test proving the two mechanisms genuinely compose (an ancestor's `visibleWhen` and a descendant's own must both hold).

Running the full suite next - not just the one new file - found five more pre-existing tests broken by the real tree-shape change, each assuming `main.optionsPanel`/`main.songProperties`/etc. were still direct children of `main`: two in `tests/compiler.test.js` (plus one message-content check that assumed `"showAbout"` stayed unresolved, which the new `show` verb now resolves), two in `tests/emit.test.js`, one in `tests/yaml.test.js` (a hardcoded top-level item count and its per-index scalar list). All five were traced individually and updated to the real, correct shape; none hid an actual regression once checked. A genuinely expected, measured (not assumed) side effect of the wrapping itself: nesting the six regions one level deeper costs exactly one more panel's own padding on any `width: fill` control among them (`editPad`'s own resolved width drops from what a naive "should stay pixel-identical" guess predicted to the real, smaller, correct value) - not a bug, and not unique to this spec, since spec 045's own `sampleEditNormalView` wrapper already did the identical thing to its own contents without comment.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 700 tests, all passing, up from 690 (2 in `tests/recipes.test.js`, 2 in `tests/nibbles.test.js`, 6 in the new `tests/nibblesMainView.test.js`, net of the five pre-existing fixes above). `node compiler/generate.js`: 16 warnings, down from 25 (nine `unresolved-action-reference` entries resolved via the new `show` verb and `openNibbles`'s own `open`). `node build.mjs app`: loose ends 80, down from 82 - `nibbles.open`/`main.show` both properly mapped and absent from the loose-ends list entirely; seven new `mocked-command` entries for the pre-existing `show*` siblings, a recorded byproduct.

Not done and recorded as such: the real screen content behind `nibblesPanel` (spec 049's own), the feature registry / lazy loading (a real, already-existing, port-wide limitation - `nibblesGrid`'s own controller and draw kind already import `domain/nibbles/index.js` at module scope, matching every prior specialized leaf's own precedent, so Nibbles' engine code is already part of the main bundle regardless of any registry wiring; fixing that is a separate, larger renderer-architecture concern neither this spec nor the next one addresses), and `showOptions`/`showFileOperations`/`showSampleEditor` (left exactly as unmapped as before, just recategorized). No browser verification occurred in this environment. No Git commit was created.

What remains is entirely the screen itself: score/lives display, the difficulty/wrap/grid config controls, Start/Exit/HighScore buttons, the info overlay, the high-score table's own visual list, and the still-unaddressed feature-registry gap - each a later spec's own call to make.**

User direction: continue with the Nibbles screen next. The one genuinely new authored vocabulary this screen needed was a single verb, `start` (`startNibbles` -> `nibbles.start`) - confirmed empirically first, as always, that nothing existing matched; every other action resolved through recipes this port already has (`toggle-generic` for Wrap/Grid, `set-value` for the difficulty radioGroup). Two more `CONDITION_FIELD_ALIASES` entries (`wrap`, `grid`) joined the list - the third and fourth toggle controls in this port needing the identical seam specs 042/045/046 already established, each confirmed unresolved before being added rather than assumed from precedent alone.

The one real new mechanism: score, lives and the info panel's own text need to stay live while the game plays, but no existing specialized leaf's own controller has ever called `app.state.set()` directly, and `nibblesGrid`'s own `onFrame` (spec 047) only drives the simulation, not any surrounding screen control. `domain/playback/index.js#startObserving` turned out to be the exact precedent needed: a self-perpetuating `requestAnimationFrame` loop, started by a capability call, publishing derived values to the state store on its own schedule. `domain/nibbles/index.js` gained the identical shape (`startObservingStatus`/`stopObservingStatus`, started by `openNibblesView` and stopped by `showMain`), publishing score/lives/level/status-text/button-label/an info-panel-visibility flag every frame.

Found a real registry gap via the compiler's own warning, not assumed: authoring `field:` on a `button` (for the primary button's own dynamic label) produced an `unknown-component-property` warning - `runtime/componentTypes.js`'s own `button` entry had simply never declared `field`, even though the renderer's generic text resolution (`mountGeneric.js#textOf`) already reads it for any type regardless of declaration; `label` was just the only type to have exercised and therefore documented it before now. Fixed by declaring it on `button` too, with the identical wording `label`'s own entry already uses - a real, accurate addition, not a workaround. Also hit the identical "checkbox"-word false positive in `tests/componentTypes.test.js` a third time (specs 042 and 045 each hit it once already) - a new `compiler/recipes.js` comment describing the Wrap/Grid toggle controls used the literal word "checkboxes," tripping the same scanning test both prior specs already tripped; fixed the same way, reworded to "toggle controls." Recorded plainly rather than glossed over: this project's own established lesson (avoid a component type's own bare name in prose, anywhere the scan walks) evidently still needs relearning per spec rather than becoming automatic.

`resetToIdle()` ports v1's own real behavior, not an assumption: `renderTarget.onHide()` tears down the whole `Game()`/`GameControls()` pair (nibbles.js:1286-1297), so the next `Nibbles.init()` always constructs a brand-new `Game()` whose own `state` starts at `GAMSESTATE.IDLE` by simple variable initialization - meaning v1 always reopens Nibbles at "Ready Player 1?" regardless of how the previous session ended, never mid-game. Since this port's own `domain/nibbles/` is a persistent module-level singleton rather than a torn-down-and-rebuilt object, `openNibblesView` now calls `resetToIdle()` explicitly to match - verified with a full-session test (play down to game-over, reopen, assert `IDLE` again), not merely by reasoning about the source.
