## 2026-09-15 — Spec 050 implemented: the FT-only side panel, closing package P10

User direction: continue with the FT-only side panel next - the sample editor's own last remaining named piece, blocked since spec 042 on two recorded "missing mechanisms." Re-reading `sampleView.js`'s own `EVENT.trackerModeChanged` handler (1004-1029) in full, rather than trusting spec 042's own one-example summary, found both mechanisms smaller than estimated. `disabledWhen` already existed as a real, generically-evaluated runtime mechanism (spec 015 INT-016) - only the *compiler* path from an authored `rule:` to it was missing, closed with one new `RULE_RECIPES` entry (`disabled-unless-active`, mirroring `highlighted-when-active` but negated: "disabled unless X is active" reads more naturally than a double-negative for this handler's own `disabled = !inFTMode()` shape) and one new `CONDITION_FIELD_ALIASES` entry (`ft mode`). "A control's min/max varies by tracker mode" turned out to apply to exactly one field - only `fineTuneSlider` actually re-bounds; the other seven disabled controls (panning, fadeout, relative note, three vibrato values) keep one fixed range regardless of mode - so the second "mechanism" needed no new authoring vocabulary at all: since ProTracker's own finetune range (`-8..7`) is a strict subset of FastTracker's (`-128..127`), the wide range is authored as the ceiling and the narrow one is enforced inside the capability handler, the identical "authored ceiling, real clamp" shape `loop-start`/`loop-length` already use (spec 042).

`domain/playback/index.js` gained `ft-mode.active` publishing, added at the same two places `song.title` already is (`loadModule`, `openFiles`) - the only two places anything in this port learns a module has loaded. Kept there rather than in `domain/sampleEditor` or `domain/song`, the same reasoning that already put `getSampleRateForPeriod` in `domain/playback` for spec 046: it is playback/format state, not song-document or sample-edit state. All eight new sample-editor capabilities (finetune/panning/fadeout/relative-note/vibrato speed-depth-sweep/vibrato wave) resolved through the existing `set-value` recipe with no new verb needed - confirmed empirically before writing any YAML, as always.

Two real, confirmed bugs were found in quick succession while verifying the *generated code*, not just the compiled outline - the same discipline that has caught a mistake in nearly every spec this session. First: after adding the `disabledWhen`-generating branch to `compiler/emit.js`, the outline showed the rule bound correctly, but the generated JavaScript for `ftOnlyRow`'s own module never carried `disabledWhen` at all. `emit.js` turned out to have two entirely separate node-construction paths - one for a module's own root, one for an inline child - and only the inline-child path had ever generated `highlightedWhen`/`disabledWhen` from a rule binding. This is the exact same class of gap spec 025 already found and fixed once, for a different pair of properties (`breakpoints`/`visibleWhen`): "two separate node-construction code paths and only one carried [them] to a *generated* application" - invisible until this spec, like that one, put the relevant property directly on a module-owning container for the first time. Fixed the same way spec 025 did: duplicated the branch into the second path rather than unifying them.

Second, found immediately after by reading `mountGeneric.js#syncDisabled` directly rather than assuming a container's own `disabled` state cascades to its children the way an unmet `visibleWhen` already removes a whole subtree: it does not. `syncDisabled` looks up each node's own controller and silently skips any node that has none - and both `panel` (`ftOnlyRow` itself) and `radioGroup` (the vibrato-waveform group) are declared non-interactive with no controller at all; only the `slider`/`spinBox`/`radioItem` leaves actually have one. Design.md's own first draft had already recorded this exact contingency before any YAML was written ("if a container's own disabled does not propagate to its children, each control gets its own identical rule instead"), so the fix was simply applying the already-planned fallback: eleven identical `rule: disabled unless ft mode is active` lines - seven sliders/spinboxes and four radio items individually - not one on the row.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 728 tests, all passing, up from 714 (3 in `tests/recipes.test.js`, 2 in `tests/emit.test.js` - one against the real generated tree, one a synthetic module-root-path test since the real YAML no longer exercises that shape directly - 1 in `tests/playback.test.js`, 8 in `tests/sampleEditor.test.js`). `node compiler/generate.js`: 16 warnings, 0 errors, unchanged. Read back `app-outline.json`: all eight bindings match design.md's table exactly, and all eleven interactive descendants of `ftOnlyRow` individually carry the `disabled-unless-active` binding. `node build.mjs app`: loose ends rose from 84 to 88, entirely the four new vibrato-waveform `radioItem`s' own `inert-control` entries - the same established category every other authored `radioGroup`'s items already carry; none of the eight new commands are mocked. `node build.mjs dist` still blocked only by the same pre-existing `esbuild` version mismatch every spec since 019 has recorded.

Not done and recorded as such: envelope editing, the Loop/Vibrato tab switch (both sections shown together instead, gated only by `disabledWhen`), instrument-property undo (unchanged from spec 042's own recorded limitation). No browser verification occurred in this environment. No Git commit was created.

**Package P10 is now closed.** The feature registry (spec 039), the sample editor (specs 040-046, 050) and Nibbles (specs 047-049) are all real and delivered - the roadmap's own two named tenants (open decision 3), each with DSP/mechanism specs followed by a real screen. The one recorded, deliberate residual gap - neither real tenant's own specialized-leaf domain code is actually lazy-loaded, since every specialized leaf's own controller and draw kind import their domain module at renderer module scope regardless of the feature registry's own existence - is a port-wide architectural limitation, not something P10's own acceptance criteria (properties of the registry itself, already proven against mock features in spec 039) ever asked this package to fix. What comes next is P11 (the remaining specialized surfaces - the multitrack arrangement view, the piano, the envelope editor, the scopes, the VU meters).

## 2026-09-15 — Spec 051 implemented: the multitrack arrangement view's engine, opening package P11

User direction: continue with P11 next. Read all three of its v1 source files in full before scoping anything (`multitrack.js`, 803 lines; `dawtrack.js`, 449 lines; `trackControl.js`, 115 lines) and chose the multitrack arrangement view first - the first-named and largest of P11's five surfaces - following this roadmap's own established "mechanism, then interactive leaf, then screen" pattern (loop creator: specs 043/044/045; Nibbles: specs 047/048/049). This spec is the headless mechanism only: per-track note/effect segmentation, move-note editing, and a real track mute/solo audio effect - no canvas, no drag gesture, no live `AnalyserNode` yet.

The most significant finding was architectural, not a bug: v1 keeps **three independent, locally-mirrored copies** of the same mute/solo interaction rule ("soloing a track mutes every other track; un-soloing restores them") - `multitrack.js`, `trackControl.js` (the classic grid's own smaller header), and `audio.js` (the one copy that actually matters, since it sets real `FilterChain` volumes) - all three kept in sync only by broadcasting `EVENT.trackStateChange` and each independently reacting to it. v2 never ported that listener at all when the engine was first adapted (spec 012) - nothing triggered it at the time, and it is genuinely new P11 territory, not a dropped P2 feature. Rather than reproduce v1's own triplication, this spec consolidates it: a new `domain/arrangement/index.js` is the single owner of every track's mute/solo flag, calling one new, additive `domain/playback/index.js#setTrackVolumes` (itself a thin forward to one new additive function in `domain/playback/engine/audio.js`, a parameterized port of the module's own former inline `updateTrackVolumes()` math) whenever it changes. No broadcast event is needed in v2, because nothing else independently mirrors the state anymore. A known v1 quirk was found by reading `dawtrack.js`/`trackControl.js` side by side and preserved rather than "fixed": muting a currently-soloed track clears only that track's own solo flag, with no cascade restoring the other tracks it had muted, because v1's own mute-button handler never passes the `wasSolo` argument its solo-button handler does.

Six small, additive one-line forwards were added to `domain/playback/index.js` (`getFineTuneForNote`, `getFineTuneForPeriod`, `getFtNotePeriod`, `usesLinearFrequency`, `getTickTiming`, `emulatesProtracker1OffsetBug`) and two to `domain/song/index.js` (`getInstrumentAt`, `getRawNoteName`) - all on the same footing spec 046's `getSampleRateForPeriod` already established, so `domain/arrangement` never imports `domain/playback/engine/` directly, matching `domain/sampleEditor/{loopCreator,resample}.js`'s own precedent exactly. `moveNote` itself (one undoable edit clearing a source cell and populating a destination) was added to `domain/song/index.js`, not `domain/arrangement` - it is exactly the shape of pattern-cell mutation that module's own `putNote`/`removeNote` already own, needing the same private diff helpers; `domain/arrangement#moveNote` is a one-line forward.

Three real findings surfaced only while writing tests, not while reading v1's source:

1. **`getNoteName`'s own placeholder question** (an open item design.md deliberately left unresolved) turned out to hide a second, more surprising fact: `_getNoteName` checks `note.index` before `note.period`, *unconditionally of tracker mode*, unlike `formatNoteName`'s own mode-branching lookup. Because `Note#setPeriod` always back-fills `note.index` too, a ProTracker note's arrangement-view label is actually resolved through the FT note table, not the period table - a real, different display path for the same cell, faithfully ported as a new `domain/song#getRawNoteName`, not "fixed" to agree with `formatNoteName`. The test's own first draft assumed the two would agree and failed (`'C-5' !== 'C-3'`) before this was traced.
2. **`getFineTuneForPeriod(period, 0)` is not a no-op** - it performs a real table lookup (`periodNoteTable[period].tune[8]`) that can shift the period by a fraction even at finetune zero, which a hand-traced expected value in the sample-length-cap test got wrong by one step before the test was rewritten to compute its own expected values through the same already-tested forwards `getPerTrackBlocks` itself composes, rather than a second, independent hand calculation.
3. **Neither available fixture has a non-looping sample** - `wizballalt.mod`'s 31 instruments and `sin.xm`'s one instrument all loop, so the length-cap branch (which explicitly skips a looping sample) had nothing to exercise. Rather than add a new fixture, the test temporarily disables a real instrument's own loop and swaps in a larger synthetic sample array, safe because a fresh `Tracker.processFile` call rebuilds every instrument object from scratch, so nothing leaks into any other test.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 748 tests, all passing, up from 728 (4 in `tests/song.test.js`, 4 in `tests/playback.test.js`, 12 in a new `tests/arrangement.test.js` - segmentation boundaries and sticky-instrument carry-forward, `0C`/FastTracker volume-column overrides against both a `.mod` and a `.xm` fixture, effect flattening, the sample-length cap and its cross-note accumulation, an enabled loop never being capped, `moveNote` forwarding, and the full mute/solo interaction table including the preserved quirk). This spec touches no YAML/`capabilities.js`: `node compiler/generate.js` (16 warnings, 0 errors) and `node build.mjs app` (loose ends unchanged at 88) both confirmed unaffected by an unchanged run, the same precedent specs 039/040/043/047 already established for an engine-first spec.

Not done and recorded as such: the interactive specialized-component leaf (`multitrackView` - scroll/zoom, drag-to-move-note, the live per-track oscilloscope, waveform thumbnails, the per-track control-strip drawing), the classic grid's own smaller mute/solo header, and the screen (View-menu wiring). No browser verification occurred in this environment. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-15 — Spec 052 implemented: the interactive multitrack view

User direction: continue with the multitrackView interactive leaf next. Gave spec 051's own headless engine its interactive front end - the port's sixth specialized-component leaf, following the same "engine, then interactive leaf" split spec 043/044 already established for the loop creator. `multitrackGeometry.js` (new, pure) ports `multitrack.js`'s own scroll/zoom/seek/scrollbar-thumb formulas and `dawtrack.js`'s own control-button layout exactly; `runtime/multitrackController.js` (new) dispatches five gesture regions (both scrollbars, the header ruler, the control strip, and the lane area's own note-drag-or-pan branch) the same way `multitrack.js#onDragStart`'s own priority order does; the draw kind reads `domain/song`/`domain/arrangement` directly, the same no-bound-field contract every specialized leaf's own draw kind already has.

One new, genuinely new mechanism: the live oscilloscope needs a per-track `AnalyserNode`, which only `domain/playback/engine/audio.js` can create - a new `domain/playback/index.js#getTrackScopeData(track, target)` lazily creates and connects one analyser per track, growing on demand rather than reacting to `EVENT.trackCountChange` (the same "consolidate v1's own event-driven bookkeeping into a direct call" choice spec 051 already made for mute/solo). `domain/playback/silentContext.js#StubNode` gained a `getByteTimeDomainData` stub (fills with 128, silence) so this is testable headlessly at all - the first specialized leaf in this port driven by live audio analysis rather than discrete song/pattern/sample data.

Three deliberate visual simplifications were recorded rather than silently dropped, all in requirements.md before any drawing code was written: no waveform thumbnails inside blocks (a block is a flat instrument-tinted rectangle with a name label), no scale-9 bordered block/button panel image, and the record button is drawn and wired only to a controller-level `setRecordHandler` hook - no FX panel exists anywhere in this port yet to give it a real destination, mirroring `nibblesController`'s own precedent of separating `handle()`'s input vocabulary from screen-level wiring.

Three real findings surfaced only while writing tests, none from misreading the source:

1. **A real geometry defect in the control-strip button layout**, found by running the controller tests against a plausible-looking but too-short rect, not by inspection: `controlButtonRect`'s own ported formula (`buttonSpace = floor((trackHeight - 78)/4)`) goes negative once a lane's own height drops below roughly 90px, silently resolving every hit-test in that lane to the wrong button. Not a bug introduced here - `dawtrack.js`'s own identical formula has the same characteristic at v1's own `MIN_TRACK_H` (24px) - but worth recording for whichever later spec picks this leaf's own real viewport height. Fixed in the test suite (a realistic 90px-per-track rect), not in the geometry itself.
2. **Two sign/direction mistakes in the first draft of the wheel tests**: horizontal scroll is `scrollX -= deltaX * 0.5` (a positive `deltaX` scrolls left, not right) and the zoom factor is `deltaY > 0 ? 1.1 : 1/1.1` (zooming in needs a *positive* `deltaY`) - both the opposite of the first guess, fixed by using the sign the ported formula actually expects. The zoom-recenter math itself also drifts by up to one step, an inherent `floor()` rounding characteristic of the same formula `multitrack.js#onMouseWheel` has, not a bug - the test asserts "within one step," not an exact match.
3. **A cross-test interaction caught only by the assertion itself failing**: soloing one track already mutes every other track by cascade (spec 051's own `setTrackSolo`), so testing the mute button on an already-cascade-muted track right afterward actually toggles it back off - fixed by resetting every track's own state between the two button assertions.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 778 tests, all passing, up from 748 (10 in a new `tests/multitrackGeometry.test.js`, 11 in a new `tests/multitrackController.test.js`, 8 in a new `tests/multitrackView.test.js`, 1 in `tests/playback.test.js`). This spec touches no YAML/`capabilities.js`: `multitrackView` is registered but not yet placed in any component tree, the same "mechanism, not yet placed" state `loopCreatorView` was left in between specs 044 and 045 - `node compiler/generate.js` (16 warnings, 0 errors) and `node build.mjs app` (loose ends unchanged at 88) both confirmed unaffected by an unchanged run.

Not done and recorded as such: the menu bar, the classic grid's own smaller mute/solo header (`trackControl.js`), and the screen wiring (a View-menu entry, real layout, breakpoints) - the next spec's own, the same way spec 045/049 each followed their own mechanism/overlay spec. No browser verification occurred in this environment. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-15 — Spec 053 implemented: the multitrack screen

User direction: continue with the multitrack screen next. Placed `multitrackView` for the first time, closing the three-part arc P11's own arrangement-view surface opened with spec 051.

The real work of this spec was scoping it honestly, not authoring YAML: reading `controlPanel.js`'s own two view-toggle icon buttons ("Column view"/"Row view", `EVENT.showView("main"/"multitrack")`) made clear that v1's multitrack view is an *alternate* to the classic, column-based pattern grid, toggled in place. Grepping `app.yaml` and every pattern-adjacent component file for `patternGrid`, though, found nothing at all - the classic grid (specs 030/031) has never been authored into any screen, a real, standing P7 gap this port has carried since spec 031 recorded it as unallocated, unmentioned by any of the specs in between (P8 through P10, and specs 051/052). With no classic grid screen to toggle *away from*, building the row/column toggle here would either be a dead control or require this spec to also build P7's own leftover screen - a real, substantial, unrelated piece of work, not P11's. Recorded as out of scope rather than either quietly skipped or silently absorbed: no toggle, no classic-grid mute/solo header (`trackControl.js` has nowhere to attach either), no View-menu entry (nothing to switch to or from).

With that resolved, the actual placement was small: one new `components/multitrackPanel.yaml` (a plain wrapper panel around `multitrackView`, both `width: fill, height: fill`), added to `normalMainView`'s own content list (spec 048) between `editPad` and `songPatternList` - no `visibleWhen` gate, matching those two siblings' own precedent, and no `capabilities.js` mapping at all, since `multitrackView`'s own controller already calls `domain/song`/`domain/arrangement` directly for every gesture (confirmed by re-reading spec 052's own requirements.md rather than assuming a Nibbles-style screen with several new surrounding controls was needed - it draws its own control strip, blocks, effects and live scope inline, so nothing ordinary needed authoring around it).

Two pre-existing tests broke from the new sibling alone, not from any behavior change - the same "hardcoded position" class of break specs 048/049 each hit once before: `tests/server.test.js`'s own alphabetical file-listing assertion, and `tests/yaml.test.js`'s own positional index into `normalMainView`'s raw parsed content, shifted by the new entry. Both fixed by updating the expectation, not the code.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 782 tests, all passing, up from 778 (4 new in `tests/multitrackScreen.test.js`, mirroring `tests/nibblesMainView.test.js`'s own convention: `computeGenericLayout()` against the real, compiled `app.yaml`, seeded with `main-view.value` as the real app's own initial `""`, not JS's `undefined`). `node compiler/generate.js` (16 warnings, 0 errors) and `node build.mjs app` (loose ends unchanged at 88) both confirmed unaffected by an unchanged run - no command was authored, so neither a new `mocked-command` nor an `unresolved-*` entry was even possible.

**The multitrack arrangement view's own three-part arc (specs 051/052/053) is complete.** What remains of P11 is the piano, the envelope editor, the scopes and the VU meters - and, recorded again here rather than left implicit, the classic pattern grid's own still-unauthored screen (a P7 gap, not P11's, but one that will keep blocking a real row/column toggle and the classic mute/solo header until some later spec closes it). No browser verification occurred in this environment. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-15 — Spec 054 implemented: the VU meter

User direction: continue with P11 next. Chose the VU meter (`vumeter.js`, 135 lines, read in full) over the piano, the envelope editor and the scopes: it is the smallest of P11's remaining four surfaces, genuinely passive (no click/drag/keyboard in v1 at all, unlike every specialized leaf before it), and - the real motivator - `app.yaml` has carried a bare scalar reference, `volume meter: volumemeter`, since the application shell was first authored, reported every run since as an `unresolved-component-reference` warning (one of this build's own 16 standing warnings). Reading `compiler/convert.js#convertReference` before writing anything found that a bare scalar resolves against a component *file* (`components/<name>.yaml`), not a type name - the file itself had simply never existed. This spec created it, closing a real, already-flagged gap rather than opening new territory.

Built engine, leaf and placement together in one pass - the same "small enough for one spec" call Nibbles' own combined engine+leaf spec (047) already made, rather than the three-part arc the multitrack view needed. `domain/playback/index.js` gained `getMasterScopeData` (lazily creates a `ChannelSplitter(2)` and two analysers, connected once to `Audio.cutOffVolume` - the mix's own final gain node, the same real node v1's own `menu.js#vumeter.connect` taps, not any per-track signal spec 052's own `getTrackScopeData` already covers). `renderers/canvas/vuMeterGeometry.js` (new, pure) ports the dynamic-range formula and the dot-zone thresholds exactly; dots draw as flat rectangles by zone color, not v1's own skin-atlas sprite images - the same flat-fill simplification `nibblesGrid`/`multitrackView` already made, and, this time, the *only* real visual detail this leaf has. `volumeMeter`'s own controller is the smallest in this port - hover/focused/disabled only, no gesture vocabulary at all, existing only because `ticks: true` (needed for the meter to keep redrawing) requires a controller to attach to regardless of whether it has anything real to do.

One real design correction, caught immediately by re-running the compiler rather than assuming success: authoring the new placement with `layout: {width: fixed, height: fixed}` (the bare keyword, the shape every prior specialized leaf's own placement uses) replaced the one warning being closed with *two new* `layout-default-fallback` ones - `resolveLayoutFields` only has a fallback size for a `styled: true` type with a skin entry or one of four hardcoded types, and grepping `skin/default.yaml` confirmed no prior specialized leaf appears there either; each instead sidesteps this entirely with `width: fill, height: fill`, which was not the right fit for a leaf sharing a fixed row with the menu bar and notifications. Fixed by authoring the actual numbers (`160`/`16`) directly - self-sufficient, no default lookup, no warning.

Three pre-existing tests broke from the resolution itself, not from any bug, fixed the same way similar breaks have been fixed all through this port: two assertions in `tests/compiler.test.js` that pinned the *unresolved* state exactly (including a placeholder node's own id, which changes once it resolves to a real component's own authored name); one loose-end category count in `tests/emit.test.js`; one file listing in `tests/server.test.js`.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 797 tests, all passing, up from 782 (5 in a new `tests/vuMeterGeometry.test.js`, 4 in a new `tests/vuMeterController.test.js`, 5 in a new `tests/volumeMeterView.test.js`, 1 in `tests/playback.test.js`). `node compiler/generate.js`: 15 warnings, down from 16 - confirmed by diff, not just a fresh zero-error run. `node build.mjs app`: loose ends 87, down from 88.

What remains of P11 is the piano, the envelope editor and the scopes - and the same still-open P7 gap (the classic pattern grid's own screen) recorded again in spec 053. No browser verification occurred in this environment. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-15 — Spec 055 implemented: the envelope editor's engine

User direction: continue with P11 next. Chose the envelope editor over the piano and the scopes: it closes a real, already-recorded gap ("no envelope editing" - both spec 042 and spec 050 listed it among the sample editor's own known limits), and, unlike the piano, needs no new live-audio-preview mechanism at all (`instrument.play()`-driven note audition is a real, not-yet-built capability this port has never needed - the piano would require it, the envelope editor does not).

Read `envelope.js` (239 lines, the drag graph) and `envelopePanel.js` (258 lines, the surrounding panel) in full before scoping, and confirmed the envelope *data* itself needed no new engine work: `domain/playback/engine/models/instrument.js#volumeEnvelope`/`panningEnvelope` and `engine/fileformats/fasttracker.js#processEnvelope` already carry it, ported wholesale, simply unused until now. This spec is the headless mechanism only, matching the "engine, then interactive leaf, then screen" pattern loop creator (043-045) and the multitrack arrangement view (051-053) already completed - the port's eighth specialized-component leaf and its screen follow in later specs. A new internal sibling module, `domain/sampleEditor/envelope.js` (mirroring `loopCreator.js`'s own precedent exactly), gained: `getEnvelope` (a safe, falsy-default snapshot - v1 itself never explicitly resets `sustain`/`loop`/their own point indices back to false/0, only ever setting them from a real XM's own envelope-type bitmask, so every read has to be falsy-safe the same way `envelopePanel.js#setInstrument` already is); `setPoint` (the drag-commit clamp - point 0 never moves horizontally, every other point clamps to its own immediate neighbors or 324 for the last one, y always 0-64); `addPoint`/`removePoint` (both real, easy-to-miss no-ops on a disabled envelope - the guard sits above both button handlers rather than being named as a shared precondition; add either reuses an existing past-count point, repositioning it only if it does not already sit past the previous point, or appends a brand-new one 10 units out, both gated by a "within 10 of the canvas's own right edge" guard; remove floors at a count of 2 and re-clamps `sustainPoint`/`loopStartPoint`/`loopEndPoint`); the three plain toggles (`setEnabled`/`setSustain`/`setLoop`, no other side effect, matching v1's own checkbox handlers exactly).

Two of the test suite's own first-draft expectations were wrong, caught immediately by running them: a "clamped at the previous point" case passed a value already above the floor (nothing should have clamped at all); and a "reused point gets repositioned" case wrongly expected the reused point's own `y` to reset the way a brand-new point's does - re-reading `envelopePanel.js:47-50` showed the reuse branch only ever reassigns `x`. Both fixed in the test's own expectations, not the ported code. A third small thing: `Tracker.getCurrentInstrument()` for the unused instrument slot 0 is `undefined`, not an empty instrument object - the first draft's own setup assertion tried to read a property off it and threw, fixed by asserting `undefined` directly.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 813 tests, all passing, up from 797 (16 in a new `tests/envelope.test.js`, against a real `.xm` fixture's own genuinely enabled-with-sustain volume envelope and disabled panning envelope, plus hand-built instrument-shaped objects for the pure clamp/branch math). This spec touches no YAML/`capabilities.js`: `node compiler/generate.js` (15 warnings, unchanged) and `node build.mjs app` (loose ends unchanged at 87) both confirmed unaffected by an unchanged run.

Not done and recorded as such: the interactive drag-based envelope graph (the port's eighth specialized-component leaf), the surrounding panel (title bar, enable checkbox, add/remove buttons, sustain/loop checkboxes and spinboxes), wiring it twice (volume and panning) into the sample-editor screen, and instrument-property undo (unchanged from spec 042's own recorded, port-wide limitation) - all a later spec's own. No browser verification occurred in this environment. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-15 — Spec 056 implemented: the interactive envelope view

User direction: continue with the interactive envelope leaf next. Gave spec 055's own headless engine its interactive front end - the port's eighth specialized-component leaf, following the same "engine, then interactive leaf" split spec 043/044 already established for the loop creator.

The real new thing this spec had to solve, not merely port: this is the first specialized leaf in the whole port that needs to know *which* instance of a shared concept it addresses - v1 itself instantiates `Envelope(type)`/`EnvelopePanel(type)` twice, once for `"volume"` and once for `"panning"`. Every specialized leaf before it (the pattern grid, the waveform view, the loop creator, Nibbles, the multitrack view, the VU meter) addresses exactly one domain concept with no variant at all. Resolved the same way `rangeSlider`'s own per-node `vertical` override already works (spec 018): one new node-level property, `envelopeType`, read by `mountGeneric.js`'s own controller-construction loop right next to where `node.vertical` already is - one controller factory, one draw kind, one behavior name, not two parallel component-type registrations for a difference that is otherwise nothing but which data the same leaf reads.

A second real mechanism gap, found by tracing how "which point is the pointer nearest" could possibly work at all: `mountGeneric.js`'s own fine-grained `pointermove` forwarding (continuous position, not just enter/leave) is hardcoded to exactly one behavior today (`"flyout"`, spec 020's own submenu row-hover preview). This leaf needs the identical mechanism - widened to include `"envelope"` too, rather than given a second, parallel one.

`renderers/canvas/envelopeGeometry.js` (new, pure) ports the fixed 324x64 logical coordinate space and its own y-flip (an envelope's value increases upward, the opposite of screen-pixel y) exactly, plus the nearest-point hit test; `runtime/envelopeController.js` (new) drags *relatively* - it captures the active point's own start position and the pointer's own logical-space position at drag start, then on every move computes `start + (current - start)` and hands the candidate straight to spec 055's own `setPoint`, which already owns every clamp bound. No "ghost while dragging, commit on release" the way the multitrack view's own note-drag (spec 052) needs - envelope point edits have no undo in v1 either, so every drag event writes live, matching v1's own `onDrag` calling `me.refresh()` on every move.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 835 tests, all passing, up from 813 (5 in a new `tests/envelopeGeometry.test.js`, 11 in a new `tests/envelopeController.test.js` against `sin.xm`'s own real envelope - including a hand-derived real drag with a real clamp, confirmed correct on the first run - 6 in a new `tests/envelopeView.test.js`). This spec touches no YAML/`capabilities.js`: `envelopeView` is registered but not yet placed in any component tree, the same "mechanism, not yet placed" state `loopCreatorView`/`multitrackView` were each left in between their own mechanism and screen specs - `node compiler/generate.js` (15 warnings, unchanged) and `node build.mjs app` (loose ends unchanged at 87) both confirmed unaffected by an unchanged run.

Worth recording precisely because it is unusual: every clamp/branch/coordinate computation matched the design sketch on the first test run, including the hand-derived real-fixture numbers - no correction needed this time, after several specs in a row each finding at least one along the way.

Not done and recorded as such: the surrounding panel (title bar, enable checkbox, add/remove buttons, sustain/loop checkboxes and spinboxes), wiring it twice (volume and panning) into the sample-editor screen, dynamic cursor hinting, and instrument-property undo (unchanged from spec 042) - a later spec's own. No browser verification occurred in this environment. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-15 — Spec 057 implemented: the envelope screen

User direction: continue with P11 next. Placed `envelopeView` for the first time, twice - closing the envelope editor's own three-part arc (055 engine, 056 leaf, 057 screen).

The real work this time was scoping, not YAML, starting from a wrong assumption caught before it went any further: grepping only `script/src/ui/` for anything importing `envelope.js`/`envelopePanel.js` found nothing, which looked like dead code. Widening the search found `script/plugins/sampleeditor/sampleView.js` - the real, live sample editor this whole port's own P10 work has been based on throughout specs 040-046/050 - imports and instantiates `EnvelopePanel` twice (`sampleView.js:245,248`). Not dead code; corrected before scoping any further. Reading that file's own layout code then found the real narrowing this spec needed: v1 shows both envelope panels only when the screen is in neither of two other view modes, `isMaximized` (the waveform expanded to fill the screen) nor `moreExpanded` (a second row of sample panels shown instead) - and neither mode exists anywhere in v2, a "no expand/collapse" scope decision specs 041/042 already recorded and nothing since revisited. Rather than build two entire screen modes just to decide when this one row shows, both envelope panels are shown always, disabled outside FastTracker mode instead - the same fallback shape spec 050's own `ftOnlyRow` already established, and a real justification beyond reusing a pattern: an envelope has no audible effect at all outside FastTracker mode either way.

Confirmed every one of sixteen new actions' own derived command name and six new highlight-rule aliases empirically, via `node -e` against the real `compiler/recipes.js`, before writing any YAML - the standing discipline this whole roadmap has followed. Fourteen of the sixteen actions resolved through the existing `set-value` recipe with no new vocabulary at all; the envelope's own `+`/`-` point buttons needed two genuinely new verbs (`add`/`remove`); the three checkbox pairs (enabled/sustain/loop, times two types) needed six new `CONDITION_FIELD_ALIASES` entries, the identical "deriveMockFieldName's dot-joining never agrees with toggle-generic's hyphen-joined field" gap `loop enabled`'s own alias first closed at spec 042. `domain/sampleEditor/index.js` gained twelve new `STATE_FIELDS` and sixteen capabilities generated from one small internal factory (`envelopeCapabilities(type)`) rather than sixteen hand-written near-duplicates - a real duplication removed, not a premature one, since this is the first time this file has needed the identical eight operations twice for two variants of one concept (mirroring spec 056's own `envelopeType` finding, one layer up).

A real, confirmed bug was found by a test with a deliberately adversarial ordering, not by inspection: the extended `publishInstrumentFields`'s own "no instrument" branch has an early `return`, so the two new envelope-republish calls - added directly after the "has instrument" branch's own last line - never ran once an instrument became unselected, leaving all twelve envelope fields silently stale. A test that toggles a real field, switches to no instrument, republishes, and asserts the field actually reverts to its own all-falsy default caught this on the first run; fixed by restructuring with an `else` and moving both calls after the whole `if`/`else`, unconditional either way.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 849 tests, all passing, up from 835 (3 in `tests/recipes.test.js`, 7 in `tests/sampleEditor.test.js`, 4 in a new `tests/envelopeScreen.test.js` mirroring spec 048/053's own `computeGenericLayout()` convention). `node compiler/generate.js` (15 warnings, unchanged - confirmed empirically before authoring, then confirmed again after) and `node build.mjs app` (loose ends unchanged at 87; a targeted grep for "envelope" in `LOOSE-ENDS.md` returns nothing - none of the sixteen new commands is a `mocked-command`) both confirmed. The identical "checkbox"-word false positive `tests/componentTypes.test.js`'s own scanning test has now caught a fourth time (specs 042/045/049, now this one) and was fixed the same way again.

**The envelope editor's own three-part arc (055/056/057) is complete.** What remains of P11 is the piano and the scopes - and, still recorded, the classic pattern grid's own still-unauthored screen (a P7 gap, not P11's). No browser verification occurred in this environment. No Git commit was created.

Fixed the same way: an explicit number (`80`, matching `measure()`'s own default).

## 2026-09-15 — Spec 060 implemented: the piano's own live-audio note-preview engine

User direction: continue with the piano next. Opens P11's own last remaining surface by building the one genuinely new mechanism it needs, deferred across five prior specs (051-059) for exactly this reason: triggering a real, audible note from a click, independent of the sequencer. `instrument.play()`/`instrument.noteOff()` (`domain/playback/engine/models/instrument.js`) were ported wholesale with the rest of the sequencer, in package P2, and have sat completely unused ever since - this spec is their first real caller.

Re-read `script/src/ui/input.js#handleNoteOn`/`handleNoteOff` (734-907) in full, the shared handler for both real keyboard typing and piano clicks/drags. Found a real, easy-to-miss requirement by reading `instrument.noteOff`'s own body rather than assuming: its non-FT branch silently no-ops unless the played note's own handle has `.isKey = true` set - v1 sets this explicitly (`keyDown[index].isKey = true;`) right after `instrument.play()` returns, one line most callers of `.play()` would have no reason to expect matters. Ported faithfully.

The other real finding came from writing the relativeNote test (PREVIEW-006), not from reading source: `instrument.play()` itself *ignores* whatever period it is handed in FastTracker mode, recomputing it from scratch via `getPeriodForNote(noteIndex)` - which, for an instrument using linear frequency (as `sin.xm`'s own does), uses a completely different formula (`7680 - (noteIndex-1)*64`) than the `FTNotes[noteIndex].period` value this spec's own code (faithfully mirroring v1) computes and assigns to `note.period` right before calling `play()`. That assignment is genuinely dead for live preview either way (in both v1 and this port) - it only ever mattered for the recording path (`Editor.putNote`), which is out of scope here. Caught by a first test assertion computing an "expected" playback rate independently and getting a number four orders of magnitude off; fixed by testing the transpose *relatively* instead (key 37 shifted by +12 sounds identical to key 49 unshifted) rather than reproducing `getPeriodForNote`'s own internal branching in a second place.

Scoped deliberately narrow, all recorded rather than silently dropped: no recording via piano clicks (v2's own real keyboard note entry, `runtime/gridController.js` spec 031, already has the opposite, pre-existing asymmetry - it records without ever previewing audio - so this spec closes the mirror-image gap for the piano rather than building a third, combined mechanism neither surface needs yet); no sustain-note toggle (`SETTINGS.sustainKeyboardNotes` is `false` in v1 and was never a user-facing option there either); no MIDI note-off pattern-registration side effect (moot - only ever reachable from real MIDI input, never a mouse/touch-driven piano).

`domain/song/keyboardNotes.js#resolveNote` (spec 031) is reused directly with no changes at all - confirmed by re-deriving its own arithmetic rather than assuming: the piano's own key index (built next, spec 061) is already fully octave-combined the same way `resolveNote`'s internal `index` variable is, so calling it with `octave: 0` reproduces the identical resolution with no double-counting. `domain/playback/index.js` gained three exports (`previewNoteOn`, `previewNoteOff`, `isPreviewNoteActive`) and one private `Map` (`activeKeys`, mirroring `keyDown[]`) - no `app` object needed at the real call site (`previewNoteOn(index)`; every other controller-invoked domain call in this port, e.g. `arrangement.toggleTrackMute`, already takes none), but accepted as a second, optional, trailing argument forwarded only to `ensureEngine`'s own lazy audio-context bootstrap, letting tests supply a fake `app` carrying `silentContext.js`'s stub the same way `tests/playback.test.js`'s own `loadModule` test already does.

Headless - no YAML/`capabilities.js` touched. 4 new tests (`tests/piano.test.js`). Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 870 tests, all passing, up from 866. `node compiler/generate.js` (15 warnings, unchanged) and `node build.mjs app` (90 loose ends, unchanged) both confirmed unaffected, as expected with no YAML change.

**P11's own last surface is open but not yet built.** The piano's own interactive leaf (key hit-testing/drawing/drag, spec 061) and screen placement (the toggle panel, close button, octave spinbox, spec 062) remain. No browser verification occurred in this environment. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-15 — Spec 061 implemented: the piano's own interactive leaf

Continued straight on from spec 060 in the same session (no new user direction needed - "the piano" already covers this). Gives spec 060's own headless preview engine its interactive front end: `pianoView`, the port's tenth specialized-component leaf, following the same "engine, then interactive leaf" split this port used for the loop creator, multitrack view and envelope view.

Re-read `pianoView.js` (252 lines) in full, already substantially covered during spec 060's own research. Ported `getKeyAtPoint`'s own two-region hit test (white-key territory below a computed `bKeyHeight` threshold; black-key territory above it, split into 13 half-white-key-width sub-columns, two of which - the E/F and B/C seams - fall through to the white key beneath) and `renderInternal`'s own two tiling loops into `renderers/canvas/pianoGeometry.js` (new, pure): `keyAtPoint(local, rect)` (widget-relative, 0 for a miss) and `keyRectsFor(rect, baseOctave)` (the drawable rect list, each already tagged with the *combined* index `domain/playback#previewNoteOn`/`previewNoteOff`/`isPreviewNoteActive` all expect). Verified empirically (`node -e`) before writing any test: `keyAtPoint` at the center of each of `keyRectsFor`'s own returned rects resolves back to that same rect's own combined index, for both white and black keys, and a `y < 0` miss returns 0.

`runtime/pianoController.js` (new) ports `onDown`/`onDrag`'s own identical bodies (v1 literally shares one implementation between the two) as a single `pressAt` helper: resolve the point, no-op if it is the same key already held (PIANO-003, matching v1's own `key !== prevDown` guard) or the point misses entirely, otherwise release whichever key was held and press the new one. Reads `domain/song#getCurrentOctave()` fresh on every event rather than keeping a synced local copy the way v1's own closure-based `octave` variable does - since v2's octave state already has exactly one owner, a second copy could only drift, never help. `blur` and `setDisabled(true)` both release a held key, matching the contract every other specialized leaf's controller already has for losing focus/becoming disabled mid-gesture.

One real, first-of-its-kind test-infrastructure wrinkle, not a product bug: `previewNoteOn` (spec 060) calls `ensureEngine`, and a real controller call site never supplies the `app` that lets a test point it at a silent context - every controller-invoked domain call up to now (`arrangement.toggleTrackMute`, etc.) never needed to bootstrap the audio engine itself. Solved by having each test file's own setup call `previewNoteOn` directly, once, with a fake `app` carrying `silentContext.js`'s stub (an index that resolves to no note, so the only effect is the bootstrap) - after which every subsequent bare `previewNoteOn(index)` call (from the controller, exactly as production makes it) reuses the already-initialized engine, since `ensureEngine`'s own `engineReady` guard is now satisfied.

`runtime/componentTypes.js`/`runtime/behaviors.js` gained the `pianoView` type/`"piano"` behavior registration, the same generic wiring every prior specialized leaf used (no per-node options needed, unlike `envelopeView`'s own `envelopeType`). `renderers/canvas/drawKinds.js` gained a `pianoView` entry: flat-colored white/black key rectangles, highlighted via `isPreviewNoteActive`, no skin-atlas sprites - the same simplification `nibblesGrid`/`multitrackView`/`volumeMeter` already made.

14 new tests across three new files (`tests/pianoGeometry.test.js` 4, `tests/pianoController.test.js` 6, `tests/pianoView.test.js` 4). Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 884 tests, all passing, up from 870. `node compiler/generate.js` (15 warnings, unchanged) and `node build.mjs app` (90 loose ends, unchanged) both confirmed - no YAML/`capabilities.js` touched at all.

**The piano's own key grid is built but not yet placed anywhere.** What remains of the piano arc - and of P11 entirely - is screen placement: the `piano.active`-gated toggle panel, the close button, and the octave spinbox (spec 062). No browser verification occurred in this environment. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-15 — Spec 062 implemented: the piano screen, closing P11

Continued straight on from spec 061 in the same session. Places `pianoView` for the first time - a toggle-shown panel with a close button and an octave spinbox - closing the piano's own three-part arc (060 engine, 061 leaf, 062 screen) and, with it, **P11: the port roadmap's last package of remaining specialized surfaces.**

Re-read `script/src/ui/mainPanel.js:51-73,194-197` (the piano panel's own real host in v1 - not `pianoView.js` itself, which only draws the keys): it is a *docked* panel, shown or hidden below whichever of the classic grid/multitrack view/Nibbles is currently active, shrinking the remaining space rather than replacing anything. This shaped the placement decision directly: `components/pianoPanel.yaml` (new) is a *sibling* of `normalMainView`/`nibblesPanel` in `app.yaml`'s own `main.content` list, gated only by `piano.active` - independent of `main-view.value`/`pattern-view.value`, so it shows or hides regardless of which of those is active underneath, exactly matching v1's own behavior (confirmed both directions with a dedicated test). A fixed `height: 200` is used (v1's own `Layout.pianoHeight` varies 100/150/200 by breakpoint; this port does not build responsive breakpoints, an already-recorded scope boundary).

Two real, confirmed findings surfaced by testing, not by reading source:

1. **`togglePiano`/`piano.active` were already wired to a real, pre-existing capability** - `domain/settings/index.js#togglePiano`, a persisted `SETTINGS.showPiano`-equivalent toggle that predates this whole session - not a generated mock as design.md's own sketch had assumed (matching `top-view.set`/`pattern-view.set`'s own precedent). Corrected once discovered; zero new loose ends from this spec at all.
2. **A real, confirmed, previously-untested bug** in `runtime/genericLayout.js#isVisible`: `compiler/yaml.js` has no boolean/number literal type at all (every scalar is a plain string), so `visibleWhen: {oneOf: [true]}` compiles to the string `"true"`, not the JS boolean `true` - but a field driven by a *real* capability (as opposed to a generated mock's own string-labeled toggle) can genuinely hold a JS boolean (`domain/settings#applyToggle`'s own `next`). The mismatch meant `piano.active: true` could never satisfy `oneOf: ["true"]`, caught immediately by the first layout test written for this spec. Checking `nibbles-info-visible.value` (spec 049, the only other boolean-valued `visibleWhen` gate in the whole app) directly with `computeGenericLayout` confirmed it has had the identical, silently-broken shape since it was authored - `nibblesInfoPanel` has never actually been able to show, and nothing tested it end-to-end before now. Fixed at the root (`isVisible` coerces the live value to a string before comparing `oneOf`, safe for every existing string-valued gate too), with a dedicated regression test added to `tests/viewVisibility.test.js` (spec 025's own home for `visibleWhen` tests) rather than only exercised incidentally through the piano's own screen test.

One new, genuinely real capability: `domain/song/index.js#applyOctaveChange` (the octave spinbox) - a distinct wrapper from the existing `setCurrentOctave(value)` (called internally with a plain number by `runtime/gridController.js`'s own F1-F7 keys, spec 031), since one capability's own `(app, detail)` signature can't double as the other's. Authored with the wide FT ceiling (`min: 0, max: 7`) and the narrower ProTracker range enforced inside the capability itself via `setCurrentOctave`'s own existing clamp - the same "wide ceiling, narrow clamp inside the capability" shape spec 050's own finetune capability already established.

9 new tests (5 in `tests/pianoScreen.test.js`, 3 extending `tests/song.test.js`, 1 regression test in `tests/viewVisibility.test.js`). Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 893 tests, all passing, up from 884. `node compiler/generate.js` (15 warnings, unchanged) and `node build.mjs app` (90 loose ends, unchanged - `current-octave.set` is real, `piano.toggle` already was) both confirmed.

**P11 is complete.** Every specialized surface this package named - the multitrack arrangement view, the VU meter, the envelope editor, the scopes, the classic pattern grid's own screen and toggle (closing the standing P7 gap along the way), and now the piano - is built, placed, and tested. The only recorded, real follow-ups still open from this whole arc are the classic grid's own smaller per-track mute/solo/FX header (`trackControl.js`, spec 059's own recorded gap) and the piano's own pattern-sidebar entry point (`patternSidebar.js`, spec 062's own recorded gap) - both genuinely buildable, neither blocking anything. No browser verification occurred in this environment. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-15 — Spec 063 implemented: random song, and a P9/P12 priority decision

User direction: "continue with P12 next." Before starting, flagged that P12 is explicitly optional in the roadmap ("H", conditional on a reconsidered open decision) while P9 (persistence/providers/file operations) is still open and part of the required "it ships" milestone ("G") - and that P12 itself bundles two separable things (a DOM renderer, which the design doc says "buys no parity," and an accessibility layer, which the same doc says is worth deciding on its own merits). Asked the user directly rather than guessing; the answer was to switch to P9 instead.

Continuing P9 (last touched by spec 038, XM export), read every prior spec's own recorded "remains unallocated" list (033-038) to find the next well-scoped, unblocked slice rather than attempting the whole remaining package at once - it is large (Dropbox, disk-operation screens for samples/playlists, a `Playlist` domain model, the WAV/MP3 render pipeline, HippoPlayer's own screen wiring). Picked `randomSong`/`randomSongXM` (the File menu's "Open Random MOD/XM Song," `unresolved-action-reference` warnings since the shell was authored): small, fully unblocked (needs only `domain/playback#loadModule`, already built), and explicitly named as remaining in three prior specs' own scope boundaries.

Re-read `script/src/ui/diskOperations.js#playRandomSong` (634-654) and `script/src/models/playlist.js` (266 lines, in full) before scoping anything. Two real findings shaped the cut: `Tracker.autoPlay` (already ported wholesale in P2) does not itself start playback despite its name - it only gates `EVENT.songEnd`/endless-loop detection during an *already-playing* song, relevant only to playlist auto-advance - so it is not set here, a real simplification, not an oversight. `randomPlayList`/`generatePlayList` and HippoPlayer's own disk-ops wiring both genuinely depend on a `Playlist` domain model (current index, next/prev, shuffle, `.pls`/`.m3u` parse/export, auto-advance) that is a real, separate, moderately-sized piece of work - cut cleanly from this spec rather than grown into by accident.

`compiler/recipes.js` gained one new `verb-action` verb, `random` - the first action names in this port with no leading verb at all ("random song"/"random song xm"), confirmed unsupported by every existing recipe before authoring anything. As a recorded side effect (the same class spec 048's own "show" verb addition already had once), `randomPlayList` also now resolves, to `play-list.random` - left unmapped on purpose (a `mocked-command` loose end, not `unresolved-action-reference`), since mapping it needs the `Playlist` model above.

A real architectural-boundary violation was caught by running the test suite, not anticipated up front: the first draft placed `fetchRandomModuleUrl`'s own `fetch()` and hardcoded API host directly in the new `domain/randomSong/index.js`, and `tests/domainBoundary.test.js`'s own "only `domain/providers/` may name a provider host" check failed immediately. Fixed at the architectural level, not by weakening the test: the function moved into `domain/providers/bassoon.js` (the same file already hardcoding this exact host for `proxyUrl`), re-exported from `domain/providers/index.js` the same way `proxyUrl`/`isProxyUrl` already are - `domain/randomSong/index.js` now only ever calls it, never names the host itself. `tests/randomSong.test.js` was added to that boundary test's own allowlist (it legitimately needs the literal host string as a fake-fetch map key).

6 new tests (`tests/randomSong.test.js`) plus 2 extending `tests/recipes.test.js`. Two pre-existing tests broke from the real, expected count changes and were fixed: `tests/compiler.test.js`'s own `unresolved-action-reference >= 10` threshold (now `>= 7`, three resolved) and `tests/domainBoundary.test.js`'s own allowlist.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 901 tests, all passing, up from 893. `node compiler/generate.js` - 12 warnings, down from 15 (`randomSong`/`randomSongXM`/`randomPlayList` all resolved). `node build.mjs app` - 88 loose ends, down from 90 (`song.random`/`song-xm.random` are real; `play-list.random` is a new, expected mock).

## 2026-09-15 — Spec 064 implemented: sample export (WAV/RAW/XI)

User direction: "continue with P9 next." Read every prior P9 spec's own "remains unallocated" list (033-038, 063) again before picking a slice: Dropbox, the `Playlist` model, MP3/the render pipeline and a save/export screen are each their own real, separately-scoped piece of work; sample export (WAV/RAW/XI) was the smallest, most unblocked one left, and spec 038 had already deliberately factored `fasttracker.js`'s own `writeInstrumentSamples()` helper "ready for a later sample-export spec's own `writeXI` port to reuse" - this is that spec.

Read `script/src/ui/diskOp_Save.js` (the disk-operations save panel) and `script/src/audio/riffWave.js` in full, plus re-read `fasttracker.js#writeXI` (680-809, already read once for spec 038's own research). Confirmed, by diffing both v1 blocks side by side rather than trusting the shared name alone, that `writeXI`'s own per-sample header/data block really is byte-for-byte identical to what `writeInstrumentSamples()` already ports - so `domain/moduleExport/fasttracker.js#writeXI(instrumentIndex)` needed only its own fixed 298-byte outer header (signature, name, tracker-name block, format version, the 96-entry sample-number table, both envelopes' points/counts/sustain/loop bytes, vibrato, fadeout, 22 reserved bytes, sample count - summed by hand from v1's own field widths to confirm the total, not trusted from v1's own comment) before delegating straight to the existing helper.

New sibling module `domain/moduleExport/sample.js` ports `encodeRIFFsample` (`writeWav`, mono 8000Hz RIFF PCM at 8 or 16 bits, matching v1's own "how can we know this from raw samples?" hardcoded rate) and the save panel's inline RAW 8-bit branch. That RAW branch has a real, confirmed defect, not a stylistic quirk: it allocates `sample.length` bytes, writes two zero bytes first, then writes only `sample.length - 2` data bytes - a file of the promised length starting with two spurious zero bytes and silently missing its own last two data points, with no plausible reader relying on that shape. Fixed rather than ported (`writeRaw` writes exactly `sampleData.length` bytes, one per sample point), matching the bar spec 034/037/040/043 already set for this class of finding.

`domain/moduleExport/index.js#exportSample(app, format)` is the new capability (`format` one of `"xi"`/`"wav8"`/`"wav16"`/`"raw8"`), reading the live current instrument/sample from `Tracker` and dispatching to the right writer. Refactored `exportModule`'s own inline Blob/`URL.createObjectURL`/synthetic-`<a>`-click block into a shared `downloadBuffer()` helper rather than duplicating it a second time. Ran `node compiler/generate.js` before writing this spec's own requirements.md and confirmed no authored YAML anywhere references sample export (12 warnings, none naming it) - unlike module export, which reused an already-authored "Save" menu item, there is no save/export screen yet for a sample to be exported from, so `exportSample` stays unwired to any `capabilities.js` entry, matching every "engine first" spec since 039 (`domain/features`, `domain/sampleEditor/processing.js`, `domain/arrangement`, `domain/sampleEditor/envelope.js`).

`writeXI`'s own round trip needed a genuinely different verification shape than specs 037/038's: v2's ported engine never gained an XI reader (never in this port's scope, since v2 only loads `.mod`/`.xm`), so there is no real read path to reload the exported bytes through the way MOD/XM export could. `tests/sampleExport.test.js` adds a test-local `parseXI()` function, hand-written directly from `script/src/fileformats/fasttracker.js#loadXI`'s own field order - production code neither needs nor gets this reader, only the test does. Verified against the real `sin.xm` fixture's own instrument 1 (a real, enabled volume envelope, the same fixture/instrument `tests/envelopeController.test.js` already uses), asserting every decoded field against the *source* instrument's own live fields directly (name, sample count, both envelopes' points/count/sustain/loop/enabled flags, vibrato, fadeout, and every per-sample volume/finetuneX/panning/relativeNote/loop-bounds/bits/length/data field) - passed on the first real run once two off-by-two byte offsets in the *test's own* separate WAV-header assertions (unrelated to the XI code) were found and fixed.

7 new tests (`tests/sampleExport.test.js`). Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 908 tests, all passing, up from 901. `node compiler/generate.js` (12 warnings, unchanged) and `node build.mjs app`/`dist` (88 loose ends, unchanged) both confirmed unaffected, as expected with no YAML/compiler/`capabilities.js` change.

**P9 continues.** Sample export (WAV/RAW/XI) is done; playlist export (PLS/M3U/JSON), MP3 export, Dropbox as a save target, and a save/export screen (the UI to reach any of `exportModule`/`exportSample` beyond the File menu's own "Save") remain P9's own unallocated pieces - MP3 in particular still needs the vendored-encoder-vs-server-`lame.exe` discrepancy spec 033 already found reconciled before it can be properly scoped. ## 2026-09-15 — Spec 065 implemented: the Playlist domain model

User direction: "continue with P9 next." Picked up the piece spec 063 deliberately cut when it built `openRandomSong`/`openRandomSongXM`: `randomPlayList`/`generatePlayList` both genuinely need a real `Playlist` domain model, recorded then as "a real, separate piece of work," and left `play-list.random` as a `mocked-command` on purpose.

Read `script/src/models/playlist.js` (266 lines) in full and re-read `script/src/ui/diskOperations.js#generatePlayList` (665-678, first read for spec 063's own research). `domain/providers/bassoon.js` gained `fetchGeneratedPlayList()` (hitting `api/generatelist`), re-exported from `domain/providers/index.js` the same way `fetchRandomModuleUrl` already is - the "only `domain/providers/` may name a provider host" rule spec 063 established, applied a second time without needing to invent anything new. `domain/playlist/index.js` (new) ports the rest: state (`currentPlaylist`/`currentIndex`/shuffle order), PLS/M3U import (`parsePlaylist`), PLS/M3U/JSON export (`writePlaylist`/`exportPlaylist` - the "playlist export" line P9's own acceptance criteria have named as unallocated since spec 037), and next/prev/shuffle navigation (`playTrack`/`nextTrack`/`previousTrack`/`toggleShuffle`) built on `domain/playback#loadModule`. `openGeneratedPlaylist` is the new capability behind `play-list.random`, mapped in `capabilities.js` for the first time - the File menu's already-authored "Open Random Playlist" item, a `mocked-command` since the shell existed.

Two v1 quirks were found and ported faithfully rather than "fixed," since neither has any observable effect: `moveIndex`'s own `playOrder.indexOf(currentIndex) || 0` fallback is inert (JS's `-1` is truthy, so the intended "not found" default never triggers, but `playOrder` always contains every index whenever a playlist is set, so the branch it guards can never actually run); `isFormatSupported`'s `switch` carries a `case ".pls":` that can never match, since the extension it switches on is always computed without a leading dot. A third, real asymmetry was found only while writing the M3U round-trip test, not predicted: v1's own M3U reader ignores every `#EXTINF:-1,<title>` line entirely, deriving a track's title from its URL's own last path segment unconditionally - so exporting then re-parsing M3U does not preserve a title the way PLS/JSON do, not a bug (confirmed by reading the whole `m3u` branch - no code path anywhere reads that title), just a real, worth-recording gap between v1's own write and read sides. The test's own first draft assumed title preservation and failed immediately, corrected to assert the real behavior.

A real, pre-existing test-infrastructure gap was found and fixed along the way, not introduced by this spec: `tests/domainBoundary.test.js#sourceFiles()` named `"domain/song"` and `"domain/moduleExport"` individually in its own directory walk rather than `"domain"` as a whole, so every domain folder added since spec 033 - `settings`, `providers`, `randomSong`, `sampleEditor`, `nibbles`, `arrangement`, `diskOperations` - was silently never scanned by any of that file's four boundary checks (confirmed by instrumenting the walk directly before touching the real file, not assumed). Fixed at the point that mattered - `domain/playlist/` is the first new domain folder since the gap existed whose own correctness genuinely depends on the "only `domain/providers/` names a provider host" check actually running. Widening the walk alone surfaced only pre-existing false positives (`localStorage`/`modarchive.org` mentioned as prose in doc comments across `domain/nibbles/`, `domain/settings/` and `domain/playback/engine/fileformats/detect.js`, none of them real code violations); fixed properly by stripping both block and line comments before each content check (preserving newlines through a block-comment strip, so line numbers in a real future offender stay accurate) rather than narrowing the walk back down or special-casing those three files.

21 new tests (`tests/playlist.test.js`), mirroring `tests/randomSong.test.js`'s own fake-fetch/fake-app convention for navigation/`openGeneratedPlaylist` and `tests/sampleExport.test.js`'s own DOM-stub convention for `exportPlaylist`. Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 929 tests, all passing, up from 908. `node compiler/generate.js` (12 warnings, unchanged) confirmed. `node build.mjs app` - 87 loose ends, down from 88 (`play-list.random` is now a real command, not `mocked-command`); `node build.mjs dist` succeeds.

**P9 continues.** Playlist export (PLS/M3U/JSON) is done; MP3 export, Dropbox as a save target, auto-advance on song end, the Bassoon/Hippo disk-operations playlist listboxes, and a save/export screen (the UI to reach `exportModule`/`exportSample`/`exportPlaylist` beyond the File menu's own "Save"/"Open Random Playlist") remain P9's own unallocated pieces. No browser verification occurred in this environment. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-15 — Spec 066 implemented: playlist auto-advance on song end

User direction: "continue with P9 next." Of the pieces spec 065 left open (auto-advance, the disk-operations playlist listboxes, MP3 export, Dropbox, a save/export screen), picked auto-advance: it directly completes something that already half-works end to end (`play-list.random` fetches and plays a generated playlist's first track) into something that actually plays *through* the playlist, the way v1 does - the smallest, most valuable remaining gap, not a disconnected new mechanism.

Re-read `script/src/models/playlist.js`'s own `EVENT.songEnd` handler (249-258) and `engine/tracker.js`'s two trigger sites (330, 379, unchanged by any prior spec) before designing. Confirmed `EVENT.songEnd` has been real, already-firing engine behavior since package P2 - gated entirely on `Tracker.autoPlay`, a plain property nothing in `domain/playback/` had ever set or subscribed to, so it has sat dead from v2's own perspective for the whole roadmap so far.

`domain/playback/index.js` gained two small, additive exports: `setAutoPlay(value)` (a thin `Tracker.autoPlay = Boolean(value)` forward) and `onSongEnd(handler)` (wraps the ported engine's own `EventBus.on(EVENT.songEnd, ...)`/`.off`, returning an unsubscribe function) - the same "only this file reaches the engine" shape every prior playback addition (`setTrackVolumes`, `getTrackScopeData`, `previewNoteOn`) already follows, needing no new import at all (`EventBus`/`EVENT` were already imported here since spec 012). `domain/playlist#playTrack` now arms both flags on every call, matching v1's own `Tracker.autoPlay = true; playListActive = true;` exactly; a new `advanceOnSongEnd(app, delaySeconds)` is the handler's own body, made an ordinary exported async function rather than a private closure specifically so a test can invoke the whole delay/stop/settle/advance chain directly instead of needing to trigger a real `EVENT.songEnd` through the engine's own scheduler end to end. Registered exactly once, at module load, mirroring v1's own single permanent subscription - registering per `playTrack` call instead would have accumulated duplicate subscriptions against the engine's persistent `EventBus` singleton, confirmed by reading `engine/eventBus.js` itself (a plain module-scoped object, not recreated per `createEngine()` call).

One real v1 quirk was confirmed, not fixed: `Tracker.autoPlay`/`playListActive` are never reset once set, for the rest of the session, even if a totally unrelated module is later loaded through a different path entirely - confirmed by reading the whole of `playlist.js` for any reset of either flag (there is none). This directly shaped the new test file section's own structure rather than being caught as a surprise mid-write: the "never armed" case can only be exercised as the very first test in the file, before any other test's own `playTrack` call makes the flag permanently true for the rest of that file's run - called out with its own comment at exactly that point. A second real finding, made before writing any code rather than discovered by a failing test: v1's own handler calls `Tracker.reset()` between `stop()` and the next load, but `engine/tracker.js#reset` (2023-2025) only calls `resetDefaultSettings()` - work the next track's own `loadModule`/`Tracker.processFile` call already redoes in full when it parses that module's header, so not porting it changes no observable outcome.

3 new tests, extending `tests/playlist.test.js`: the no-op-before-ever-armed case (placed first in the file, as above); `playTrack` arming auto-advance, confirmed indirectly through `advanceOnSongEnd`'s own success (there is no direct getter for either flag, matching v1's own write-only shape); and `advanceOnSongEnd` itself, asserted against a loose elapsed-time lower bound (not a brittle exact one) to confirm both the given delay and the fixed 100ms settle actually elapse, landing on the correct next playable track through the same three-track/gap-entry fixture spec 065's own navigation tests already established.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 932 tests, all passing, up from 929. `node compiler/generate.js` (12 warnings, unchanged) and `node build.mjs app`/`dist` (87 loose ends, unchanged) both confirmed unaffected, as expected with no YAML/compiler/`capabilities.js` change - this spec only extends two already-real capabilities from the inside.

**P9 continues.** Auto-advance is done; MP3 export, Dropbox as a save target, the Bassoon/Hippo disk-operations playlist listboxes, and a save/export screen (the UI to reach `exportModule`/`exportSample`/`exportPlaylist` beyond the File menu's own two already-authored items) remain P9's own unallocated pieces. No browser verification occurred in this environment. No Git commit was created.

- **Open decision 9**: Dropbox as a save/load target, deferred as "a later spec" since spec 034 (034, 037, 038, 064, 065, 067 all repeated the same call, each accurate when written) is now permanently out of scope. Its own real, recorded reason has not changed - the OAuth redirect URI is hardcoded to the original author's own domain, a hosting/product decision - only the "later" framing does.

Both `010-port-roadmap/design.md` (P9's own design sketch and acceptance line) and `010-port-roadmap/tasks.md` (P9's own header verification line, plus an amendment note after spec 067's own closing bullet) got the same kind of superseding amendment note spec 050 used once already for hiss reduction, rather than rewritten history.

Three domain READMEs were updated to match, since they are living documentation (not spec history) and one of them (`domain/providers/README.md`) was additionally found to be stale in an unrelated way while making this edit: its own "what this deliberately does not do" section still said `randomPlayList`/`generatePlayList` "remain unwired - they need a `Playlist` domain model this port does not have yet," true when written (spec 063) but wrong since spec 065 built exactly that and wired `play-list.random` - fixed alongside the Dropbox/MP3 wording rather than left to rot further. `domain/moduleExport/README.md`'s own "what this deliberately does not do" section had the identical kind of staleness (still listing playlist export as needing "a concept this port does not have yet," done since spec 065; still describing `exportSample` as "nowhere to be triggered from yet," wired since spec 067) - fixed the same way. `domain/playback/README.md`'s own old MP3 note (predating decision 5's first reversal, so already directionally correct again by coincidence) was reworded to cite the real, current decision instead of its own stale "needs a CDN encoder" reasoning.
