## 2026-09-16 — Spec 068 implemented: loading a playlist file

User direction: "continue with P9 next." After yesterday's scope amendment (MP3/WAV whole-song export and Dropbox permanently dropped), P9's only recorded remaining piece was the Bassoon/Hippo disk-operations playlist listboxes. Scoping that screen directly first found a real, more fundamental gap underneath it: `domain/playback/engine/tracker.js#processFile` still computes a `FILETYPE.playlist` detection result (the object literal and detector branches from package P2 are all still there, byte-for-byte) but nothing has ever acted on it - confirmed by reading the function directly rather than assuming from the detector's own continued presence. A playlist file loaded through `domain/playback#loadModule` has silently done nothing since the very first playback spec. Closing that gap first, before the screen that needs it, matches this whole roadmap's own "mechanism before screen" pattern.

Read `script/src/tracker.js#processFile` (2124-2263) in full, focusing on its own `FILETYPE.playlist` branch (2248-2258): a `.json` file is already `{title, modules}` (`JSON.parse` only); anything else goes through `Playlist.parse`. `domain/playlist/index.js` gained the direct v2-native equivalent - `parsePlaylistFile(text, name)` (the same dispatch, reusing `parsePlaylist` unchanged for `.pls`/`.m3u`) and `openPlaylistUrl(app, url)` (fetches, parses, calls `setPlaylist`). Placed in `domain/playlist/`, not `domain/playback/`, for a real architectural reason confirmed before writing anything: `domain/playback/index.js` cannot import `domain/playlist/` without creating a circular dependency, since `domain/playlist` already imports `domain/playback#loadModule`, and `domain/playback`'s own documented contract is module playback only. `openPlaylistUrl` reuses `domain/providers#fetchModuleUrl` - the identical proxy-retry-on-network-failure contract `loadModule` itself already relies on for cross-origin fetches, not a second, parallel helper. No auto-play: v1's own `processFile` branch only ever calls `Playlist.set`, matched exactly (auto-play only happens from `Editor.js`'s own page-bootstrap path, reading a URL's own `index` query parameter - out of scope, spec 065's own recorded boundary on v1's browser-URL query-string sync).

Also read `script/src/ui/diskOperations.js`'s own `"playlists"`/`"hippo"` disk-op targets (586-626) and `Editor.unpackUrl` (685-702) - the still-unallocated screen work this mechanism exists for - closely enough to record real, useful groundwork in this spec's own requirements.md rather than leaving the next spec to redo the same research from scratch: of the nine real files under `playlists/*.json`, six (`demoscene2023`-`2026`, `DSR2024`, `chippies`, `raw_diskmag`) turned out to be ≥90% absolute-URL, real, fetchable entries - a much smaller curation job than `data/modules.json`'s own (spec 036), which had to filter out sixteen `demomods/`-relative entries from what was mostly relative content. `demosongs.json` is the one that *is* 100% `demomods/`-relative (the same folder spec 036 already declined to copy). `main.json`'s own "My Favorites" entry (`unpackUrl("favorites")` → a server-relative `/favorites.pls`) turned out to be a different, server-side, anonymous-session feature entirely unrelated to `domain/settings/favorites.js` (spec 033) - confirmed by tracing `unpackUrl` itself rather than assumed from the shared name.

8 new tests, extending `tests/playlist.test.js` (reusing its own established fake-fetch convention, since `openPlaylistUrl` calls the same `fetchModuleUrl` `domain/randomSong`'s own tests already exercise this way): `parsePlaylistFile` for both branches (`.json` round-trips unchanged; `.pls`/`.m3u` compared directly against `parsePlaylist`'s own output rather than hand-duplicated); `openPlaylistUrl` fetching a real `.json` and a real `.pls` playlist and setting each as current; a non-OK response, a rejected fetch (with the proxy retry also failing), malformed JSON, and well-formed JSON with no `modules` array each resolving to `false` with the previously-current playlist left completely untouched.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 952 tests, all passing, up from 944. `node compiler/generate.js` (12 warnings, unchanged) and `node build.mjs app`/`dist` (101 loose ends, unchanged) both confirmed unaffected, as expected with no YAML/compiler/`capabilities.js` change - this spec is headless, matching every "engine first" spec since 039.

**P9's last remaining piece is now narrower and better-scoped than before**: the Bassoon "Playlists" and Hippo disk-op targets (extending `domain/diskOperations/`, a new curated local playlist snapshot, and `fileOperationsPanel.yaml`'s own target radioGroup) are the only work left, with the format-loading mechanism they need now real and tested, and the curation research already done. No browser verification occurred in this environment. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-16 — Spec 069 implemented: the disk-operations screen's playlist targets, closing P9

User direction: "continue with P9 next." With spec 068's own mechanism (`openPlaylistUrl`) real, this was the actual screen work it existed for - and P9's own last remaining piece.

Re-read `script/src/ui/diskOperations.js`'s own `"playlists"`/`"hippo"` targets and `Editor.unpackUrl` (already read once during spec 068's own research) and, critically, `script/src/provider/hippo.js`'s own `get`/`loadFromApi` in full before writing any test - confirming the real wire URL a `"playlist/<name>"` request actually hits is `apiUrl + param` (the bare filename, e.g. `.../api/hippo/chippies.prg`), *not* `apiUrl + "playlist/" + param` as a first, wrong guess at the test's own fake-fetch URL assumed. That wrong guess failed immediately and was corrected by re-reading the real source a second time rather than adjusting the test to whatever felt plausible.

The curation work anticipated by spec 068's own research turned out smaller in the two ways that research had already flagged, and one more found only by inspecting the actual content directly: of the seven curatable files (`demoscene2023`-`2026`, `DSR2024`, `chippies`, `raw_diskmag`), only three needed any filtering at all, and only ten entries total across them (out of roughly 340 real entries) - not the wholesale per-file curation `data/modules.json`'s own sixteen-entry filter (spec 036) might have suggested was typical. A one-off script (not committed) read all seven real files, dropped only entries whose own `url` was present but not absolute, and wrote `assets/data/playlists.json` plus one curated `assets/data/playlists/<name>.json` per playlist - preserving every field a source entry has (title/url/author/group/info/link), not the smaller hand-picked field set `modules.json` uses, since nothing here reads this file selectively. A handful of entries with no `url` at all turned out, on inspection, to be section headers (`{"title": "Gerp 2023", "icon": "icons/gerp.png"}`) grouping the flat list visually - not broken links needing the same filter, since `domain/playlist`'s own navigation (spec 065) already treats a no-`url` entry as a gap to skip past, exactly what a header needs. "Demo Songs" (100% `demomods/`-relative, the same folder spec 036 already declined to copy) and "My Favorites" (confirmed, by re-tracing `unpackUrl("favorites")`, to be a different, server-side, anonymous-session feature entirely unrelated to `domain/settings/favorites.js`) were both excluded outright.

`domain/diskOperations/index.js` gained two more targets in its own existing shape (one cached tree per target, root-fetch-then-flatten, a leaf click that acts on the row): `rootFor()` gained `loadPlaylistsRoot` (mirroring `loadBassoonRoot` exactly) and a direct `browseHippo("playlists")` call; `setBrowserRow()`'s leaf branch gained two more `currentTarget` checks ahead of the existing `loadModule` fallback - Playlists calls `openPlaylistUrl` (spec 068); Hippo calls its own provider's `"playlist/<name>"` verb directly and hands the result straight to `setPlaylist`, since (confirmed by reading `loadFromApi` directly) that verb already returns a ready `{title, modules}` object with no text to fetch-and-parse at all, unlike every other browse() call this file already makes. That same reading found `domain/providers/hippo.js#browse()`'s own JSDoc had never actually said its two verbs return different shapes (an array vs. an object, or `[]` on a failed fetch for the object case) - corrected without changing any behavior, since this spec is the module's first real second caller.

`build.mjs`'s dist media-copy list gained one more relative path, `"data/playlists"`, following spec 036's own precedent of generalizing that list rather than hardcoding a second copy loop. Running a real `dist` build against it immediately (not anticipated) surfaced a genuine, latent bug: the existing copy loop's own directory listing filtered out dotfiles but never checked for a directory entry, so once `assets/data/` gained its own `playlists/` subdirectory, the loop tried `copyFileSync` on that directory and crashed with `ENOTSUP`. Fixed at the point the bug actually lived (skip directory entries in the same filter), not worked around by restructuring the asset layout to avoid nesting.

6 new tests extending `tests/diskOperations.test.js`, reusing its own established fake-fetch convention (spec 036): `setDiskTarget(Playlists)` publishing rows from a faked snapshot fetch and tolerating a failed one; a Playlists leaf click fetching and setting a real playlist; `setDiskTarget(Hippo)` publishing rows from a faked API listing; a Hippo leaf click setting a playlist directly, and a failed one leaving the current playlist untouched. `domain/providers/hippo.js#reset()` (built in spec 034, unused by any test until now) had to be added to the file's own `beforeEach` once a second test's own dependence on `browse("playlists")` caught its module-level memoization returning an earlier test's own stale fake data instead of asking `fetch` again.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 958 tests, all passing, up from 952. `node compiler/generate.js` (12 warnings, unchanged) confirmed. `node build.mjs app` - 103 loose ends, up from 101 (exactly the two new `inert-control` radio items - the target radioGroup's own `change` command, `disk-target.set`, was already real since spec 036). `node build.mjs dist` succeeds, copying all seven new curated playlist files alongside `playlists.json` itself.

## 2026-09-16 — Correction: v1's real UI layout model, and a new steering doc

User direction, after reviewing the visual-parity audit's own first-pass report: the audit's central finding ("v1 uses floating overlay panels over an always-visible main screen; v2 should build a real overlay/z-order mechanism to match") was wrong. The resemblance was real - Options/File Operations/Sample Edit/Nibbles do each have a bordered look and an "X" close button - but the mechanism underneath is not an overlay at all.

The real model, recorded verbatim from the user as new steering documentation (`v2/project/specs/ui-layout-model.md`, added to `specs/index.md`'s own steering list alongside `product.md`): the application is divided into five fixed horizontal sections - menu bar, song properties, control bar, pattern (itself split vertically into a sidebar and a main area), and piano. Each section can swap in one of several "full section" panels, in place, never as a floating layer. Options/File Operations/Nibbles swap into the **song properties section**; Sample Edit swaps into the **pattern section**. The close button doesn't dismiss an overlay - it returns that one section to its own default content. The explicit goal, in the user's own words: avoid floating panels as much as possible, specifically to prevent panel overlap/clutter - "every section clicks into place" - though not claimed as a 100%-consistent invariant already, just the target to design toward.

This means v2's own real gap is narrower and different in kind than the audit's first pass concluded: `normalMainView` currently conflates several of v1's own distinct sections into one combined vertical stack (`fileOperationsPanel`/`optionsPanel`/`sampleEditPanel`, each `top-view.value`-gated, sit as plain siblings next to `editPad`/`patternViewPanel`/`songPatternList`/`songProperties`, which have no such gating and always show, all under one `main-view.value` gate) - not that it lacks an overlay mechanism it needs to build. The fix direction is giving v2 real, independent, section-scoped swap zones (at minimum a real "song properties section" and a real "pattern section"), not adding z-order/layering machinery.

`v2/project/specs/010-port-roadmap/visual-parity-plan.md` (written by this session's own audit fork just before this correction) was updated throughout to match: its own central finding, the screen-by-screen table, every per-screen write-up that had called v1's sections "floating overlays," and Task 5 (re-scoped from "design and land the overlay mechanism" to "give v2 real, independent, section-scoped swap zones matching v1's five fixed sections") were all corrected in place rather than left with a materially wrong conclusion for whoever picks this plan up next. No code/YAML changes were made as part of this correction - it is a documentation fix, restoring the plan's own accuracy before any of its tasks are executed.

`010-port-roadmap/visual-parity-plan.md`'s own "Prioritized task list" was replaced with a new "Ordered small steps" list, split into two groups: steps 1-8 are pure restructuring (give the pattern section and the song-properties section their own real, bounded top-level wrappers; move `infoBar`/`songPatternList` into the section they actually belong to per the map; scope `sampleEditPanel`/`optionsPanel`/`fileOperationsPanel`/`nibblesPanel` as each section's own local alternate instead of siblings in `normalMainView`'s shared, conflated stack; then the two already-identified reshaping tasks for Options/File Operations) - each independently testable via `computeGenericLayout` against the real compiled `app.yaml`, the same convention every prior spec in this port used, plus a real screenshot; steps 9-12 are net-new content v1 has that v2 has never built (the missing song-properties content, the missing pattern sidebar, the missing `TrackControl` S/M/FX header, the piano's responsive height shrink), each its own separate, larger initiative, explicitly not required to unblock the structural steps before them.

No code/YAML changes were made in this pass - purely research and planning, building the reference map future implementation steps will check themselves against.

## 2026-09-16 — Visual-parity plan, step 1: editPad, plus three real systemic bugs found and fixed

User direction: proceed with step 1 of the UI-alignment plan (`editPad.yaml`'s missing button size). The one-line fix the plan predicted turned out to need two real changes, and verifying it against a real screenshot (not just the compiled-outline test convention this whole test suite already relies on) surfaced three genuine, confirmed, system-wide bugs no existing test had ever caught - none of them specific to editPad.

`editPad.yaml` needed `direction: row` added (its two Track/Pattern columns were meant to sit side by side) and its bare `height: fixed` (no `size:`, defaulting to the generic 28px panel fallback) removed in favor of real, content-driven auto sizing. Verified via `computeGenericLayout` against the compiled outline first - looked correct there - but a live screenshot of the real generated app still showed the old, broken, stacked-and-clipped layout. Traced why by diffing the compiled outline against the actually-emitted `app/ui/editPad.js`: **`compiler/emit.js#layoutLiteral()` only ever wrote `width`/`height`/`resizable` into a generated node's own `layout: {...}` literal - `direction` was never emitted at all, for any node, anywhere in the whole application.** Confirmed system-wide by grepping every file under `app/ui/`: exactly one `direction` existed in any of them, and only inside a `breakpoints` override (serialized as a raw JSON blob through an entirely different code path that happens to preserve it). Every authored `direction:` in every component YAML across the whole app had been silently dropped from the real generated application - the compiled dev-time outline (`app-outline.json`) had it correctly the whole time, which is exactly why the dozens of existing `computeGenericLayout`-against-the-compiled-outline tests throughout this test suite never caught it: that check path never touches `emit.js` at all, only `compiler/convert.js`.

Fixing `emit.js` alone still wasn't enough - re-screenshotting after that fix showed no change at all. `runtime/components.js#component()` (the plain-object node constructor every generated `app/ui/*.js` file's own `component("panel", {...})` call runs through at load time) independently reconstructs its own `layout` object from `props.layout`, and its own object literal *also* only copied `width`/`height`/`resizable` - the identical one-line omission, in a second, completely separate place, affecting hand-written glue code exactly as much as generated code. Both needed the same one-line fix before `direction: "row"` actually took effect in a real browser.

With both direction fixes in place, editPad rendered correctly (Track/Pattern side by side, compact) - but running the full suite showed two more real failures: `patternViewScreen.test.js`'s own `patternGridPanel`/`multitrackViewPanel` now computed to zero height. Traced this all the way down rather than weakening the assertions to match: `normalMainView` itself (`app.yaml`) had no authored `height` at all, defaulting to auto - confirmed, not assumed, by varying the test's own viewport height from 300 to 2000px and finding `normalMainView`'s own computed height never changed at all, proving it was never actually anchored to real available space to begin with. None of its own `height: fill` descendants - `patternViewPanel`, and everything nested under it - had ever received genuine, viewport-relative space; they were only ever getting whatever a bottom-up intrinsic-measurement fallback happened to produce, which is why growing `editPad`'s own real height by 80px directly stole exactly 80px from `patternViewPanel`'s own share, in a `normalMainView` whose own total (visibly, mechanically, in the numbers) never moved regardless of the real viewport. Adding the one missing `height: fill` to `normalMainView` fixed this at the actual root - and, as a direct, confirmed side effect (not a coincidence chased after the fact), resolved both `patternViewScreen.test.js` failures on its own; neither assertion needed to be touched or weakened.

One welcome, unplanned discovery along the way, deliberately not chased further this session: v1's Options panel now renders noticeably more compactly (each settings category as one real row, not a stacked full-width section) purely as a side effect of the `direction` fix - `optionsPanel.yaml`'s own per-category rows had already authored `direction: row`, silently broken the exact same way editPad's was, this whole time. Real, visible improvement, but still not v1's actual 6-column grid - that stays the visual-parity-plan's own Step 7, unchanged.

One test needed a real, honest update, not a weakening: `tests/nibblesMainView.test.js`'s own "editPad's authored `height: fixed` is unaffected by nesting depth" assertion no longer had a valid example, since editPad is no longer `height: fixed` at all. Substituted `main.top.volumemeter` (spec 054's own genuinely fixed, real-numbered height, `16`) - proves the identical underlying property the original assertion was actually testing, just with a still-valid example.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 958/958 passing. `node compiler/generate.js` (12 warnings, unchanged) and `node build.mjs app`/`dist` (103 loose ends, unchanged) both confirmed - none of these four fixes (editPad's own two YAML properties, `emit.js`, `components.js`, `normalMainView`'s missing `height: fill`) touch any authored command or add a new one. Confirmed live against the real running app in headless Chromium at every stage, not just the compiled-outline test convention.

`010-port-roadmap/visual-parity-plan.md`'s own Step 1 entry updated in place with the full finding, marked done. Step 2 (re-verify the File Operations empty-list finding under the real dev server) is next. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-16 — Visual-parity plan, step 2: the File Operations empty-list finding was real

User direction: continue with step 2 (re-verify the File Operations empty-list finding under a real dev server, since the original audit pass suspected it might just be an artifact of its own simplified static-serving setup). It was not - it was a real, confirmed bug, only visible because this pass added console/network logging to the same live-browser check the original audit had only screenshotted.

The real error: `store: unknown field "rows.main.fileOperationsPanel.browser"`, thrown from `domain/diskOperations/index.js#publish()`'s own `app.state.set(...)` call, every time any of the five disk-op targets (Bassoon/ModArchive/Modules.pl/Playlists/Hippo) tried to publish its own row list. Two separate, real defects, found by tracing the actual thrown error rather than guessing at the cause:

First, `domain/diskOperations/index.js`'s own `BROWSER_ROWS_FIELD` constant hardcoded a stale node id - `"main.fileOperationsPanel.browser"`, one level shallower than the real, current tree shape (`"main.normalMainView.fileOperationsPanel.browser"`, confirmed directly against the compiled outline). Fixed the constant, and updated `tests/diskOperations.test.js`'s own 10 hardcoded occurrences of the identical stale string - its own fake `app.state` (a plain, non-validating Map) never checks field names, so it had never caught this drift either.

Fixing the id alone did not fix the crash - the corrected field name was *still* rejected as "unknown." Traced one level deeper: `compiler/emit.js`'s own state-field derivation switch - the one piece of code that decides which fields get seeded into every generated `state.js`, so a capability has somewhere real to write to - has a case for every other specialized list-like behavior (`list`, `disclose`, `tabs`, `collapsible`, `digitEntry`) but had **never had one for `fileBrowser`'s own `"rowList"` behavior**, confirmed by reading the whole switch rather than assumed from the symptom. Neither its `rows.<id>` field nor its `scroll.<id>` field (reused from `listController.js` unchanged, exactly as `runtime/fileBrowserController.js`'s own header comment already documents) had ever been registered, for any fileBrowser node, since the type was first built (spec 035/036) - confirmed directly in `runtime/store.js`, whose `set()`/`subscribe()` both strictly reject any field not pre-seeded this way. This meant *every* disk-op target, in the real generated application, had been throwing on its very first publish since those specs shipped - completely invisible until now because every existing test in this whole port's own test suite uses a fake `app.state` stand-in that never validates field names the way the real store does. This is a real, confirmed gap in the test convention itself, not just something specific to this one screen - the first time this session's live-browser verification (rather than the compiled-outline-only convention every prior spec relied on) caught something the whole existing suite structurally could not have.

Fixed by adding a `behavior === "rowList"` case to `emit.js`, registering both fields with the same shape `list`'s own two-field case already has (`[]` for rows, `0` for scroll).

Verified live, with real console/network logging, not just a screenshot: Bassoon's own curated module list populates correctly (Wizball, Enigma, Elek Funk!, Guitar Slinger, Haste, Scrambled Mind, Space Song, Street Jungle, each with a real file size); spec 069's own Playlists list (DemoScene 2023-2026, DSR 2024, Chippies, RAW diskmag, each with a real track count) - built two sessions ago and never visually verified in a real browser until right now - also populates correctly. No console errors either way.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 958/958 passing. `node compiler/generate.js` (12 warnings, unchanged) and `node build.mjs app`/`dist` (103 loose ends, unchanged; `state.js` now lists two more real, correctly-seeded fields) both confirmed.

`010-port-roadmap/visual-parity-plan.md`'s own Step 2 entry updated in place with the full finding, marked done. Step 3 (give the pattern section a real, bounded top-level wrapper) is next. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-16 — Visual-parity plan, step 4: sampleEditPanel scoped to the pattern section

User direction: continue with step 4 (scope `sampleEditPanel` as the pattern-section wrapper's own alternate, toggled by a field local to that wrapper rather than the shared, too-broad `top-view.value`). `patternSection`'s own content (built in step 3) now holds two mutually-exclusive children instead of `editPad`/`infoBar`/`patternViewPanel` directly: a new `patternSectionDefault` wrapper (gated on a new field, `pattern-section-view.value`, `oneOf: [""]`, holding those same three nodes in the same order) and `sampleEditPanel` itself, moved in beside it and re-gated onto the same new field (`oneOf: ["Sample Edit"]`, replacing its old `top-view.value` condition), plus a `height: fill` it never needed before (it previously shared the whole stack rather than filling one bounded section).

The control bar's "Sample Edit" button keeps dispatching the one shared `top-view.set` command every `topViewGroup` item already used (spec 025/026's own mechanism, untouched) - what changed is what that command does. A new module, `domain/appView/index.js`, holds `setTopView(app, detail)`, mapped in `capabilities.js` in place of the `GENERATED MOCK` every other pure-UI-state field still uses; it writes *both* `top-view.value` and `pattern-section-view.value` on every click, mirroring v1's own real mechanism - `appMainPanel.js` and `patternPanel.js` each independently subscribe to the same single `EVENT.showView` broadcast and react to whichever value it last carried, so selecting "Sample Edit" is *why* `appMainPanel.js`'s own switch falls through to its "restore the always-present view" branch (closing Options/File Operations as a side effect, not a separate command) - not a coincidence of two unrelated switches. Concretely: selecting "Sample Edit" sets `top-view.value` to `""` and `pattern-section-view.value` to `"Sample Edit"`; selecting anything else sets `top-view.value` to that value and `pattern-section-view.value` back to `""`.

Verified against the compiled outline directly (both states produce correct, non-zero rects for the right node, and an absent rect for the other) and live in the browser through all four combinations in sequence: Sample Edit alone; File Operations after it (Sample Edit closes, pattern section reverts to `editPad`/`infoBar`/pattern-grid, File Operations shows above in the song-properties area); Sample Edit again (File Operations closes, Sample Edit reopens); Options after that (Sample Edit closes again, Options shows) - all four matched the intended, v1-mirroring behavior exactly, with screenshots confirming each state.

Six pre-existing tests across five files had hardcoded the old shape and needed tracing and fixing individually: `compiler.test.js` (the `sampleEditPanel.visibleWhen` assertion, plus two `infoBar` id assertions needing the new `patternSectionDefault` segment inserted), `envelopeScreen.test.js` (its `stateValueOf` fixture and three id-path assertions), `nibblesMainView.test.js` (`WRAPPED_REGION_IDS`, two tests' own `stateValueOf` fixtures needing `pattern-section-view.value` explicitly seeded to `""` - the same "real seeded default, not JS's `undefined`" gotcha this whole session keeps hitting - plus one test split in two since "fileOperationsPanel/sampleEditPanel both need their own `top-view.value`" stopped being one true claim once the two got separate fields), `patternViewScreen.test.js` (its shared `layoutWith` helper needing the same seeding, plus every `editPad`/`patternViewPanel` id updated one level deeper), and `yaml.test.js` (the raw-YAML structural assertions for `normalMainView`'s and `patternSection`'s own content lists).

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 960/960 passing (959 before this step + 1 new test, from `nibblesMainView.test.js`'s split). `node compiler/generate.js` (12 warnings, unchanged) and `node build.mjs app`/`dist` both confirmed - `top-view.set` no longer appears as a `mocked-command` loose end, the one intended, real change to that report.

Verified against the compiled outline directly (`songProperties` computes to a real, bounded 160px-tall rect; `songPatternList` fills essentially all of it) and live in the browser: the song/pattern list at the bottom of the screen renders identically to before this step, pixel-for-pixel - a pure internal restructuring, not a visible change, exactly as intended. v1's own real position for this section (directly under the menu bar, above the control bar) is deliberately *not* addressed here - this step only scopes/bounds it where it already sits; reordering is out of this step's scope per the plan's own wording.

Four pre-existing tests across four files had hardcoded `songPatternList`'s old flat-sibling id or the old empty-`songProperties` layout shape and needed tracing and fixing individually: `emit.test.js` (the `songProperties` node's own `layout` assertion), `nibblesMainView.test.js` (`WRAPPED_REGION_IDS` plus one inline id list), `patternViewScreen.test.js` (the "patternViewPanel sits between editPad and songPatternList" test's own two id references), and `yaml.test.js` (`normalMainView`'s own content-list assertions, one fewer top-level item now that `songPatternList` moved into `songProperties.yaml`'s own content).

`010-port-roadmap/visual-parity-plan.md`'s own Step 5 entry updated in place with the full finding, marked done. Step 6 (scope `optionsPanel`/`fileOperationsPanel`/`nibblesPanel` as this new song-properties wrapper's own alternates) is next. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-16 — Visual-parity plan, step 6: song-properties alternates, and a real step-4 bug found and fixed

User direction: continue with step 6 (scope `optionsPanel`/`fileOperationsPanel`/`nibblesPanel` as the song-properties wrapper's own alternates). Understanding this properly required reading `script/src/ui/app/controlPanel.js` in full for the first time - which surfaced a real bug in step 4's own, already-"done" work, confirmed live before touching anything: v1's three control-bar buttons (File Operations/Options/Sample Edit) are `Checkboxbutton`s - three genuinely **independent** toggles, not one mutually-exclusive radioGroup. Live proof: opening Sample Edit, then clicking File Operations, leaves both open at once (screenshot: the waveform editor fully visible in the pattern area, and the LOAD MODULE file browser visible in the song-properties strip above it, at the same time) - something step 4's own shared `setTopView` command, writing two fields in lockstep, could never produce. I stopped and asked the user how to proceed (fix now as part of step 6 / fix as its own separate step first / defer it) - the user chose to fix it now, as part of step 6, since step 6 touches the exact same routing code anyway.

The real rule, confirmed by reading `controlPanel.js`'s own `EVENT.showView` listener directly: File Operations and Options share one slot (`appMainPanel.js`'s own `_currentView`), and Nibbles shares that *same* slot (`appMainPanel.js`'s `pluginRenderHook`, target `"main"`, explicitly hides file-ops/options first) - but Sample Edit lives in a second, completely independent slot (`patternPanel.js`'s own `currentView`), never touched by the other three. `"topmain"`/`"bottommain"`/`"main"` are three reset granularities matching this exactly (top section only / pattern section only / both).

Fixed by replacing `controlBar.yaml`'s `topViewGroup` radioGroup with `topViewButtons`, a `buttonGroup` of three independent buttons, each with its own rule and its own click action - matching v1's real shape, including "click again to close" (a `radioGroup` cannot express this at all - confirmed by reading `mountGeneric.js`'s own radio-selection code, which never dispatches `change` for re-clicking an already-selected item). `domain/appView/index.js#setTopView` was replaced with three independent `toggle*` exports, no cross-writes between fields any more. Three new `compiler/recipes.js` `CONDITION_FIELD_ALIASES` entries let each button's rule compare a string field to a specific value.

A new field, `song-properties-view.value`, is now the real local switch for `songProperties.yaml`'s three alternates (File Operations/Options/Nibbles - all three genuinely share v1's one slot). `pattern-section-view.value` keeps Sample Edit's job, now genuinely independent. `nibblesPanel` - previously a large inline block and a full top-level sibling of `normalMainView`, gated on `main-view.value` - moved into a new file, `components/nibblesPanel.yaml`, as `songProperties`'s third alternate.

This surfaced an even larger corrected assumption, from spec 048 originally: **Nibbles does not replace the main content area at all.** Confirmed three ways: reading `appMainPanel.js`'s own `pluginRenderHook` handler (`_customPanel = new Panel(0, 0, this.width, this.height)` - `this` is the fixed-160px song-properties panel itself); the real, live console log while opening Nibbles in v1: `"Set Nibbles render target to {width: 1200, height: 160}"`; and a screenshot of the actual, playable game genuinely cramped into that one strip, with the control bar/edit pad/scopes/pattern grid all still visible underneath. `main-view.value` is now retired entirely - `normalMainView` lost its own `visibleWhen` and is simply always present. `domain/nibbles/index.js#openNibblesView`/`showMain` were rewritten to write the two real, section-scoped fields instead, matching v1's own single "main" broadcast exactly (opening Nibbles clears Sample Edit too; "Main"/Exit reset both).

Verified against the compiled outline (all four combinations, including File Operations *together with* Sample Edit) and live in the browser through the full real-v1 sequence: Sample Edit alone; File Operations added on top (both visible - matching the real v1 screenshot); File Operations toggled back off (Sample Edit still showing); Nibbles opened while Sample Edit was open (Sample Edit closes, Nibbles shows, pattern section untouched); Options clicked while Nibbles was open (Nibbles closes, Options shows, pattern section still untouched). One visual difference from v1 remains, deliberately out of scope: the song-properties section still renders at the bottom of the screen, not under the menu bar - this step fixed scoping/independence only, not position.

Fourteen pre-existing tests across nine files needed tracing and fixing individually, including a full rewrite of `nibblesMainView.test.js` (its entire premise was the now-corrected wrong assumption).

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 960/960 passing. `node compiler/generate.js` (12 warnings, unchanged) and `node build.mjs app`/`dist` (99 loose ends, down from 102) both confirmed.

`010-port-roadmap/visual-parity-plan.md`'s own Step 6 entry updated in place with the full finding, marked done. Step 7 (restructure `optionsPanel.yaml`'s content into v1's real 6-column grid) is next. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-16 — Visual-parity plan, step 7: optionsPanel's real column grid, plus a v1 quirk and a real height overflow found along the way

User direction: continue with step 7 (restructure `optionsPanel.yaml`'s content into v1's real column grid). Read `script/src/ui/optionsPanel.js` in full and screenshotted the real, live v1 panel at two widths rather than trusting the plan's own paraphrase: v1's real layout is N columns in one row (not a fixed 6, and not "1-2 rows" except below a 600px breakpoint), each column a label with its own values stacked *vertically* underneath (not a horizontal row of buttons, which is what v2's old shape had); any checkbox is pinned to one fixed band at the bottom of the whole panel, independent of that column's own stack height.

A real discrepancy: v1's own `optionDefs` array authors seven option columns, but confirmed at two widths (1280px and 1920px) that v1 only ever renders six - "Play from" (Pattern start/Current pos) never appears, even with plenty of empty space to its right. A real, pre-existing quirk in v1 itself. Since v2's `optionsPanel.yaml` already had a working `playFromRow` from an earlier spec, it was kept as a genuine seventh column rather than deleted to match v1's own gap - documented as a deliberate, called-out deviation.

Rebuilt the content as a `direction: row` grid of seven `width: fill` columns (each `[label, radioGroup(direction: column) items]`), plus a separate checkbox row below it with `width: fill` spacers standing in for the three columns that author no checkbox - keeping every real checkbox aligned under its own column.

Two real, confirmed things found while verifying:
- `compiler/convert.js#convertItems()` hardcodes every radioGroup/buttonGroup item's own `layout` to a fixed default, silently discarding any `layout:` an author puts on an individual `items:` entry (confirmed by tracing why `layout: { height: 18 }` per item had zero effect on the compiled outline). Real, pre-existing, but fixing it is a systemic compiler change affecting every radioGroup/buttonGroup in the app - left as a known, recorded gap, not fixed here.
- With this engine's real ~20px-tall auto-sized items (not v1's own hand-tuned pixel-perfect widgets), the tallest column (Screen Refresh: label + 4 values + a checkbox) needed close to 150px - `songProperties.yaml`'s existing fixed 160px (step 5's own number, matching v1's default) left only ~112px after the row's own padding, causing real, confirmed clipping (the checkbox row rendered fully off the bottom edge). Fixed by raising `songProperties.yaml`'s own height from 160 to 220 - a deliberate deviation, needed by this step's own two additions (the extra column, and taller real controls), not a mistake. Confirmed live that File Operations/Nibbles/songPatternList (this section's other three alternates) all just gained a little extra headroom as a harmless side effect.

Verified live in the browser side by side with a fresh v1 screenshot: all seven columns render with values correctly stacked, checkboxes correctly aligned under their own columns, and clicking a value button still dispatches its own real command and highlights correctly. Two tests needed updating for the new 220px figure (`emit.test.js`'s own `songProperties` layout assertion, `nibblesMainView.test.js`'s own "bounded, not full-viewport" height check).

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 960/960 passing. `node compiler/generate.js` (12 warnings, unchanged) and `node build.mjs app`/`dist` (99 loose ends, unchanged) both confirmed.

`010-port-roadmap/visual-parity-plan.md`'s own Step 7 entry updated in place with the full finding, marked done. Step 8 (restructure `fileOperationsPanel.yaml`'s Load side into columns, and re-check `nibblesGrid`'s own sizing) is next. No Git commit was created.

## 2026-09-16 — Visual-parity plan, step 8: fileOperationsPanel's real column grid, and a recurring stale-id bug found again

User direction: continue with step 8 (restructure `fileOperationsPanel.yaml`'s Load side into columns; re-check `nibblesGrid`'s own sizing). Read `script/src/ui/diskOperations.js` and its four sub-panels in full: v1's real layout is four columns - Action, Type (Module/Sample/Playlist, shared by load and save), From/To (a target list that changes both its own label and its own items depending on Action and Type), and a wider fourth column (Load label+listbox, or Save type/filename/Export). v2 has never built a "Type" selector at all - adding one now would be new functionality, not restructuring, so per this whole step range's own stated scope it was deliberately left out; the real result is three columns (Action/target-or-save-type/content), not v1's four - documented as a known, deliberate gap.

Rebuilt the content as `diskOperationsGrid`: `actionColumn`, `targetColumn` (holding the existing "From" target list and the existing "Save" type list as two `disk-action.value`-gated alternates in the same column slot, matching how v1's own single target panel swaps its own label in place), and `contentColumn` (`width: fill`, holding the load label+browser and the save sub-panels as two more alternates). Every field/command reused exactly as already built - no new capability needed.

A real, confirmed bug found live, the same recurring class this whole initiative keeps hitting: clicking a "From" target threw `store: unknown field "rows.main.normalMainView.fileOperationsPanel.browser"` and the module list never populated. `domain/diskOperations/index.js`'s own `BROWSER_ROWS_FIELD` constant still hardcoded the id from before *both* step 6 (which moved this panel into `songProperties`) and this step (which nested `browser` three levels deeper) - the identical "hardcoded full id, never updated when the node moves" defect step 2 already found and fixed once. Fixed the constant; `tests/diskOperations.test.js`'s own 10 hardcoded occurrences of the same stale string needed the identical fix, for the identical reason (its own fake store never validates field names).

`nibblesGrid`'s own sizing, re-checked as asked: no change needed. `nibbles.js`'s own real render-target sizing derives the grid's size dynamically from whatever height it's given, never a hardcoded pixel value - v2's existing `width: fill, height: fill` already matches that exactly, now that it's properly bounded (step 6) with a bit more room (step 7).

Verified live in the browser: Load (Bassoon's module list populating correctly, no console errors), Save→Module, and Save→Sample all render in the new three-column shape and work correctly; Nibbles re-checked, unaffected. Four tests in `saveExportScreen.test.js` needed their own id-path constants updated for the new nesting.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 960/960 passing. `node compiler/generate.js` (12 warnings, unchanged) and `node build.mjs app`/`dist` (99 loose ends, unchanged) both confirmed.

`010-port-roadmap/visual-parity-plan.md`'s own Step 8 entry updated in place with the full finding, marked done. Steps 1-8 (all pure restructuring) are now complete. ## 2026-09-16/17 — Spec mislabeling fixed (069 -> 070, retroactively registered), then spec 071: song-properties content

Housekeeping first: steps 1-8 of the visual-parity plan had been informally called "spec 069" throughout (in code comments and tests), colliding with the real, already-registered spec 069 (disk-operations-playlists). User direction: register steps 1-8 retroactively as spec 070, freeing 069 for its real feature. Ten files' own "069" references were fixed via a blanket substitution, with `domain/diskOperations/index.js`/`tests/diskOperations.test.js` deliberately excluded (their own spec-069 references are legitimate). `project/specs/070-ui-section-alignment/{requirements,design,tasks}.md` written retroactively, summarizing all eight steps; `project/specs/index.md` gained its own row and its "next available number" footer moved to 071.

Then spec 071 itself - step 9 of the same plan: the song-properties section's own remaining real content (the logo/tracker/corner banners, the mod-name inputbox, the instrument listbox, the six spinboxes), closing the song/pattern list's own four buttons as a documented side effect. Two scoping decisions made by the user via explicit choice: build everything in one spec except drag-to-reorder (deferred); and accept a second, narrow, documented exception letting `domain/playback`'s own `loadModule`/`openFiles` call `domain/song#publishSongProperties` directly, rather than routing song-properties' own initial data through some new indirection.

Implementation: `domain/song/format.js` (new, `formatInstrumentIndex`, ported verbatim including its real double-space rendering quirk); new pattern-table primitives and row publishers in `domain/song/index.js`; `domain/playback/index.js` gained `setBPM`/`setSongTitle` and the new `publishSongProperties(app)` call at load time; `components/instrumentList.yaml` (new, `fileBrowser` reused as the only mechanism here supporting a variable-length, data-bound row list - confirmed the real row shape, `{title, ...}`, directly off the draw kind rather than trusting a paraphrase); `components/songPatternList.yaml`'s own listbox replaced with a real `fileBrowser` backed by the same new domain data.

Three real, previously-latent bugs found and fixed while verifying live in a real browser (Playwright), none caught by any of the 985 pre-existing tests:
- The asset pipeline (`compiler/skin.js#compileSkin()`) only ever scanned skin.yaml's own styles for asset references, never the compiled app tree - so `togglecorner`/`tracker` (referenced only through plain `image` nodes, not a skin style) were silently missing from `node build.mjs dist`'s release atlas, though they rendered fine in dev mode. Fixed with a new, generic `registerAssetUse`/`assetPropertyOf` mechanism (a declarative `assetProperty` on `icon`/`image` in the type registry, read with no type-name branch, keeping `tests/componentTypes.test.js`'s architecture-enforcement test green).
- A three-layer label-fallback chain (`compiler/convert.js#applyLabel` at compile time, `renderers/canvas/mountGeneric.js#labelText()` at draw time, and `runtime/components.js#component()`'s own `labelVariants(props.label, props.name)` plus `compiler/emit.js`'s decision of whether to even emit a `label:` prop) silently overrode the logo button's own intentionally empty `label: ""`, rendering its own authored name on top of the artwork. All three needed fixing/coordinating.
- `compiler/emit.js`'s `rowList` case (fileBrowser) never declared the field a `change` action's own recipe-derived name resolves to at compile time - `runtime/valueController.js#valueFieldOf(node)` finds that name via `node.bindings`, which exists on the compiler's own AST node but is never carried onto the runtime tree `runtime/components.js#component()` builds. So the real renderer wrote to a different, undeclared field (`"value.<id>"`) than the one compile time declared, and every real pointerup on a fileBrowser row with a bound `change` action (the new `instrumentList`, the new `songPatternList`, and the pre-existing disk-operations `browser`) silently dropped its own write - caught live double-clicking the instrument list. Fixed by emitting the compile-time-resolved field name explicitly, the same way every other authored value control already gets one.

A further finding, live: the fixed 220px `songPropertiesDefault` panel could not fit banner row + mod-name box + instrument list + six spinboxes + the song/pattern list's own four buttons stacked in one column - two of those buttons rendered below the panel's own clipped edge. Checked against v1's real source: v1 never stacks this content in one column at all, spreading four groups across four side-by-side columns above its own real 620px width threshold and tab-switching between them below it. Asked the user how to proceed; chosen direction went beyond simply rebuilding v1's own tab-switch - a genuinely responsive layout instead ("columns when there's room, stacking when there isn't, scrollable as a last resort"). Built entirely from existing, already-tested mechanisms: a `songPropertiesLayout` wrapper holding the four groups in v1's own left-to-right order, a `breakpoints` override (spec 025's mechanism, already used by `controlBar.yaml`) collapsing it to one stacked column below 620px, and `songPropertiesDefault` changed from a plain `panel` to a `viewport` (spec 013's scrolling clip box - its first real production use anywhere in this port) so a stacked column that overflows scrolls instead of clipping. Verified live at both 1280px (four columns, all four song/pattern-list buttons visible, nothing scrolls) and 500px (single stacked column, scrollbar appears, wheel-scrolling reveals every group down to the instrument list).

Six pre-existing tests needed their own hardcoded node-id paths updated for the new nesting (`instrumentList`/`songPatternList` moved under the new `songPropertiesLayout` wrapper); `tests/compiler.test.js`'s own asset `usedBy` assertion was relaxed to allow any `.yaml` source, not just `skin/default.yaml`, now that image/icon nodes legitimately register their own uses too.

Verification: `node --test tests/*.test.js` - 985/985 passing. `node compiler/generate.js` (12 warnings, unchanged) and `node build.mjs app`/`dist` (96 loose ends, unchanged; atlas correctly repacked with all four new assets) both confirmed. Full live-browser pass (Playwright): logo toggle, mod-name live edit, bpm spinbox stepper, song/pattern-list Ins button, instrument select + double-click opening the sample editor, and the wide/narrow/scroll layout behavior above - zero console errors throughout.

`project/specs/071-song-properties-content/tasks.md` updated to reflect completion; `project/specs/index.md` gained its own row and its "next available number" footer moved to 072. No Git commit was created.
